9//! The `/proc` directories Detcore serves itself, each on a placeholder
10//! descriptor: the caller's own descriptor directory, `/proc/<pid>/fd`, from
11//! Detcore's own descriptor table (package K slice F1, toward
12//! <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4604>), and `/proc` itself, from
13//! Detcore's procfs model (`procfs::listing`, package K slice P1), and, where
14//! the guest's procfs is not the kernel's (KVM), the system-wide directories
15//! of the `--proc` level table (`/proc/sys`, `/proc/tty`, ...:
16//! `PlaceholderDirectory::System`), from the same model, decided by the
17//! object a lookup reaches ([`Detcore::system_directory_reached`]). The
18//! models, and the parts of Linux they follow, are in
19//! [`crate::procfs::fd_directory`].
20//!
21//! An open of such a directory injects an open of the descriptor's object,
22//! with the caller's `O_CLOEXEC`, and records which directory the descriptor
23//! stands for (`DetFd::placeholder`). The object depends on one capability,
24//! whether the guest's procfs is the kernel's
25//! (`procfs_inode::tool_can_name_guest_descriptors`), and on nothing else:
26//! - where it is (ptrace), the kernel's procfs root, `openat(AT_FDCWD,
27//!   "/proc", O_RDONLY | O_DIRECTORY)`, as slice F1 opened it. The kernel
28//!   resolves every path through the descriptor: below `/proc` itself, and
29//!   through its descriptor link, exactly as Linux does. A name looked up
30//!   below a descriptor directory is looked up below that directory's own
31//!   path within procfs, from the descriptor itself (`<pid>/fd/<name>`
32//!   relative to it), and a path through a descriptor link to one goes on
33//!   below that path after the link (`/proc/self/fd/D/<pid>/fd/<name>`), so
34//!   that every lookup starts where the guest's does, whatever the caller's
35//!   root or PID namespace ([`Detcore::respelled_below_descriptor_directory`]);
36//! - where it is not (KVM, whose executor serves no procfs directory), the
37//!   placeholder directory, the empty, read-only
38//!   tmpfs hermit-cli mounts at the container's `/proc/fs/nfsd`
39//!   (`DetConfig::directory_placeholder`), procfs's own permanently empty
40//!   mount point. A name looked up below the descriptor, or through its
41//!   link, is looked up at the directory's absolute spelling (`/proc/x`,
42//!   `/proc/self/fd/N`) instead, for each injection that carries it.
43//!
44//! Either way Detcore answers every call through the descriptor from its
45//! model, with one implementation ([`Detcore::answer_placeholder_call`]):
46//! `getdents` and `getdents64`, `lseek`, `fstat` and `statx`, `fstatfs`, the
47//! fdinfo, `fcntl`'s status flags and write hints, the `O_PATH` refusals,
48//! `fchdir`, `flock` (each directory its own lock, as each has an inode of its
49//! own on Linux), and every call procfs refuses, with procfs's errno, in
50//! Linux's order of checks. Only the object differs, and the guest never sees
51//! it: the placeholder directory is hidden. Detcore's `--proc` level table
52//! hides `/proc/fs` at every level, and KVM's executor hides every other
53//! spelling of the placeholder too, for hermit-cli reserves it there
54//! (`reverie_kvm::KvmBackend::reserve_tool_directory`, which requires a
55//! read-only file system, as the fresh read-only tmpfs is): every lookup by
56//! such a spelling, and every call that changes a name whose parent the
57//! executor does not find on procfs, fails with `ENOENT`, as for the absent
58//! `/proc/fs`. Where Detcore leaves such a spelling to the executor and its
59//! parent is procfs's own `/proc/fs` (a path relative to the working
60//! directory, <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4541>, or a lookup
61//! from an inherited procfs descriptor,
62//! <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/4816>), the calls that check
63//! that parent first answer procfs's `EACCES` instead: `mkdir`, `mknod`,
64//! `unlink`, `rmdir`, `rename` (from, over and exchange), `link`,
65//! `symlink`'s new path, `fchmodat` (`chmod`) and `utimensat` (`utimes`). A
66//! creation through a final symbolic link into the placeholder, and the
67//! other cases Reverie's contract names (a race between a check and its
68//! call; a creation below another user's link under
69//! `fs.protected_symlinks`), get the read-only file system's `EROFS` (or
70//! `EXDEV` for a racing `rename`) and create nothing. An open or stat that
71//! reaches it by a spelling Detcore made itself is hidden after the call
72//! ([`Detcore::hidden_placeholder_reached`]). hermit-cli mounts the
73//! placeholder only where the guest's procfs is not the kernel's
74//! ([`crate::needs_directory_placeholder`]): on ptrace there is none.
75//!
76//! The table a descriptor directory lists is Detcore's own, which the tool
77//! keeps for the guest (ptrace, KVM) and F0a made complete: every descriptor
78//! it models, and every one it knows is open but does not model
79//! (`FileMetadata::present_unmodelled`). Where a receive may have installed
80//! descriptors Detcore cannot name (`FileMetadata::unaccounted_receipts`),
81//! and for another process's or thread's table (a descriptor a fork's child
82//! inherited), the kernel beneath the backend lists `/proc/<pid>/fd`, as
83//! before.
84//!
85//! Where Detcore runs in the guest process and discovers descriptors only as
86//! they are used (`BackendCapabilities::tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table`:
87//! DBT, SaBRe, LiteInst, in-guest-trap), the guest's table also holds the
88//! backend runtime's own descriptors, which no fact names but the one
89//! `Guest::is_runtime_owned_fd` reports (DBT's RPC socket): measured,
90//! DynamoRIO's 197 to 201 under DBT, and SaBRe's 3, 101 and 1025, in a
91//! listing of the guest's table. A listing of that table could not be
92//! Linux's, so there both directories stay the kernel's, as before (a
93//! capability-keyed residual, until a provider fact names the runtime's
94//! descriptors). Without a placeholder (`--no-namespace`, library callers,
95//! record and replay, a user mount that covers it) Detcore serves neither
96//! directory: on ptrace each is the kernel's, and where the guest's procfs is
97//! not the kernel's an open of either is refused with `EACCES` before it
98//! reaches the backend ([`Detcore::refuses_backend_procfs_directory`]), whose
99//! executor would refuse it too.
100

102// new determinization strategy: on ptrace its listing replaces the kernel's.

156/// The identities `(dev, ino)` of the objects placeholder descriptors are
157/// open on, as the guest's backend reports them, learnt at each placeholder
158/// open in this tool process: procfs's root where the guest's procfs is the
159/// kernel's, else the placeholder directory. A received directory descriptor
160/// of such an identity whose kernel position is a placeholder's tag is that
161/// placeholder ([`Detcore::recognize_received_placeholder`]); no guest
162/// descriptor on it is ever seeked into the tags' range
163/// ([`Detcore::seek_would_place_a_tag`]), so a tag proves a placeholder.
164static PLACEHOLDER_IDENTITIES: Mutex<BTreeSet<(u64, u64)>> = Mutex::new(BTreeSet::new());
165
166/// The identity `(dev, ino)` of the placeholder directory hermit-cli mounted
167/// (`DetConfig::directory_placeholder`), as the guest's backend reports it,
168/// once learnt in this tool process ([`Detcore::hidden_placeholder_identity`]):
169/// what no open or stat of the guest's may reach unless Detcore made the
170/// descriptor ([`Detcore::hidden_placeholder_reached`]).
171static HIDDEN_IDENTITY: Mutex<Option<(u64, u64)>> = Mutex::new(None);

173/// Whether this tool process has opened a placeholder: until it has, no path
174/// can lead below one and no received descriptor can be one, and nothing is
175/// read or injected to look ([`Detcore::may_respell_below_descriptor_directory`],
176/// [`Detcore::recognize_received_placeholder`]).
177static OPENED_ANY: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);
178
179/// What a placeholder descriptor is open on: the kernel's procfs root, where
180/// the guest's procfs is the kernel's (`procfs_inode::
181/// tool_can_name_guest_descriptors`: ptrace), so the kernel resolves every path
182/// through the descriptor below procfs; else the placeholder directory, an
183/// empty directory a path below which reaches nothing (KVM).
184#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
185enum PlaceholderObject {
186    /// The kernel's procfs root.
187    ProcfsRoot,
188    /// The placeholder directory hermit-cli mounted.
189    Placeholder,
190}
191
192/// The directory an open names.
193#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
194pub(super) enum OpenedDirectory {
195    /// One of the caller's own descriptor directories, by its spelling.
196    Own(DescriptorDirectoryTable),
197    /// `/proc` itself, by any spelling that reaches it ([`names_procfs_root`]).
198    Root,
199    /// The directory a descriptor link of the caller's (`/proc/self/fd/N`)
200    /// reaches: the placeholder directory `N` is.
201    Reopened(PlaceholderDirectory),
202    /// A task directory of the caller's PID namespace (package K slices K10
203    /// and 2b-1): a process's, one of its threads' (by its number) or its
204    /// `task`, with the process and the thread
205    /// ([`fd_directory::classify_task_directory`]), by any spelling that
206    /// reaches it ([`Detcore::own_task_directory`]).
207    Task(TaskDirectoryKind, i32, i32),
208    /// `/proc/self`, or `/proc/thread-self` (`thread`), itself, opened
209    /// `O_PATH` without following it: Linux opens the symbolic link, which no
210    /// backend Detcore serves the task directories on has, so the open is
211    /// refused by name ([`Detcore::open_placeholder`]).
212    SelectorLink { thread: bool },
213    /// One of the system-wide directories of the `--proc` level
214    /// (`/proc/sys`, ...), by the object a lookup reaches
215    /// ([`Detcore::system_directory_reached`]), with that object's facts.
216    System(SystemPlaceholder),
217}

219/// The calls whose path operands are each looked up relative to a directory
220/// descriptor operand, as (descriptor, path) argument indices. Not `execve`
221/// or `execveat`, whose path Detcore refuses by name where it leads below a
222/// placeholder ([`Detcore::refuse_lookup_below_placeholder`]): an exec reads
223/// its image before Detcore could stage a respelled path for it.
224fn relative_operands(sysno: Sysno) -> &'static [(usize, usize)] {
225    match sysno {
226        Sysno::openat
227        | Sysno::openat2
228        | crate::arch::FSTATAT
229        | Sysno::statx
230        | Sysno::faccessat
231        | Sysno::faccessat2
232        | Sysno::readlinkat
233        | Sysno::mkdirat
234        | Sysno::mknodat
235        | Sysno::unlinkat
236        | Sysno::fchmodat
237        | Sysno::fchmodat2
238        | Sysno::fchownat
239        | Sysno::utimensat
240        | Sysno::name_to_handle_at => &[(0, 1)],
241        // x86_64 only: aarch64 has no futimesat (utimensat above).
242        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
243        Sysno::futimesat => &[(0, 1)],
244        Sysno::symlinkat => &[(1, 2)],
245        Sysno::renameat | Sysno::renameat2 | Sysno::linkat => &[(0, 1), (2, 3)],
246        _ => &[],
247    }
248}
249
250/// The path operands of the calls that look a path up from the working
251/// directory or the root, by argument index. With [`relative_operands`]'s
252/// paths, an absolute one may lead through a descriptor link to a
253/// placeholder (`/proc/self/fd/D/N`), which
254/// [`Detcore::respelled_below_descriptor_directory`] spells as Linux resolves
255/// it.
256fn absolute_operands(sysno: Sysno) -> &'static [usize] {
257    match sysno {
258        Sysno::chdir
259        | Sysno::chroot
260        | Sysno::truncate
261        | Sysno::statfs
262        | Sysno::getxattr
263        | Sysno::lgetxattr
264        | Sysno::setxattr
265        | Sysno::lsetxattr
266        | Sysno::listxattr
267        | Sysno::llistxattr
268        | Sysno::removexattr
269        | Sysno::lremovexattr => &[0],
270        // x86_64 only: aarch64 has no non-*at forms of these calls;
271        // its libc uses the *at forms the relative table lists.
272        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
273        Sysno::open
274        | Sysno::creat
275        | Sysno::stat
276        | Sysno::lstat
277        | Sysno::access
278        | Sysno::readlink
279        | Sysno::mkdir
280        | Sysno::rmdir
281        | Sysno::unlink
282        | Sysno::chmod
283        | Sysno::chown
284        | Sysno::lchown
285        | Sysno::utime
286        | Sysno::utimes
287        | Sysno::mknod => &[0],
288        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
289        Sysno::link | Sysno::rename => &[0, 1],
290        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
291        Sysno::symlink => &[1],
292        Sysno::inotify_add_watch => &[1],
293        _ => &[],
294    }
295}
296
297/// Whether `sysno`'s lookup of its path argument `path` (by index) follows
298/// a final symbolic link, so that a path that is a descriptor link to a
299/// placeholder, and nothing more (`/proc/self/fd/D`), names the directory
300/// the link reaches rather than the link: as each call's `LOOKUP_FOLLOW`
301/// says (v6.17). The calls that operate on a final link itself (`lstat`,
302/// `readlink`, the `l*xattr` calls, `lchown`, the calls that make, remove or
303/// rename a name, `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW`, `inotify_add_watch`'s
304/// `IN_DONT_FOLLOW`) do not; nor `open` and `openat`, whose open of such a
305/// link Detcore makes itself (`OpenedDirectory::Reopened`), or which opens
306/// the link itself with `O_NOFOLLOW`.
307fn follows_final(sysno: Sysno, args: &syscalls::SyscallArgs, path: usize) -> bool {
308    const IN_DONT_FOLLOW: i32 = 0x0200_0000;
309    let flags = |index: usize| args_flag(args, index);
310    match sysno {
311        Sysno::faccessat
312        | Sysno::chdir
313        | Sysno::chroot
314        | Sysno::fchmodat
315        | Sysno::truncate
316        | Sysno::statfs
317        | Sysno::getxattr
318        | Sysno::setxattr
319        | Sysno::listxattr
320        | Sysno::removexattr => true,
321        // x86_64 only: aarch64 has no non-*at forms (nor futimesat).
322        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
323        Sysno::stat
324        | Sysno::access
325        | Sysno::chmod
326        | Sysno::chown
327        | Sysno::utime
328        | Sysno::utimes
329        | Sysno::futimesat => true,
330        crate::arch::FSTATAT | Sysno::faccessat2 | Sysno::fchmodat2 | Sysno::utimensat => {
331            flags(3) & libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW == 0
332        }
333        Sysno::statx => flags(2) & libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW == 0,
334        Sysno::fchownat => flags(4) & libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW == 0,
335        Sysno::name_to_handle_at => flags(4) & libc::AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW != 0,
336        Sysno::linkat => path == 1 && flags(4) & libc::AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW != 0,
337        Sysno::inotify_add_watch => flags(2) & IN_DONT_FOLLOW == 0,
338        _ => false,
339    }
340}
341
342/// `openat2`'s `RESOLVE_*` flags that scope a lookup to the directory it
343/// starts at, or to that directory's mount: `RESOLVE_BENEATH`,
344/// `RESOLVE_IN_ROOT` and `RESOLVE_NO_XDEV`. A respelled lookup starts at the
345/// root instead, from `AT_FDCWD`, where none of them would mean what the
346/// caller asked, so an `openat2` with one of them below a placeholder is
347/// refused (`EXDEV`, the answer each gives a lookup that leaves its scope:
348/// named residual). Detcore answers every guest `openat2` with `ENOSYS`
349/// today (`lib.rs`), so this guards the respelling for any later caller.
350const SCOPED_RESOLVE: u64 = libc::RESOLVE_BENEATH | libc::RESOLVE_IN_ROOT | libc::RESOLVE_NO_XDEV;
351
352/// The argument `index` of a call's raw arguments.
353fn argument(args: &syscalls::SyscallArgs, index: usize) -> usize {
354    match index {
355        0 => args.arg0,
356        1 => args.arg1,
357        2 => args.arg2,
358        3 => args.arg3,
359        4 => args.arg4,
360        _ => args.arg5,
361    }
362}
363
364/// `relative`, a path looked up below a placeholder directory, below `base`,
365/// the directory's absolute spelling: with its `.` components and repeated
366/// slashes left out, which name nothing (so that a valid path of `./`
367/// repeats, up to Linux's limit, stays one), keeping a final `/` where the
368/// path ends in one or in `.` (Linux then requires a directory and follows
369/// a final link; a final `..` is a directory already). A path of only `.`
370/// components, or none, is the directory itself, `base/.`. The bytes of each
371/// component are kept as they are, whatever they hold.
372fn below(base: &Path, relative: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {
373    let components = relative
374        .split(|byte| *byte == b'/')
375        .filter(|component| !component.is_empty() && *component != b".")
376        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
377    let mut out = base.as_os_str().as_bytes().to_vec();
378    if components.is_empty() {
379        out.extend_from_slice(b"/.");
380        return out;
381    }
382    for component in &components {
383        out.push(b'/');
384        out.extend_from_slice(component);
385    }
386    let last = relative
387        .rsplit(|byte| *byte == b'/')
388        .next()
389        .unwrap_or_default();
390    if relative.ends_with(b"/") || last == b"." {
391        out.push(b'/');
392    }
393    out
394}
395
396/// `relative`, the rest of a walk past a descriptor link to a placeholder
397/// directory, below `base`, the directory's canonical spelling
398/// (`/proc/<pid>/fd`, `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/fd`, `/proc`), as [`below`]
399/// gives it, after each `..` before its first name climbs out of `base`, as
400/// Linux's walk climbs out of the directory the link led to
401/// (`/proc/self/fd/D/../fd/N` is `/proc/<pid>/fd/N`,
402/// `/proc/self/fd/D/../../self/fd/N` is `/proc/self/fd/N`; the root's `..`
403/// is the root). Each directory such a `..` climbs to is an ancestor of a
404/// canonical spelling, a real directory, never a link, so resolving it here
405/// is exact, and it adds no link to the lookup's count. A `..` after a name
406/// is left to the walk past that name, and a `relative` of nothing but `.`
407/// and `..` components stays as [`below`] gives it.
408fn below_climbed(base: &Path, relative: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {
409    let names = relative
410        .split(|byte| *byte == b'/')
411        .filter(|component| !component.is_empty() && *component != b".")
412        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
413    let climbs = names.iter().take_while(|name| **name == b"..").count();
414    if climbs == 0 || climbs == names.len() {
415        return below(base, relative);
416    }
417    let mut base = base.to_path_buf();
418    let mut at = 0;
419    let mut climbed = 0;
420    while climbed < climbs {
421        let start = at;
422        while at < relative.len() && relative[at] != b'/' {
423            at += 1;
424        }
425        if &relative[start..at] == b".." {
426            base.pop();
427            climbed += 1;
428        }
429        at += 1;
430    }
431    let out = below(&base, &relative[at.min(relative.len())..]);
432    // Below the root, whose spelling ends in its `/` already.
433    match out.strip_prefix(b"/") {
434        Some(rest) if rest.starts_with(b"/") => rest.to_vec(),
435        _ => out,
436    }
437}
438
439/// What one component of a path below `/proc` is, for
440/// [`resolved_procfs_dotdot`]: a directory every procfs has, which a `..`
441/// can leave as Linux's walk does, by kind; or anything else, which it
442/// cannot (a file, a link that leads elsewhere, a name that may not exist).
443#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
444enum ProcfsLevel {
445    /// `/proc`.
446    Proc,
447    /// A process's directory: `self`, or a number.
448    Process,
449    /// A process's `task`.
450    Tasks,
451    /// A thread's directory: a number in `task`.
452    Thread,
453    /// `thread-self`'s thread, which spells back as `thread-self`.
454    ThreadSelf,
455    /// A process's or thread's `fd` or `fdinfo`.
456    Descriptors,
457    /// Anything else.
458    Opaque,
459}
460
461/// A task that a path below `/proc` names by number on the way, which
462/// Linux's walk looks up there and which must exist for the walk to go on
463/// (`proc_pid_lookup`, `proc_task_lookup`, v6.17 `fs/proc/base.c`): a task
464/// by its number at the top (`/proc/<n>`, any thread's number, as Linux finds
465/// any task there), or a thread of a process (`/proc/<n>/task/<m>`), the
466/// process `None` for the caller's own (`self`, `thread-self`).
467#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
468pub(super) struct NamedTask {
469    /// The number at the top, `None` for the caller's own process. A number
470    /// no task can have (one past any `pid_t`) is 0, which names none.
471    pub(super) process: Option<i32>,
472    /// The thread below `task`, if one is named.
473    pub(super) thread: Option<i32>,
474}
475
476/// The tasks Linux's walk looks up to reach the task directory `kind` of
477/// `process` (and `thread`, for a thread's), as
478/// [`fd_directory::classify_task_directory`] names it, for a caller whose pid
479/// and tid are `own`: another process by its number (`proc_pid_lookup`), and
480/// a thread of the process other than the caller below `task`
481/// (`proc_task_lookup`), each found only while a task holds that number.
482/// The caller's own process and thread always exist.
483fn directory_tasks(
484    kind: TaskDirectoryKind,
485    process: i32,
486    thread: i32,
487    own: (i32, i32),
488) -> Vec<NamedTask> {
489    let other = (process != own.0).then_some(process);
490    let mut tasks = Vec::new();
491    if other.is_some() {
492        tasks.push(NamedTask {
493            process: other,
494            thread: None,
495        });
496    }
497    if kind == TaskDirectoryKind::Thread && (process, thread) != own {
498        tasks.push(NamedTask {
499            process: other,
500            thread: Some(thread),
501        });
502    }
503    tasks
504}
505
506/// `path`, absolute, with each `..` resolved as Linux's walk resolves it
507/// where every component a `..` leaves is a directory every procfs has on
508/// the way down from `/proc`, so no symbolic link or file can be on the way:
509/// `self` (a link to the caller's process directory, one level down) and a
510/// numbered process directory, `thread-self` (a link to `<pid>/task/<tid>`,
511/// three levels down), a process's `task` and a numbered thread in it, and
512/// `fd` and `fdinfo`. A `..` at the root stays there. With it, every task the path names
513/// by number on the way, in order ([`NamedTask`]): the walk looks each up, so
514/// the path names what this resolves it to only while each exists, and
515/// fails with `ENOENT` at the first that does not
516/// ([`Detcore::named_tasks_exist`]). `None` for a path without `..`, and for
517/// one whose `..` follows anything else, which only the kernel's walk can
518/// resolve.
519pub(super) fn resolved_procfs_dotdot(path: &Path) -> Option<(PathBuf, Vec<NamedTask>)> {
520    let bytes = path.as_os_str().as_bytes();
521    if !bytes.starts_with(b"/") {
522        return None;
523    }
524    let components = bytes
525        .split(|byte| *byte == b'/')
526        .filter(|component| !component.is_empty() && *component != b".")
527        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
528    if !components.contains(&b"..".as_slice()) {
529        return None;
530    }
531    // A decimal number procfs looks up (no leading zero, as `name_to_int`
532    // takes), as the task number it names: 0 for one no task can have.
533    let number = |name: &[u8]| {
534        (!name.is_empty() && name[0] != b'0' && name.iter().all(u8::is_ascii_digit)).then(|| {
535            std::str::from_utf8(name)
536                .ok()
537                .and_then(|name| name.parse::<i32>().ok())
538                .unwrap_or(0)
539        })
540    };
541    // Each component kept, with what it is, whether `thread-self`'s
542    // expansion pushed it, and the process number it names (for a process
543    // directory: `None` for `self`).
544    let mut kept: Vec<(&[u8], ProcfsLevel, bool, Option<i32>)> = Vec::new();
545    let mut tasks = Vec::new();
546    for name in components {
547        if name == b".." {
548            match kept.last() {
549                None => {}
550                Some((_, ProcfsLevel::Opaque, _, _)) => return None,
551                Some(_) => {
552                    kept.pop();
553                }
554            }
555            continue;
556        }
557        let parent = kept.last().map(|(_, level, _, _)| *level);
558        let level = match (parent, name) {
559            (None, b"proc") => ProcfsLevel::Proc,
560            (Some(ProcfsLevel::Proc), b"self") => {
561                kept.push((name, ProcfsLevel::Process, false, None));
562                continue;
563            }
564            (Some(ProcfsLevel::Proc), b"thread-self") => {
565                kept.push((b"self", ProcfsLevel::Process, true, None));
566                kept.push((b"task", ProcfsLevel::Tasks, true, None));
567                kept.push((b"thread-self", ProcfsLevel::ThreadSelf, true, None));
568                continue;
569            }
570            (Some(ProcfsLevel::Proc), name) if number(name).is_some() => {
571                let process = number(name);
572                tasks.push(NamedTask {
573                    process,
574                    thread: None,
575                });
576                kept.push((name, ProcfsLevel::Process, false, process));
577                continue;
578            }
579            (Some(ProcfsLevel::Process), b"task") => ProcfsLevel::Tasks,
580            (Some(ProcfsLevel::Tasks), name) if number(name).is_some() => {
581                // The process directory two levels up.
582                let process = kept.len().checked_sub(2).and_then(|index| kept[index].3);
583                tasks.push(NamedTask {
584                    process,
585                    thread: number(name),
586                });
587                ProcfsLevel::Thread
588            }
589            (
590                Some(ProcfsLevel::Process | ProcfsLevel::Thread | ProcfsLevel::ThreadSelf),
591                b"fd" | b"fdinfo",
592            ) => ProcfsLevel::Descriptors,
593            _ => ProcfsLevel::Opaque,
594        };
595        kept.push((name, level, false, None));
596    }
597    let mut out = Vec::new();
598    let mut index = 0;
599    while index < kept.len() {
600        // `thread-self`'s expansion, whole, spells back as itself.
601        if kept[index].2
602            && kept
603                .get(index + 2)
604                .is_some_and(|(_, level, _, _)| *level == ProcfsLevel::ThreadSelf)
605        {
606            out.extend_from_slice(b"/thread-self");
607            index += 3;
608            continue;
609        }
610        out.push(b'/');
611        out.extend_from_slice(kept[index].0);
612        index += 1;
613    }
614    if out.is_empty() {
615        out.push(b'/');
616    }
617    Some((PathBuf::from(std::ffi::OsString::from_vec(out)), tasks))
618}
619
620/// The absolute guest path `path`, with each `..` resolved as Linux's walk
621/// resolves it over procfs's own directories ([`resolved_procfs_dotdot`]),
622/// where what it reaches is in the caller's own task tree: `/proc/self`,
623/// `/proc/thread-self` or `/proc/<pid>` and below, for a caller whose pid is
624/// `pid`. `Some` of the resolved path, with a final `/` where `path` ends in
625/// one or in `/.` (Linux then requires a directory, and follows a last
626/// link), or in `..` (which reaches a directory, never a link: the resolved
627/// `/proc/self` of `/proc/self/fd/..` is the directory, not the link), and
628/// the tasks the walk names on the way ([`NamedTask`]), each of which must
629/// exist for the path to name what this resolves it to. `None` for a path
630/// without `..`, one that only the kernel's walk resolves, and one that
631/// leaves the caller's tree (package K slice K10).
632pub(super) fn own_tree_dotdot(path: &[u8], pid: i32) -> Option<(Vec<u8>, Vec<NamedTask>)> {
633    let (resolved, tasks) = resolved_procfs_dotdot(Path::new(OsStr::from_bytes(path)))?;
634    let mut bytes = resolved.into_os_string().into_vec();
635    let pid = pid.to_string();
636    let mut names = bytes
637        .split(|byte| *byte == b'/')
638        .filter(|component| !component.is_empty());
639    if names.next() != Some(b"proc".as_slice()) {
640        return None;
641    }
642    match names.next() {
643        Some(b"self" | b"thread-self") => {}
644        Some(name) if name == pid.as_bytes() => {}
645        _ => return None,
646    }
647    let last_dotdot = walked_components(path).last() == Some(&b"..".as_slice());
648    if (path.ends_with(b"/") || path.ends_with(b"/.") || last_dotdot) && !bytes.ends_with(b"/") {
649        bytes.push(b'/');
650    }
651    Some((bytes, tasks))
652}
653
654/// The absolute guest path `path` without its empty and `.` components,
655/// where it is in the caller's own task tree (`/proc/self`,
656/// `/proc/thread-self` or `/proc/<pid>` and below, for a caller whose pid is
657/// `pid`) and has any: such components name nothing on Linux. A final `/` or
658/// `/.` stays a final `/`, as Linux then requires a directory and follows a
659/// final link. `None` for any other path, and for one already without them
660/// (package K slice K10).
661pub(super) fn own_tree_normalized(path: &[u8], pid: i32) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
662    if !path.starts_with(b"/") {
663        return None;
664    }
665    let names = path
666        .split(|byte| *byte == b'/')
667        .filter(|component| !component.is_empty() && *component != b".")
668        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
669    let pid = pid.to_string();
670    match names.as_slice() {
671        [b"proc", b"self" | b"thread-self", ..] => {}
672        [b"proc", process, ..] if *process == pid.as_bytes() => {}
673        _ => return None,
674    }
675    let mut normalized = Vec::with_capacity(path.len());
676    for name in names {
677        normalized.push(b'/');
678        normalized.extend_from_slice(name);
679    }
680    if path.ends_with(b"/") || path.ends_with(b"/.") {
681        normalized.push(b'/');
682    }
683    (normalized != path).then_some(normalized)
684}
685
686/// The `/proc/self` or `/proc/thread-self` link whose name is the last
687/// component of the absolute guest path `path`, with each `..` before it
688/// resolved as Linux's walk resolves it over procfs's own directories
689/// ([`resolved_procfs_dotdot`]: `/proc/self/../self` and
690/// `/proc/self/fd/../../self` end at `self`,
691/// `/proc/thread-self/../../../thread-self` at `thread-self`): `Some` of the
692/// link's own spelling and the tasks the walk names on the way, each of which
693/// must exist for the path to reach the link ([`NamedTask`]). Whether a call
694/// follows that last component (a final `/` or `/.` always does) is the
695/// caller's to tell (`files::opens_the_link_itself`). `None` for any other
696/// path (package K slice K10).
697fn selector_link_reached(path: &Path) -> Option<(PathBuf, Vec<NamedTask>)> {
698    // The last component must be the link's own name: a last `..` lands on
699    // a directory (`/proc/self/fd/..` is the process's directory), whatever
700    // spelling it resolves to.
701    let last = *walked_components(path.as_os_str().as_bytes()).last()?;
702    if last != b"self" && last != b"thread-self" {
703        return None;
704    }
705    let (resolved, tasks) =
706        resolved_procfs_dotdot(path).unwrap_or_else(|| (path.to_path_buf(), Vec::new()));
707    super::names_a_selector_link(&resolved)
708        .then(|| (Path::new("/proc").join(OsStr::from_bytes(last)), tasks))
709}
710
711/// Whether the absolute spelling `observed` ends in a name and a `/`: a last
712/// component that is no `.` or `..` (nor the root), followed by slashes. An
713/// open with `O_CREAT` of such a spelling fails with `EISDIR` before the
714/// name is looked up, where it names an existing directory that `O_EXCL`
715/// would refuse with `EEXIST` too (`lookup_fast_for_open`, v6.17
716/// fs/namei.c; a last `.` or `..` is no name, `open_last_lookups`).
717pub(super) fn ends_in_a_name_and_slash(observed: &Path) -> bool {
718    let bytes = observed.as_os_str().as_bytes();
719    bytes.ends_with(b"/")
720        && bytes
721            .split(|byte| *byte == b'/')
722            .rfind(|component| !component.is_empty())
723            .is_some_and(|last| last != b"." && last != b"..")
724}
725
726/// The absolute spelling `bytes` without its last component and the
727/// slashes around it: the directory the walk is in when it reaches that
728/// component (`/proc/self/task` of `/proc/self/task/9/`, `/` of `/proc/`).
729/// `None` for a spelling with no component.
730fn before_the_last_name(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<&[u8]> {
731    let trimmed = &bytes[..bytes.iter().rposition(|byte| *byte != b'/')? + 1];
732    let parent = &trimmed[..trimmed.iter().rposition(|byte| *byte == b'/')?];
733    Some(if parent.is_empty() { b"/" } else { parent })
734}
735
736/// The components of the absolute path `bytes` that Linux's walk looks up,
737/// without the empty and `.` ones, which name nothing.
738fn walked_components(bytes: &[u8]) -> Vec<&[u8]> {
739    bytes
740        .split(|byte| *byte == b'/')
741        .filter(|component| !component.is_empty() && *component != b".")
742        .collect()
743}
744
745/// Whether the absolute guest path `path` names `/proc` itself: its
746/// components, with the empty and `.` ones left out, are `proc` alone, or
747/// reach it back through `..` from directories every procfs has
748/// ([`resolved_procfs_dotdot`]), as `Some` of the tasks that spelling names
749/// by number on the way, each of which must exist for it to
750/// ([`Detcore::named_tasks_exist`]). Any other spelling is the kernel's to
751/// resolve.
752fn names_procfs_root(path: &Path) -> Option<Vec<NamedTask>> {
753    let (resolved, tasks) =
754        resolved_procfs_dotdot(path).unwrap_or_else(|| (path.to_path_buf(), Vec::new()));
755    (resolved.as_os_str().as_bytes().starts_with(b"/")
756        && resolved
757            .components()
758            .filter(|component| *component != std::path::Component::CurDir)
759            .eq(Path::new("/proc").components()))
760    .then_some(tasks)
761}
762
763/// Whether the absolute path `path` spells a descriptor directory, the
764/// caller's or another task's, whatever its numbers: `/proc/<n>/fd`,
765/// `/proc/<n>/task/<m>/fd` (`<n>` a number, `self` or `thread-self`), or
766/// `/dev/fd`, with empty and `.` components left out
767/// ([`Detcore::refuses_backend_procfs_directory`]).
768fn spells_a_descriptor_directory(path: &Path) -> bool {
769    let task = |name: &str| {
770        name == "self"
771            || name == "thread-self"
772            || (!name.is_empty() && name.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_digit()))
773    };
774    let names = path
775        .components()
776        .filter_map(|component| match component {
777            std::path::Component::Normal(name) => name.to_str(),
778            _ => None,
779        })
780        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
781    path.is_absolute()
782        && !path
783            .components()
784            .any(|component| component == std::path::Component::ParentDir)
785        && match names.as_slice() {
786            ["dev", "fd"] => true,
787            ["proc", pid, "fd"] => task(pid),
788            ["proc", pid, "task", tid, "fd"] => {
789                task(pid) && !tid.is_empty() && tid.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_digit())
790            }
791            _ => false,
792        }
793}
794
795/// The leading descriptor link of an absolute path (`/proc/self/fd/D`,
796/// `/dev/fd/D`, `/proc/thread-self/fd/D`, `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/fd/D`),
797/// spelled canonically, and the index in `path` just past its last
798/// component, whatever follows. Empty and `.` components before the
799/// descriptor's number name nothing, as in Linux's walk, and are skipped
800/// (`//proc/self/fd/D`, `/proc//self/fd/D`, `/proc/./self/fd/D`). The link's
801/// components are ASCII. Any other spelling (a `..` before the number, a
802/// symbolic link of the guest's to `/proc/self/fd/D`) is not recognized
803/// (named residual).
804fn descriptor_link_prefix(path: &[u8]) -> Option<(PathBuf, usize)> {
805    if !path.starts_with(b"/") {
806        return None;
807    }
808    let mut names: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
809    let mut at = 0;
810    loop {
811        while path.get(at) == Some(&b'/') {
812            at += 1;
813        }
814        let start = at;
815        while at < path.len() && path[at] != b'/' {
816            at += 1;
817        }
818        if start == at {
819            return None;
820        }
821        let name = &path[start..at];
822        if name == b"." {
823            continue;
824        }
825        if !name.is_ascii() {
826            return None;
827        }
828        names.push(std::str::from_utf8(name).ok()?);
829        match names.as_slice() {
830            ["dev", "fd", _] | ["proc", _, "fd", _] | ["proc", _, "task", _, "fd", _] => break,
831            ["dev"]
832            | ["dev", "fd"]
833            | ["proc"]
834            | ["proc", _]
835            | ["proc", _, "fd"]
836            | ["proc", _, "task"]
837            | ["proc", _, "task", _]
838            | ["proc", _, "task", _, "fd"] => {}
839            _ => return None,
840        }
841    }
842    Some((PathBuf::from(format!("/{}", names.join("/"))), at))
843}
844
845/// The leading descriptor link and the rest of an absolute path that leads
846/// through one ([`descriptor_link_prefix`]), if the path goes on past the
847/// link: a rest, or a final `/`, which follows the link (an empty rest then,
848/// the directory itself). The rest is kept byte for byte, whatever it holds.
849fn through_descriptor_link(path: &[u8]) -> Option<(PathBuf, Vec<u8>)> {
850    let (link, end) = descriptor_link_prefix(path)?;
851    let rest = &path[end..];
852    if rest.is_empty() {
853        return None;
854    }
855    let rest = &rest[rest.iter().take_while(|byte| **byte == b'/').count()..];
856    Some((link, rest.to_vec()))
857}
858
859/// How many symbolic links Linux follows to reach the directory of the
860/// descriptor link `link` ([`through_descriptor_link`]) before the link
861/// itself: `/dev/fd` (a link to `/proc/self/fd`) and then `self`, 2;
862/// `/proc/self` or `/proc/thread-self`, 1; a numbered task, 0. Each of these
863/// counts against the lookup's `MAXSYMLINKS`, as the descriptor link does
864/// (`pick_link`'s `total_link_count`).
865fn selector_links(link: &Path) -> usize {
866    let mut names = link.components().filter_map(|component| match component {
867        std::path::Component::Normal(name) => Some(name),
868        _ => None,
869    });
870    match (names.next(), names.next()) {
871        (Some(first), _) if first == "dev" => 2,
872        (_, Some(second)) if second == "self" || second == "thread-self" => 1,
873        _ => 0,
874    }
875}
876
877/// procfs's largest pathname, `PATH_MAX`, with its NUL.
878const PATH_LIMIT: usize = libc::PATH_MAX as usize;
879
880/// How many descriptor links to placeholder directories a respelled path may
881/// lead through, as Linux's lookup follows at most `MAXSYMLINKS` links
882/// (<https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.17/include/linux/namei.h>).
883/// The same bound limits the `self` and `thread-self` links a `..` spelling
884/// of a rendered file passes (`walk_lock_or_fdinfo_alias`).
885pub(super) const LINK_LIMIT: usize = 40;
886
887/// One path operand of a call, respelled as Linux resolves it below a
888/// placeholder directory ([`Detcore::respelled_below_descriptor_directory`]).
889#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
890pub(crate) struct RespelledOperand {
891    /// The index of the call's directory descriptor argument, which the
892    /// respelled call gives as `AT_FDCWD`, for a path respelled absolutely;
893    /// `None` for one looked up from where the guest's was (the same
894    /// descriptor, or the working directory).
895    pub(crate) dirfd: Option<usize>,
896    /// The index of the path argument.
897    pub(crate) path: usize,
898    /// The respelled path, without its NUL.
899    pub(crate) bytes: Vec<u8>,
900}
901
902/// The argument of a call that names the descriptor it operates on, for the
903/// calls Detcore answers through a placeholder descriptor
904/// ([`Detcore::answer_placeholder_call`]): `Some(index)` for a call that
905/// operates on its descriptor operand `index`, the path calls among them that
906/// name it with an empty path and `AT_EMPTY_PATH` (or, for `utimensat`, no
907/// path at all). Every other call that names a descriptor reaches it as the
908/// kernel's descriptor table does (`close`, the `dup`s, `fcntl`'s
909/// table commands, `poll` and `epoll`, which see a directory with no `poll`
910/// method alike on every file system), or looks a name up below it
911/// ([`relative_operands`]).
912fn placeholder_operand<M: MemoryAccess>(
913    memory: &M,
914    sysno: Sysno,
915    args: &syscalls::SyscallArgs,
916) -> Option<usize> {
917    let names_itself = |path: usize, flags: usize| {
918        args_flag(args, flags) & libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH != 0
919            && PathPtr::from_ptr(argument(args, path) as *const libc::c_char).is_some_and(
920                |pointer| {
921                    pointer
922                        .read(memory)
923                        .is_ok_and(|path: PathBuf| path.as_os_str().is_empty())
924                },
925            )
926    };
927    match sysno {
928        Sysno::getdents64
929        | Sysno::lseek
930        | Sysno::fchdir
931        | Sysno::fcntl
932        | Sysno::read
933        | Sysno::readv
934        | Sysno::pread64
935        | Sysno::preadv
936        | Sysno::preadv2
937        | Sysno::write
938        | Sysno::writev
939        | Sysno::pwrite64
940        | Sysno::pwritev
941        | Sysno::pwritev2
942        | Sysno::ioctl
943        | Sysno::fsync
944        | Sysno::fdatasync
945        | Sysno::syncfs
946        | Sysno::flock
947        | Sysno::fchmod
948        | Sysno::fchown
949        | Sysno::fgetxattr
950        | Sysno::fsetxattr
951        | Sysno::flistxattr
952        | Sysno::fremovexattr
953        | Sysno::ftruncate
954        | Sysno::fallocate
955        | Sysno::fadvise64
956        | Sysno::readahead
957        | Sysno::fstatfs => Some(0),
958        // x86_64 only: aarch64 has getdents64 alone (above).
959        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
960        Sysno::getdents | Sysno::sync_file_range => Some(0),
961        // aarch64 number 84 is sys_sync_file_range with the same
962        // (fd, offset, nbytes, flags) order: arm64 does not select
963        // __ARCH_WANT_SYNC_FILE_RANGE2.
964        #[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]
965        Sysno::sync_file_range => Some(0),
966        // A file-backed mapping only: an anonymous one ignores its
967        // descriptor argument (`ksys_mmap_pgoff`).
968        Sysno::mmap if args.arg3 as i32 & libc::MAP_ANONYMOUS == 0 => Some(4),
969        // `futimens`: no path at all names the descriptor itself.
970        Sysno::utimensat if args.arg1 == 0 => Some(0),
971        Sysno::utimensat if names_itself(1, 3) => Some(0),
972        Sysno::fchmodat2 if names_itself(1, 3) => Some(0),
973        Sysno::fchownat if names_itself(1, 4) => Some(0),
974        Sysno::faccessat2 if names_itself(1, 3) => Some(0),
975        // `name_to_handle_at` is not here: Detcore answers every one with
976        // `EOPNOTSUPP` (`Detcore::handle_syscall_event`), Linux's answer for
977        // procfs, which exports no handles.
978        _ => None,
979    }
980}
981
982/// The flags argument `index` of a call, as an `int`.
983fn args_flag(args: &syscalls::SyscallArgs, index: usize) -> i32 {
984    argument(args, index) as i32
985}
986
987impl<T: RecordOrReplay> Detcore<T> {
988    /// Whether Detcore serves placeholder directories in this run: where the
989    /// object it opens for one exists ([`PlaceholderObject`]: the container's
990    /// own procfs, `DetConfig::container_procfs`, where the guest's procfs is
991    /// the kernel's; the placeholder directory hermit-cli mounted,
992    /// `DetConfig::directory_placeholder`, where it is not, which neither
993    /// `--no-namespace`, a library caller nor a user mount over it leaves),
994    /// where Detcore keeps the guest's descriptor table (not the in-guest
995    /// backends, above), and renders procfs files (not under record and
996    /// replay). A descriptor that is one stays one, and is answered as one,
997    /// whatever its holder does later (a `chroot`, a nested PID namespace):
998    /// no placeholder is ever handed to the backend unanswered.
999    fn serves_placeholders<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> bool {
1000        let config = guest.config();
1001        let object_exists = match Self::placeholder_object(guest) {
1002            PlaceholderObject::ProcfsRoot => config.container_procfs,
1003            PlaceholderObject::Placeholder => config.directory_placeholder.is_some(),
1004        };
1005        object_exists
1006            && !config.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table
1007            && !config.recordreplay_modes
1008    }
1009
1010    /// The caller's pid and tid as the guest numbers them, where Detcore
1011    /// serves a new placeholder directory to the caller now
1012    /// ([`Self::serves_placeholders`]), and classifies the caller's spellings
1013    /// of the directories and of its descriptor links: where it knows which
1014    /// `/proc` its absolute paths reach (not after a guest `chroot`) and the
1015    /// caller's numbers (not in a nested PID namespace, nor before its
1016    /// process is known). Read from the thread's state alone, so that each
1017    /// injection can ask ([`Self::respelled_below_descriptor_directory`]).
1018    fn descriptor_directory_caller<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> Option<(i32, i32)> {
1019        let state = guest.thread_state();
1020        if !Self::serves_placeholders(guest)
1021            || state.changed_root.load(Ordering::SeqCst)
1022            || state.nested_pid_namespace
1023        {
1024            return None;
1025        }
1026        Some((state.detpid?.as_raw(), state.dettid.as_raw()))
1027    }
1028
1029    /// Whether Detcore serves a new placeholder directory to the caller now
1030    /// ([`Self::descriptor_directory_caller`]).
1031    fn serves_descriptor_directories<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &G) -> bool {
1032        Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest).is_some()
1033    }
1034
1035    /// Whether Detcore serves the task directories
1036    /// ([`PlaceholderDirectory::Task`]) of its PID namespace to the caller
1037    /// now, the caller's own (package K slice K10) and every other process's
1038    /// (package K item 2b slice 2b-1): where it serves it placeholder
1039    /// directories ([`Self::descriptor_directory_caller`], which a nested PID
1040    /// namespace has none of) and the guest's procfs is not the kernel's
1041    /// ([`PlaceholderObject::Placeholder`]: KVM, whose executor serves no
1042    /// procfs directory). Where it is (ptrace), the kernel serves them, and
1043    /// Detcore lists them from its model
1044    /// (`Detcore::modeled_directory_snapshot`), as before.
1045    pub(crate) fn serves_task_directories<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> bool {
1046        Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest).is_some()
1047            && Self::placeholder_object(guest) == PlaceholderObject::Placeholder
1048    }
1049
1050    /// The spelling the backend is given, where Detcore serves the caller's
1051    /// own task directories ([`Self::serves_task_directories`], package K
1052    /// slice K10), for `bytes`, an absolute path an injection carries: `..`
1053    /// resolved within the caller's own task tree ([`own_tree_dotdot`]), then
1054    /// a file of its thread group leader's own directory spelled as its
1055    /// process's ([`fd_directory::leader_process_spelling`]), which is the
1056    /// only spelling of it the KVM executor's `/proc` answers, and otherwise
1057    /// a spelling in that tree without its empty and `.` components
1058    /// ([`own_tree_normalized`]), which name nothing on Linux and which the
1059    /// executor does not take (`//proc/self`). `Ok(None)` where none of
1060    /// these applies, and `Err` where Linux's walk of `bytes` fails first:
1061    /// `ENOENT` at a task it names on the way that does not exist, `ELOOP`
1062    /// once it follows more `self` and `thread-self` links than
1063    /// `MAXSYMLINKS` (`rendered_procfs::past_the_link_limit`).
1064    pub(crate) async fn own_task_backend_spelling<G: Guest<Self>>(
1065        guest: &mut G,
1066        bytes: &[u8],
1067    ) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, Errno> {
1068        let Some(own) = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest) else {
1069            return Ok(None);
1070        };
1071        let resolved = Self::own_tree_resolved(guest, bytes, true).await?;
1072        let path = resolved.as_deref().unwrap_or(bytes);
1073        Ok(fd_directory::leader_process_spelling(path, own)
1074            .or(resolved)
1075            .or_else(|| own_tree_normalized(bytes, own.0)))
1076    }
1077
1078    /// The absolute guest path `bytes` with each `..` resolved within the
1079    /// caller's own task tree ([`own_tree_dotdot`]), where Detcore serves
1080    /// the caller's own task directories, after Linux's walk of it succeeds
1081    /// ([`Self::procfs_walk`], the last component's link counted where the
1082    /// call follows it, `follows_last`): `Ok(Some(resolved))`, `Ok(None)` for
1083    /// a path `own_tree_dotdot` does not resolve, `Err` where the walk fails
1084    /// first (package K slice K10).
1085    pub(crate) async fn own_tree_resolved<G: Guest<Self>>(
1086        guest: &mut G,
1087        bytes: &[u8],
1088        follows_last: bool,
1089    ) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, Errno> {
1090        let Some(own) = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest) else {
1091            return Ok(None);
1092        };
1093        let Some((resolved, tasks)) = own_tree_dotdot(bytes, own.0) else {
1094            return Ok(None);
1095        };
1096        Self::procfs_walk(guest, bytes, &tasks, follows_last, 0).await?;
1097        Ok(Some(resolved))
1098    }
1099
1100    /// Whether Linux's walk of the absolute guest path `bytes`, whose `..`
1101    /// all resolve over procfs's own directories, gets past its components
1102    /// to the last: `ELOOP` once it would follow more `self` and
1103    /// `thread-self` links than `MAXSYMLINKS`, after the `followed` links
1104    /// the lookup followed before `bytes` (a respelled path's, from
1105    /// [`Self::decided_path`]; 0 for the guest's own spelling), the last
1106    /// component's counted only where the call follows it (`follows_last`;
1107    /// `rendered_procfs::past_the_link_limit`), unless a task named before
1108    /// the first link past the limit does not exist (`ENOENT` there, which
1109    /// the walk meets first); then `ENOENT` at the first of `tasks`, the
1110    /// tasks the path names on the way, that does not exist
1111    /// ([`Self::named_tasks_exist`]).
1112    async fn procfs_walk<G: Guest<Self>>(
1113        guest: &mut G,
1114        bytes: &[u8],
1115        tasks: &[NamedTask],
1116        follows_last: bool,
1117        followed: usize,
1118    ) -> Result<(), Errno> {
1119        let components = walked_components(bytes);
1120        let walked = if follows_last {
1121            &components[..]
1122        } else {
1123            &components[..components.len().saturating_sub(1)]
1124        };
1125        if let Some(before) = super::rendered_procfs::past_the_link_limit(walked, followed) {
1126            return Err(if Self::named_tasks_exist(guest, &before).await {
1127                Errno::ELOOP
1128            } else {
1129                Errno::ENOENT
1130            });
1131        }
1132        if !Self::named_tasks_exist(guest, tasks).await {
1133            return Err(Errno::ENOENT);
1134        }
1135        Ok(())
1136    }
1137
1138    /// The caller's `/proc/self` or `/proc/thread-self` link that the
1139    /// absolute guest spelling `spelling` reaches as its last component
1140    /// ([`selector_link_reached`], `..` resolved), where Detcore serves the
1141    /// caller's own task directories and the call does not follow that
1142    /// component (the caller tells, `files::opens_the_link_itself` on the
1143    /// guest's own spelling): `Some(Ok(link))`, the link's own spelling, or
1144    /// `Some(Err(errno))` where Linux's walk to it fails first
1145    /// ([`Self::procfs_walk`], the link itself not followed, after the
1146    /// `followed` links of a respelled path). `None` for any other spelling,
1147    /// which is no link of these (package K slice K10).
1148    async fn own_selector_link<G: Guest<Self>>(
1149        guest: &mut G,
1150        spelling: &Path,
1151        followed: usize,
1152    ) -> Option<Result<PathBuf, Errno>> {
1153        if !Self::serves_task_directories(guest) {
1154            return None;
1155        }
1156        let (link, tasks) = selector_link_reached(spelling)?;
1157        Some(
1158            Self::procfs_walk(
1159                guest,
1160                spelling.as_os_str().as_bytes(),
1161                &tasks,
1162                false,
1163                followed,
1164            )
1165            .await
1166            .map(|()| link),
1167        )
1168    }
1169
1170    /// The named refusal (ARCHITECTURE.md rule 6) of a call that looks up
1171    /// the `status` of a thread of the caller's process other than its thread
1172    /// group leader, through the caller's own task tree
1173    /// (`/proc/thread-self/status` from such a thread,
1174    /// `/proc/self/task/<tid>/status`), where Detcore serves the caller's own
1175    /// task directories ([`Self::serves_task_directories`], package K slice
1176    /// K10), and only where Detcore does not render that thread's `status`
1177    /// itself: the KVM executor's `/proc` renders its process's alone, which
1178    /// on Linux is the leader's, not the thread's (`Pid`, `Name`, its signal
1179    /// and context-switch lines, `proc_pid_status`). Whether Detcore renders
1180    /// it is the open's own decision (`Detcore::kept_by_kernel`):
1181    /// `Detcore::renders_any_status` and
1182    /// `procfs::task_status::renders_task_status` for the thread, which since
1183    /// package K item 2b slice 2b-2 holds for every thread with a recorded
1184    /// name on KVM. The leader's own is its process's, which the
1185    /// backend answers ([`Self::own_task_backend_spelling`]). The call's path
1186    /// is classified as the backend would be given it, by that same
1187    /// respelling (`..` resolved, empty and `.` components left out:
1188    /// `/proc/thread-self/./status`, `/proc/self/./task/<tid>/status`), so
1189    /// that no spelling of the file reaches the backend unrefused. Left to the
1190    /// lookup: a path whose walk fails first (`ENOENT` at a thread that does
1191    /// not exist, `ELOOP` past the link limit), and one with a final `/` or
1192    /// `/.`, which asks for a directory and fails at the file with `ENOTDIR`
1193    /// on Linux and on the backend alike.
1194    pub(crate) async fn refuse_unrendered_thread_status<G: Guest<Self>>(
1195        &self,
1196        guest: &mut G,
1197        call: &Syscall,
1198    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
1199        if !Self::serves_task_directories(guest) {
1200            return None;
1201        }
1202        let own = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest)?;
1203        let (sysno, args) = (*call).into_parts();
1204        let operands = super::procfs_levels::path_operands(sysno)?;
1205        let values = [
1206            args.arg0, args.arg1, args.arg2, args.arg3, args.arg4, args.arg5,
1207        ];
1208        for &(dirfd, argument) in operands {
1209            let Some(spelling): Option<PathBuf> =
1210                PathPtr::from_ptr(values[argument] as *const libc::c_char)
1211                    .and_then(|address| address.read(&guest.memory()).ok())
1212            else {
1213                continue;
1214            };
1215            if spelling.as_os_str().is_empty() {
1216                continue;
1217            }
1218            let dirfd = dirfd.map_or(libc::AT_FDCWD, |position| values[position] as i32);
1219            Self::prepare_guest_cwd(guest, dirfd, &spelling).await;
1220            let Some(observed) = Self::observed_path(guest, dirfd, &spelling)
1221                .ok()
1222                .filter(|observed| observed.is_absolute())
1223            else {
1224                continue;
1225            };
1226            let observed = if Self::starts_at_a_recorded_link(guest, dirfd, &spelling) {
1227                observed
1228            } else {
1229                Self::decided_path(guest, &observed).map_or(observed, |(decided, _)| decided)
1230            };
1231            // As the backend would be given it: a walk that fails first is
1232            // the lookup's to answer.
1233            let observed_bytes = observed.as_os_str().as_bytes();
1234            let named = match Self::own_task_backend_spelling(guest, observed_bytes).await {
1235                Ok(spelled) => spelled.unwrap_or_else(|| observed_bytes.to_vec()),
1236                Err(_) => continue,
1237            };
1238            if named.ends_with(b"/") || named.ends_with(b"/.") {
1239                continue;
1240            }
1241            let names = walked_components(&named)
1242                .into_iter()
1243                .map(<[u8]>::to_vec)
1244                .collect::<Vec<_>>();
1245            let pid = own.0.to_string().into_bytes();
1246            let number = |name: &[u8]| -> Option<i32> {
1247                (name.first().is_some_and(|byte| *byte != b'0')
1248                    && name.iter().all(u8::is_ascii_digit))
1249                .then(|| std::str::from_utf8(name).ok()?.parse().ok())
1250                .flatten()
1251            };
1252            let thread = match names
1253                .iter()
1254                .map(Vec::as_slice)
1255                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
1256                .as_slice()
1257            {
1258                [b"proc", b"thread-self", b"status"] if own.1 != own.0 => own.1,
1259                [b"proc", process, b"task", thread, b"status"]
1260                    if (*process == b"self" || *process == pid.as_slice())
1261                        && number(thread).is_some_and(|thread| thread != own.0) =>
1262                {
1263                    number(thread).unwrap_or_default()
1264                }
1265                _ => continue,
1266            };
1267            // The tasks named on the way exist (the respelling checked them).
1268            let status_thread = NamedTask {
1269                process: None,
1270                thread: Some(thread),
1271            };
1272            if !Self::named_tasks_exist(guest, &[status_thread]).await {
1273                continue;
1274            }
1275            // A thread whose `status` Detcore renders is served on the
1276            // rendered path, by the same predicate as its open
1277            // (`Detcore::kept_by_kernel`).
1278            if self.renders_any_status() {
1279                let task = crate::procfs::BoundTask {
1280                    process: own.0,
1281                    thread: Some(thread),
1282                };
1283                let facts = crate::tool_global::task_stat_facts(guest, task).await;
1284                if crate::procfs::task_status::renders_task_status(&facts, self.comm_source(), true)
1285                {
1286                    continue;
1287                }
1288            }
1289            return Some(
1290                self.refuse_placeholder_call(
1291                    guest,
1292                    sysno,
1293                    Errno::EACCES,
1294                    &format!(
1295                        "{}, the status of thread {thread} of this process, which is not its \
1296                         thread group leader, cannot be served: Detcore renders no status of this \
1297                         thread here, and the backend's procfs renders the process's alone, which \
1298                         Linux gives the leader (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/5039)",
1299                        observed.display()
1300                    ),
1301                )
1302                .await,
1303            );
1304        }
1305        None
1306    }
1307
1308    /// The task directory of the caller's PID namespace the absolute guest
1309    /// spelling `spelling` names ([`fd_directory::classify_task_directory`]:
1310    /// the caller's own, package K slice K10, or another process's, slice
1311    /// 2b-1), where Detcore serves them ([`Self::serves_task_directories`]),
1312    /// with each `..` resolved as Linux's walk resolves it over procfs's own
1313    /// directories ([`resolved_procfs_dotdot`]): `Some(Ok((kind, process,
1314    /// thread)))`, or `Some(Err(errno))` where Linux's walk of the spelling
1315    /// stops first: `ENOENT` at a task it names on the way that does not
1316    /// exist, the directory's own process and thread included
1317    /// ([`Self::named_tasks_exist`]),
1318    /// and `ELOOP` once it would follow more `self` and `thread-self` links
1319    /// than `MAXSYMLINKS`, the final one included, which an open or stat that
1320    /// names the directory follows, after the `followed` links of a respelled
1321    /// path ([`Self::decided_path`]; `rendered_procfs::past_the_link_limit`).
1322    /// `None` for any other spelling, which the backend answers, as before.
1323    pub(super) async fn own_task_directory<G: Guest<Self>>(
1324        guest: &mut G,
1325        spelling: &Path,
1326        followed: usize,
1327    ) -> Option<Result<(TaskDirectoryKind, i32, i32), Errno>> {
1328        if !Self::serves_task_directories(guest) {
1329            return None;
1330        }
1331        let own = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest)?;
1332        let (resolved, mut tasks) = resolved_procfs_dotdot(spelling)
1333            .unwrap_or_else(|| (spelling.to_path_buf(), Vec::new()));
1334        let (kind, process, thread) = fd_directory::classify_task_directory(&resolved, own)?;
1335        tasks.extend(directory_tasks(kind, process, thread, own));
1336        Some(
1337            Self::procfs_walk(
1338                guest,
1339                spelling.as_os_str().as_bytes(),
1340                &tasks,
1341                true,
1342                followed,
1343            )
1344            .await
1345            .map(|()| (kind, process, thread)),
1346        )
1347    }
1348
1349    /// Whether the absolute guest spelling `bytes` names a directory Detcore
1350    /// serves the caller on a placeholder, where it serves the caller's own
1351    /// task directories (KVM): `/proc` itself, one of the caller's own
1352    /// descriptor directories ([`fd_directory::classify`]) or task
1353    /// directories ([`fd_directory::classify_task_directory`]), by the
1354    /// spellings Detcore resolves: through the caller's descriptor links to
1355    /// placeholder directories ([`Self::decided_path`], whose `..` can reach
1356    /// the root directory, which is no link either, and whose links count
1357    /// against the walk's limit), `/dev/fd` followed where it is the link to
1358    /// `/proc/self/fd` it is on Linux, and with each `..` resolved over
1359    /// procfs's own directories ([`resolved_procfs_dotdot`]). A spelling
1360    /// that leaves `/proc` on the way (a symbolic link elsewhere,
1361    /// `/proc/self/root`) is not one of them. For `readlink`, which fails with `EINVAL`
1362    /// for anything that is no link (`do_readlinkat`): `Some(Ok(()))` for
1363    /// such a directory, `Some(Err(errno))` where the walk to it fails first
1364    /// ([`Self::procfs_walk`]: `ENOENT` at a task named on the way, a thread's
1365    /// directory's own task included, that does not exist; `ELOOP` past the
1366    /// link limit). `None` for any other spelling (package K slice K10).
1367    pub(crate) async fn served_directory_walk<G: Guest<Self>>(
1368        &self,
1369        guest: &mut G,
1370        bytes: &[u8],
1371    ) -> Option<Result<(), Errno>> {
1372        if !Self::serves_task_directories(guest) {
1373            return None;
1374        }
1375        let own = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest)?;
1376        let path = Path::new(OsStr::from_bytes(bytes));
1377        if (bytes.ends_with(b"/") || bytes.ends_with(b"/."))
1378            && path.components().eq(Path::new("/dev/fd").components())
1379            && self
1380                .injected_link_target(guest, b"/dev/fd")
1381                .await
1382                .as_deref()
1383                == Some(Path::new("/proc/self/fd"))
1384        {
1385            return Some(Ok(()));
1386        }
1387        let (decided, followed) = match Self::decided_path(guest, path) {
1388            Ok(decided) => decided,
1389            Err(errno) => return Some(Err(errno)),
1390        };
1391        let through_link = decided.as_path() != path;
1392        let (resolved, mut tasks) =
1393            resolved_procfs_dotdot(&decided).unwrap_or_else(|| (decided.clone(), Vec::new()));
1394        let names = |directory: &str| {
1395            resolved.as_os_str().as_bytes().starts_with(b"/")
1396                && resolved
1397                    .components()
1398                    .filter(|component| *component != std::path::Component::CurDir)
1399                    .eq(Path::new(directory).components())
1400        };
1401        let served = names("/proc")
1402            || (through_link && names("/"))
1403            || fd_directory::classify(&resolved, own).is_some()
1404            || match fd_directory::classify_task_directory(&resolved, own) {
1405                Some((kind, process, thread)) => {
1406                    tasks.extend(directory_tasks(kind, process, thread, own));
1407                    true
1408                }
1409                None => false,
1410            };
1411        if !served {
1412            return None;
1413        }
1414        Some(
1415            Self::procfs_walk(
1416                guest,
1417                decided.as_os_str().as_bytes(),
1418                &tasks,
1419                true,
1420                followed,
1421            )
1422            .await,
1423        )
1424    }
1425
1426    /// The answer to an `O_CREAT` open of the absolute guest spelling
1427    /// `spelling` of one of the caller's own task directories
1428    /// ([`Self::own_task_directory`]'s spellings, whether or not the task
1429    /// exists), where it ends in a name and a `/`
1430    /// ([`ends_in_a_name_and_slash`]): Linux's walk reaches that last name
1431    /// and fails with `EISDIR` before it looks the name up
1432    /// (`lookup_fast_for_open`, v6.17 fs/namei.c), so the only earlier
1433    /// failure is the walk of the components before it
1434    /// ([`Self::procfs_walk`]: `ENOENT` at a task they name that does not
1435    /// exist, `ELOOP` past the link limit, after the `followed` links of a
1436    /// respelled path). `None` for any other spelling, or where Detcore does
1437    /// not serve those directories.
1438    async fn own_task_creation_through_slash<G: Guest<Self>>(
1439        guest: &mut G,
1440        spelling: &Path,
1441        followed: usize,
1442    ) -> Option<Errno> {
1443        if !Self::serves_task_directories(guest) {
1444            return None;
1445        }
1446        let own = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest)?;
1447        let (resolved, _) = resolved_procfs_dotdot(spelling)
1448            .unwrap_or_else(|| (spelling.to_path_buf(), Vec::new()));
1449        fd_directory::classify_task_directory(&resolved, own)?;
1450        let parent = before_the_last_name(spelling.as_os_str().as_bytes())?;
1451        let (_, tasks) = resolved_procfs_dotdot(Path::new(OsStr::from_bytes(parent)))
1452            .unwrap_or_else(|| (PathBuf::new(), Vec::new()));
1453        Some(
1454            Self::procfs_walk(guest, parent, &tasks, true, followed)
1455                .await
1456                .err()
1457                .unwrap_or(Errno::EISDIR),
1458        )
1459    }
1460
1461    /// The caller's pid and tid as Detcore numbers its tasks, which a
1462    /// placeholder's binding names its task by, in every state of the caller
1463    /// (after a `chroot`, in a nested PID namespace): for telling whether a
1464    /// descriptor directory's table is the caller's own. `None` before the
1465    /// caller's process is known.
1466    fn caller_numbers<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> Option<(i32, i32)> {
1467        let state = guest.thread_state();
1468        Some((state.detpid?.as_raw(), state.dettid.as_raw()))
1469    }
1470
1471    /// What a placeholder descriptor is open on in this run
1472    /// ([`PlaceholderObject`]), by the one capability that says whether the
1473    /// guest's procfs is the kernel's.
1474    fn placeholder_object<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> PlaceholderObject {
1475        if procfs_inode::tool_can_name_guest_descriptors(&guest.config().backend) {
1476            PlaceholderObject::ProcfsRoot
1477        } else {
1478            PlaceholderObject::Placeholder
1479        }
1480    }
1481
1482    /// The placeholder state of descriptor `fd`, if it is one.
1483    pub(crate) fn placeholder_of<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G, fd: RawFd) -> Option<PlaceholderState> {
1484        if fd < 0 {
1485            return None;
1486        }
1487        guest
1488            .thread_state()
1489            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.placeholder())
1490            .ok()
1491            .flatten()
1492    }
1493
1494    /// The placeholder directory that the caller's descriptor link `path`
1495    /// (the whole of it: `/proc/self/fd/N`, `/dev/fd/N`, ...) reaches, if it
1496    /// is one. A process's link (`/proc/self/fd/N`) reads the thread group
1497    /// leader's table, so a thread that holds a table of its own (cloned
1498    /// without `CLONE_FILES`) does not answer it from its own; a thread's
1499    /// link (`/proc/thread-self/fd/N`) reads the caller's.
1500    fn own_link_directory<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G, path: &Path) -> Option<PlaceholderDirectory> {
1501        let own = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest)?;
1502        Self::own_link_directory_of(guest, path, own)
1503    }
1504
1505    /// [`Self::own_link_directory`] for a caller whose pid and tid are `own`,
1506    /// for a link spelled within the procfs a placeholder's object is
1507    /// (`/proc/<pid>/fd/N` below procfs's root, `self` the caller's process
1508    /// there), which is the caller's in every state of the caller.
1509    fn own_link_directory_of<G: Guest<Self>>(
1510        guest: &G,
1511        path: &Path,
1512        own: (i32, i32),
1513    ) -> Option<PlaceholderDirectory> {
1514        let link = crate::syscalls::namespace::own_descriptor_link(path, Some(own))?;
1515        if link.table == crate::syscalls::namespace::DescriptorTable::Process {
1516            let state = guest.thread_state();
1517            if state.dettid.as_raw() != own.0 && !state.holds_process_files() {
1518                return None;
1519            }
1520        }
1521        Self::placeholder_of(guest, link.fd).map(|state| state.directory)
1522    }
1523
1524    /// The placeholder directory that an open of `observed` (the guest's
1525    /// absolute spelling) names, whose procfs spelling is `spelling`
1526    /// (`Detcore::procfs_spelling_at`), if Detcore serves it: one of the
1527    /// caller's own descriptor directories ([`fd_directory::classify`]),
1528    /// `/proc` itself by any spelling that reaches it ([`names_procfs_root`]),
1529    /// `/dev/fd` where it is the symbolic link to `/proc/self/fd` it is on
1530    /// Linux, or a descriptor link of the caller's to a placeholder. The last
1531    /// two are symbolic links: an open with `O_NOFOLLOW` that does not follow
1532    /// them anyway (a trailing `/`) opens the link itself (`O_PATH`) or fails
1533    /// with `ELOOP`, which the kernel answers. Linux refuses the flags
1534    /// `build_open_flags` refuses before it looks any name up, so where
1535    /// Detcore answers the open at all, those flags are its answer, before
1536    /// any the walk would give (v6.17 fs/open.c, `do_sys_openat2`); an open
1537    /// Detcore then serves checks the rest (`open_placeholder`).
1538    pub(super) async fn descriptor_directory_opened<G: Guest<Self>>(
1539        &self,
1540        guest: &mut G,
1541        observed: &Path,
1542        spelling: &Path,
1543        flags: OFlag,
1544    ) -> Option<Result<OpenedDirectory, Errno>> {
1545        let opened = self
1546            .descriptor_directory_reached(guest, observed, spelling, flags)
1547            .await?;
1548        Some(match super::rendered_procfs::open_flags_refused(flags) {
1549            Some(errno) => Err(errno),
1550            None => opened,
1551        })
1552    }
1553
1554    /// [`Self::descriptor_directory_opened`], before the flags
1555    /// `build_open_flags` refuses.
1556    async fn descriptor_directory_reached<G: Guest<Self>>(
1557        &self,
1558        guest: &mut G,
1559        observed: &Path,
1560        spelling: &Path,
1561        flags: OFlag,
1562    ) -> Option<Result<OpenedDirectory, Errno>> {
1563        if !self.serves_descriptor_directories(guest) {
1564            return None;
1565        }
1566        let own = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest)?;
1567        // A spelling through a descriptor link to a placeholder directory
1568        // (`/proc/self/fd/D/.`, `/proc/self/fd/D/N`) names what Linux
1569        // resolves it to, with the links it followed on the way counted.
1570        let (decided, links) = match Self::decided_path(guest, observed) {
1571            Ok(decided) => decided,
1572            Err(errno) => return Some(Err(errno)),
1573        };
1574        let (observed, spelling) = if decided.as_path() == observed {
1575            (observed, spelling)
1576        } else {
1577            (decided.as_path(), decided.as_path())
1578        };
1579        if let Some(table) = fd_directory::classify(spelling, own) {
1580            return Some(Ok(OpenedDirectory::Own(table)));
1581        }
1582        if let Some(tasks) = names_procfs_root(spelling) {
1583            // A task the spelling names on the way that does not exist ends
1584            // Linux's walk there.
1585            return Some(if Self::named_tasks_exist(guest, &tasks).await {
1586                Ok(OpenedDirectory::Root)
1587            } else {
1588                Err(Errno::ENOENT)
1589            });
1590        }
1591        // `/proc/self` and `/proc/thread-self` are links: where Detcore
1592        // serves the directories they reach, an open that does not follow
1593        // the last one (by any spelling that ends at it, `..` resolved:
1594        // `/proc/self/../self`) is answered as Linux answers an open of a
1595        // symbolic link (v6.17 fs/namei.c): the flags `build_open_flags`
1596        // refuses (`EINVAL`), then the walk to the link (`ENOENT` at a task
1597        // named on the way that does not exist, `ELOOP` past the link limit),
1598        // then in `do_open` an existing name with `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` (`EEXIST`)
1599        // and `O_DIRECTORY` (`ENOTDIR`), then `may_open`'s `ELOOP` for a
1600        // link. With `O_PATH`, which skips all but the `O_DIRECTORY` check,
1601        // Linux opens the link itself, which is refused by name. Whether the
1602        // open follows the last component is told from the guest's own
1603        // spelling (a final `/` or `/.` follows it).
1604        if super::opens_the_link_itself(observed, flags)
1605            && let Some(link) = Self::own_selector_link(guest, spelling, links).await
1606        {
1607            let path_only = flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH);
1608            return Some(
1609                if let Some(errno) = super::rendered_procfs::open_flags_refused(flags) {
1610                    Err(errno)
1611                } else {
1612                    link.and_then(|link| {
1613                        if !path_only && flags.contains(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_EXCL) {
1614                            Err(Errno::EEXIST)
1615                        } else if flags.contains(OFlag::O_DIRECTORY) {
1616                            Err(Errno::ENOTDIR)
1617                        } else if !path_only {
1618                            Err(Errno::ELOOP)
1619                        } else {
1620                            Ok(OpenedDirectory::SelectorLink {
1621                                thread: link.ends_with("thread-self"),
1622                            })
1623                        }
1624                    })
1625                },
1626            );
1627        }
1628        // An `O_CREAT` open (not `O_PATH`, which drops `O_CREAT`) by a
1629        // spelling that ends in a name and a `/` fails with `EISDIR` once the
1630        // walk reaches that name, before the name is looked up
1631        // (`lookup_fast_for_open`): of a task directory, only the walk of the
1632        // components before it can fail first, whether or not the task the
1633        // name gives exists.
1634        let creates_through_slash = !flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH)
1635            && flags.contains(OFlag::O_CREAT)
1636            && ends_in_a_name_and_slash(observed);
1637        if creates_through_slash
1638            && let Some(answer) =
1639                Self::own_task_creation_through_slash(guest, spelling, links).await
1640        {
1641            return Some(Err(answer));
1642        }
1643        if let Some(directory) = Self::own_task_directory(guest, spelling, links).await {
1644            return Some(
1645                directory
1646                    .map(|(kind, process, thread)| OpenedDirectory::Task(kind, process, thread)),
1647            );
1648        }
1649        if super::opens_the_link_itself(observed, flags) {
1650            return None;
1651        }
1652        if let Some(directory) = Self::own_link_directory(guest, observed) {
1653            // The open follows this last link too, within the one budget,
1654            // unless it fails at the link's name first (`creates_through_slash`).
1655            if links + selector_links(observed) + usize::from(!creates_through_slash) > LINK_LIMIT {
1656                return Some(Err(Errno::ELOOP));
1657            }
1658            return Some(Ok(OpenedDirectory::Reopened(directory)));
1659        }
1660        let dev_fd = observed
1661            .components()
1662            .filter(|component| *component != std::path::Component::CurDir)
1663            .eq(Path::new("/dev/fd").components());
1664        if dev_fd
1665            && self
1666                .injected_link_target(guest, b"/dev/fd")
1667                .await
1668                .as_deref()
1669                == Some(Path::new("/proc/self/fd"))
1670        {
