1671            return Some(Ok(OpenedDirectory::Own(DescriptorDirectoryTable::Process)));
1672        }
1673        None
1674    }
1675
1676    /// The device a `/proc` directory Detcore serves reports, the one the
1677    /// guest's mount table shows at `/proc`: Detcore's own procfs device
1678    /// (`procfs_inode::VIRTUAL_PROC_DEVICE`) where it virtualizes metadata,
1679    /// and otherwise the guest's procfs device itself, which the mount table
1680    /// then shows as it is (`rendered_procfs::proc_device`).
1681    fn reported_procfs_device<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> u64 {
1682        if guest.config().virtualize_metadata {
1683            procfs_inode::VIRTUAL_PROC_DEVICE
1684        } else {
1685            super::rendered_procfs::proc_device().unwrap_or(procfs_inode::VIRTUAL_PROC_DEVICE)
1686        }
1687    }
1688
1689    /// The device that keys the inode numbers of the `/proc` directories
1690    /// Detcore serves, and their entries', with their paths: the device of
1691    /// the guest's procfs as the guest's own stat of it reports it, never the
1692    /// placeholder's, whose device the host chose. Where the backend's `/proc`
1693    /// beneath is the guest's kernel procfs
1694    /// (`procfs_inode::tool_can_name_guest_descriptors`: ptrace), the tool's
1695    /// own view of it (`rendered_procfs::proc_device`), which is the
1696    /// guest's, as the tool runs in the guest's mount namespace; elsewhere
1697    /// (KVM) the device that backend reports for its procfs files
1698    /// (`procfs_inode::KVM_SYNTHETIC_PROC_DEVICE`). Either way the device the
1699    /// descriptor on `/proc` itself reported before slice P1, so the numbers
1700    /// are the ones they were.
1701    pub(crate) fn procfs_key_device<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> u64 {
1702        if procfs_inode::tool_can_name_guest_descriptors(&guest.config().backend) {
1703            super::rendered_procfs::proc_device().unwrap_or(0)
1704        } else {
1705            procfs_inode::KVM_SYNTHETIC_PROC_DEVICE
1706        }
1707    }
1708
1709    /// The target of the symbolic link at the absolute guest path `link`, by
1710    /// one injected `readlinkat`, or `None`.
1711    pub(crate) async fn injected_link_target<G: Guest<Self>>(
1712        &self,
1713        guest: &mut G,
1714        link: &[u8],
1715    ) -> Option<PathBuf> {
1716        const LINK_CAPACITY: usize = 32;
1717        const TARGET_CAPACITY: usize = 64;
1718        let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
1719        let link = stack.push(super::rendered_procfs::c_string::<LINK_CAPACITY>(link));
1720        let target = stack.reserve::<[u8; TARGET_CAPACITY]>();
1721        let stack_guard = stack.commit().ok()?;
1722        let length = guest
1723            .inject(
1724                syscalls::Readlinkat::new()
1725                    .with_dirfd(libc::AT_FDCWD)
1726                    .with_path(PathPtr::from_ptr(link.as_raw() as *const libc::c_char))
1727                    .with_buf(Some(target.cast::<libc::c_char>()))
1728                    .with_buf_len(TARGET_CAPACITY),
1729            )
1730            .await;
1731        let bytes = match length.map(|length| length as usize) {
1732            Ok(length) if length < TARGET_CAPACITY => {
1733                let mut bytes = vec![0; length];
1734                guest
1735                    .memory()
1736                    .read_exact(target.cast::<u8>(), &mut bytes)
1737                    .ok()
1738                    .map(|()| bytes)
1739            }
1740            _ => None,
1741        };
1742        drop(stack_guard);
1743        Some(PathBuf::from(String::from_utf8(bytes?).ok()?))
1744    }
1745
1746    /// What Detcore's process table says now of the task `binding` names,
1747    /// and whether that is still the task the directory was bound to (it
1748    /// exists and no other task has taken its numbers since).
1749    async fn bound_task<G: Guest<Self>>(
1750        guest: &mut G,
1751        binding: &DescriptorDirectoryBinding,
1752    ) -> (crate::procfs::listing::ProcfsTaskAnswer, bool) {
1753        let thread = (binding.table == DescriptorDirectoryTable::Thread).then_some(binding.tid);
1754        let answer = crate::tool_global::procfs_task(
1755            guest,
1756            crate::procfs::BoundTask {
1757                process: binding.pid,
1758                thread,
1759            },
1760        )
1761        .await;
1762        let current = answer.exists
1763            && answer.process_incarnation == binding.process_incarnation
1764            && answer.thread_incarnation == binding.thread_incarnation;
1765        (answer, current)
1766    }
1767
1768    /// Whether the placeholder directory `directory` still names a directory:
1769    /// `/proc` always does; a descriptor directory or a task directory while
1770    /// its task does (`get_proc_task`, [`Self::bound_task`]).
1771    async fn placeholder_current<G: Guest<Self>>(
1772        guest: &mut G,
1773        directory: &PlaceholderDirectory,
1774    ) -> bool {
1775        match directory {
1776            PlaceholderDirectory::Root | PlaceholderDirectory::System(_) => true,
1777            PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(binding) => Self::bound_task(guest, binding).await.1,
1778            PlaceholderDirectory::Task(binding) => Self::bound_own_task(guest, binding).await.1,
1779        }
1780    }
1781
1782    /// The binding of the task directory `kind` of process `pid`, with `tid`
1783    /// the directory's thread for a thread's directory and the opener
1784    /// otherwise ([`fd_directory::classify_task_directory`]): the caller's
1785    /// own (package K slice K10) or another process's (slice 2b-1), with the
1786    /// incarnation
1787    /// of each of its numbers now, as Linux binds the directory's inode to
1788    /// the task at the lookup (`proc_pid_instantiate`, `proc_task_instantiate`).
1789    async fn task_directory_binding<G: Guest<Self>>(
1790        &self,
1791        guest: &mut G,
1792        kind: TaskDirectoryKind,
1793        (pid, tid): (i32, i32),
1794    ) -> TaskDirectoryBinding {
1795        let thread = (kind == TaskDirectoryKind::Thread).then_some(tid);
1796        let answer = crate::tool_global::procfs_task(
1797            guest,
1798            crate::procfs::BoundTask {
1799                process: pid,
1800                thread,
1801            },
1802        )
1803        .await;
1804        TaskDirectoryBinding {
1805            kind,
1806            pid,
1807            tid,
1808            process_incarnation: answer.process_incarnation,
1809            thread_incarnation: answer.thread_incarnation,
1810            device: Self::procfs_key_device(guest),
1811        }
1812    }
1813
1814    /// What Detcore's process table says now of the task the task directory
1815    /// `binding` names, and whether that is still the task it was bound to.
1816    async fn bound_own_task<G: Guest<Self>>(
1817        guest: &mut G,
1818        binding: &TaskDirectoryBinding,
1819    ) -> (crate::procfs::listing::ProcfsTaskAnswer, bool) {
1820        let thread = (binding.kind == TaskDirectoryKind::Thread).then_some(binding.tid);
1821        let answer = crate::tool_global::procfs_task(
1822            guest,
1823            crate::procfs::BoundTask {
1824                process: binding.pid,
1825                thread,
1826            },
1827        )
1828        .await;
1829        let current = answer.exists
1830            && answer.process_incarnation == binding.process_incarnation
1831            && answer.thread_incarnation == binding.thread_incarnation;
1832        (answer, current)
1833    }
1834
1835    /// Whether every task in `tasks` exists now, as Linux's walk finds it
1836    /// ([`NamedTask`]): any task by its number at the top of `/proc`, and a
1837    /// thread of that process below its `task` (`Scheduler::procfs_task`),
1838    /// the caller's own process for `self`.
1839    pub(super) async fn named_tasks_exist<G: Guest<Self>>(
1840        guest: &mut G,
1841        tasks: &[NamedTask],
1842    ) -> bool {
1843        for task in tasks {
1844            let process = match task.process {
1845                Some(process) => process,
1846                None => match guest.thread_state().detpid {
1847                    Some(pid) => pid.as_raw(),
1848                    None => return false,
1849                },
1850            };
1851            if process <= 0 || task.thread.is_some_and(|thread| thread <= 0) {
1852                return false;
1853            }
1854            let answer = crate::tool_global::procfs_task(
1855                guest,
1856                crate::procfs::BoundTask {
1857                    process,
1858                    thread: task.thread,
1859                },
1860            )
1861            .await;
1862            if !answer.exists {
1863                return false;
1864            }
1865        }
1866        true
1867    }
1868
1869    /// The identity `(dev, ino)` of the placeholder directory hermit-cli
1870    /// mounted, as the guest's backend reports it, learnt once in this tool
1871    /// process: from a placeholder Detcore opened on it (where it is the
1872    /// object, [`Self::open_placeholder`]), or else by one injected `stat` of
1873    /// its path, which no guest name spells (Detcore hides `/proc/fs`).
1874    /// `None` without a placeholder, or where that `stat` fails or does not
1875    /// find a directory.
1876    pub(crate) async fn hidden_placeholder_identity<G: Guest<Self>>(
1877        guest: &mut G,
1878    ) -> Option<(u64, u64)> {
1879        if let Some(identity) = *HIDDEN_IDENTITY.lock().unwrap() {
1880            return Some(identity);
1881        }
1882        let placeholder = guest.config().directory_placeholder.clone()?;
1883        let bytes = placeholder.as_os_str().as_bytes().to_vec();
1884        let stat = Self::injected_path_stat(guest, &bytes).await.ok()?;
1885        if stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT != libc::S_IFDIR {
1886            return None;
1887        }
1888        let identity = (stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino);
1889        *HIDDEN_IDENTITY.lock().unwrap() = Some(identity);
1890        Some(identity)
1891    }
1892
1893    /// `stat` of the absolute path `path`, following a final link, by one
1894    /// injected `newfstatat` from `AT_FDCWD`, the path and the buffer staged
1895    /// together on Reverie's guest stack.
1896    async fn injected_path_stat<G: Guest<Self>>(
1897        guest: &mut G,
1898        path: &[u8],
1899    ) -> Result<libc::stat, Errno> {
1900        const PATH_CAPACITY: usize = 64;
1901        if path.len() >= PATH_CAPACITY {
1902            return Err(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG);
1903        }
1904        let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
1905        let staged = stack.push(super::rendered_procfs::c_string::<PATH_CAPACITY>(path));
1906        let buffer = stack.reserve::<libc::stat>();
1907        let stack_guard = stack.commit().map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)?;
1908        let looked_up = guest
1909            .inject(crate::arch::fstatat_syscall(
1910                syscalls::Fstatat::new()
1911                    .with_dirfd(libc::AT_FDCWD)
1912                    .with_path(PathPtr::from_ptr(staged.as_raw() as *const libc::c_char))
1913                    .with_stat(Some(reverie::syscalls::StatPtr(buffer)))
1914                    .with_flags(nix::fcntl::AtFlags::empty()),
1915            ))
1916            .await;
1917        let stat = looked_up.and_then(|_| guest.memory().read_value(buffer));
1918        drop(stack_guard);
1919        stat
1920    }
1921
1922    /// Whether a guest call that named a path reached the placeholder
1923    /// directory, as the result `(mode, dev, ino)` the backend gave for it
1924    /// shows: a directory of the placeholder's identity
1925    /// ([`Self::hidden_placeholder_identity`]). Detcore makes every
1926    /// descriptor it opens on the placeholder itself, without the guest's
1927    /// call, so a guest open or stat that reaches it did so by a spelling
1928    /// Detcore's `--proc` level table could not resolve
1929    /// (`/proc/self/root/proc/fs/nfsd`, `/proc/1/root/...`,
1930    /// `/dev/fd/<a descriptor on />/proc/fs/nfsd`), and fails with `ENOENT`
1931    /// as `/proc/fs` does. Wherever a placeholder is mounted, on every
1932    /// backend.
1933    pub(crate) async fn hidden_placeholder_reached<G: Guest<Self>>(
1934        guest: &mut G,
1935        mode: u32,
1936        dev: u64,
1937        ino: u64,
1938    ) -> bool {
1939        if guest.config().directory_placeholder.is_none() || mode & libc::S_IFMT != libc::S_IFDIR {
1940            return false;
1941        }
1942        Self::hidden_placeholder_identity(guest).await == Some((dev, ino))
1943    }
1944
1945    /// [`Self::hidden_placeholder_reached`], where it is decided without
1946    /// learning the placeholder's identity: `false` without a placeholder or
1947    /// for what is no directory, the comparison once the identity is learnt,
1948    /// and `None` until it is, for the caller to ask
1949    /// [`Self::hidden_placeholder_reached`] in a boxed future, so that the
1950    /// stat and open handlers that ask stay small.
1951    pub(crate) fn hidden_placeholder_known<G: Guest<Self>>(
1952        guest: &G,
1953        mode: u32,
1954        dev: u64,
1955        ino: u64,
1956    ) -> Option<bool> {
1957        if guest.config().directory_placeholder.is_none() || mode & libc::S_IFMT != libc::S_IFDIR {
1958            return Some(false);
1959        }
1960        HIDDEN_IDENTITY
1961            .lock()
1962            .unwrap()
1963            .map(|identity| identity == (dev, ino))
1964    }
1965
1966    /// The bytes of a stat call's result buffer at `buffer`, `size` long,
1967    /// before the call writes it, where a placeholder directory exists (KVM:
1968    /// `DetConfig::directory_placeholder`), so that a call that reaches the
1969    /// directory can leave the buffer as Linux leaves it on its `ENOENT`,
1970    /// untouched ([`Self::hidden_placeholder_stat`]). `None` elsewhere, and
1971    /// for a buffer that cannot be read, which the call cannot write either.
1972    pub(crate) fn stat_buffer_before<G: Guest<Self>>(
1973        guest: &G,
1974        buffer: Option<usize>,
1975        size: usize,
1976    ) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
1977        guest.config().directory_placeholder.as_ref()?;
1978        let start = reverie::syscalls::Addr::<u8>::from_raw(buffer?)?;
1979        let mut bytes = vec![0u8; size];
1980        guest.memory().read_exact(start, &mut bytes).ok()?;
1981        Some(bytes)
1982    }
1983
1984    /// A stat call's answer where its result, which the backend wrote to the
1985    /// guest's buffer at `buffer`, is the placeholder directory's
1986    /// ([`Self::hidden_placeholder_reached`]): `ENOENT`, as for `/proc/fs`,
1987    /// with the buffer as it was before the call (`before`,
1988    /// [`Self::stat_buffer_before`]), as Linux's `ENOENT` leaves it, so that
1989    /// nothing of the tmpfs (its device, inode or times) reaches the guest.
1990    pub(crate) fn hidden_placeholder_stat<G: Guest<Self>>(
1991        guest: &mut G,
1992        buffer: usize,
1993        before: Option<Vec<u8>>,
1994    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
1995        if let (Some(start), Some(before)) = (AddrMut::<u8>::from_raw(buffer), before) {
1996            let _ = guest.memory().write_exact(start, &before);
1997        }
1998        Err(Errno::ENOENT.into())
1999    }
2000
2001    /// [`Self::hidden_placeholder_reached`], decided at once where
2002    /// [`Self::hidden_placeholder_known`] decides it, and otherwise in a boxed
2003    /// future.
2004    pub(crate) async fn reaches_hidden_placeholder<G: Guest<Self>>(
2005        guest: &mut G,
2006        mode: u32,
2007        dev: u64,
2008        ino: u64,
2009    ) -> bool {
2010        match Self::hidden_placeholder_known(guest, mode, dev, ino) {
2011            Some(known) => known,
2012            None => Box::pin(Self::hidden_placeholder_reached(guest, mode, dev, ino)).await,
2013        }
2014    }
2015
2016    /// Whether an open of the absolute guest path `observed`, whose procfs
2017    /// spelling is `spelling`, must be refused with `EACCES` before it reaches
2018    /// the backend: `/proc` itself, or a descriptor directory, opened where
2019    /// the guest's procfs is not the kernel's (KVM) and Detcore serves no new
2020    /// placeholder to the caller: no placeholder in the run (`--no-namespace`,
2021    /// a library caller, a user mount over the placeholder), or a caller in a
2022    /// nested PID namespace, whose numbers Detcore does not know: the
2023    /// owner-approved answer for these configurations. The backend's executor
2024    /// serves no procfs directory either (reverie-linuxemu refuses `/proc`
2025    /// with `EACCES` since package K slice P2); Detcore answers first, so the
2026    /// answer is Detcore's on every backend that needs it.
2027    pub(crate) async fn refuses_backend_procfs_directory<G: Guest<Self>>(
2028        guest: &mut G,
2029        observed: &Path,
2030        spelling: &Path,
2031    ) -> bool {
2032        let config = guest.config();
2033        if Self::placeholder_object(guest) != PlaceholderObject::Placeholder
2034            || config.backend.tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table
2035            || config.recordreplay_modes
2036            || Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest).is_some()
2037        {
2038            return false;
2039        }
2040        if spells_a_descriptor_directory(observed) || spells_a_descriptor_directory(spelling) {
2041            return true;
2042        }
2043        match names_procfs_root(spelling) {
2044            Some(tasks) => Self::named_tasks_exist(guest, &tasks).await,
2045            None => false,
2046        }
2047    }
2048
2049    /// Open the placeholder directory `opened` names, with the guest's open
2050    /// `flags`, by a spelling that ends in a name and a `/` where
2051    /// `name_and_slash` says so ([`ends_in_a_name_and_slash`]): refuse what
2052    /// Linux refuses for a directory, then inject the
2053    /// open of the object that stands for it ([`PlaceholderObject`]), record
2054    /// the descriptor and which directory it is, register it for its tag, and
2055    /// name its description to the lock ledger, which follows every table
2056    /// that references it from now on (`procfs::locks::LockLedger`, for its
2057    /// `flock` locks).
2058    pub(super) async fn open_placeholder<G: Guest<Self>>(
2059        &self,
2060        guest: &mut G,
2061        flags: OFlag,
2062        opened: OpenedDirectory,
2063        name_and_slash: bool,
2064    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
2065        // As Linux refuses an open of a directory: invalid combinations
2066        // first (`build_open_flags`), then `O_CREAT` by a spelling that ends
2067        // in a name and a `/` (`lookup_fast_for_open`'s EISDIR), an existing
2068        // name with `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` (EEXIST), an `O_TMPFILE` below an
2069        // immutable directory (EPERM) or one that has no `tmpfile`
2070        // (EOPNOTSUPP), `O_CREAT` (`do_open`'s EISDIR), a write
2071        // or `O_TRUNC` (`may_open`'s EISDIR), `O_NOATIME` for a caller that
2072        // neither owns the directory nor may act as its owner (the end of
2073        // `may_open`, EPERM), and last `O_DIRECT`, which procfs cannot do
2074        // (`do_dentry_open`'s EINVAL). An `O_PATH` open makes none of these
2075        // checks. Measured on Linux 7.1.
2076        if let Some(errno) = super::rendered_procfs::open_flags_refused(flags) {
2077            return Err(errno.into());
2078        }
2079        let path_only = flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH);
2080        let (pid, tid) = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest).ok_or(Errno::ENOENT)?;
2081        let directory = match opened {
2082            OpenedDirectory::Own(table) => {
2083                let thread = (table == DescriptorDirectoryTable::Thread).then_some(tid);
2084                let answer = crate::tool_global::procfs_task(
2085                    guest,
2086                    crate::procfs::BoundTask {
2087                        process: pid,
2088                        thread,
2089                    },
2090                )
2091                .await;
2092                PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(DescriptorDirectoryBinding {
2093                    table,
2094                    pid,
2095                    tid,
2096                    process_incarnation: answer.process_incarnation,
2097                    thread_incarnation: answer.thread_incarnation,
2098                    device: Self::procfs_key_device(guest),
2099                    files: (table == DescriptorDirectoryTable::Thread)
2100                        .then(|| guest.thread_state().files_id()),
2101                })
2102            }
2103            OpenedDirectory::Root => PlaceholderDirectory::Root,
2104            OpenedDirectory::System(directory) => PlaceholderDirectory::System(directory),
2105            OpenedDirectory::Reopened(directory) => directory,
2106            OpenedDirectory::Task(kind, process, thread) => PlaceholderDirectory::Task(
2107                self.task_directory_binding(guest, kind, (process, thread))
2108                    .await,
2109            ),
2110            OpenedDirectory::SelectorLink { thread } => {
2111                return self
2112                    .refuse_placeholder_call(
2113                        guest,
2114                        Sysno::openat,
2115                        Errno::EACCES,
2116                        &format!(
2117                            "an O_PATH open of /proc/{} that does not follow it, which opens \
2118                             the symbolic link itself on Linux, cannot be served: Detcore serves \
2119                             the directory it leads to on this backend, and no object for the \
2120                             link (https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/5039)",
2121                            if thread { "thread-self" } else { "self" }
2122                        ),
2123                    )
2124                    .await;
2125            }
2126        };
2127        if !path_only {
2128            if name_and_slash && flags.contains(OFlag::O_CREAT) {
2129                return Err(Errno::EISDIR.into());
2130            }
2131            if flags.contains(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_EXCL) {
2132                return Err(Errno::EEXIST.into());
2133            }
2134            if flags.contains(OFlag::O_TMPFILE) {
2135                // `vfs_tmpfile` checks write permission on the directory
2136                // before it asks for `tmpfile` (v6.17 fs/namei.c:3927-3931):
2137                // a process's or a thread's directory is immutable
2138                // (`proc_pid_instantiate`, `proc_task_instantiate`,
2139                // fs/proc/base.c:3498 and :3800), which no one may write
2140                // (EPERM); `/proc`, `task` and a descriptor directory have no
2141                // `tmpfile` (EOPNOTSUPP).
2142                let immutable = matches!(
2143                    directory,
2144                    PlaceholderDirectory::Task(TaskDirectoryBinding {
2145                        kind: TaskDirectoryKind::Process | TaskDirectoryKind::Thread,
2146                        ..
2147                    })
2148                );
2149                // A system-wide directory is read-only to every caller that
2150                // may not act as its owner, and a sysctl directory to every
2151                // caller (`test_perm` of its 0555 table entry): EACCES.
2152                let unwritable = match directory {
2153                    PlaceholderDirectory::System(system) => {
2154                        system.is_sysctl() || !placeholder_owner_or_capable(&directory)?
2155                    }
2156                    _ => false,
2157                };
2158                return Err(if immutable {
2159                    Errno::EPERM
2160                } else if unwritable {
2161                    Errno::EACCES
2162                } else {
2163                    Errno::EOPNOTSUPP
2164                }
2165                .into());
2166            }
2167            if flags.contains(OFlag::O_CREAT)
2168                || flags & OFlag::O_ACCMODE != OFlag::O_RDONLY
2169                || flags.contains(OFlag::O_TRUNC)
2170            {
2171                return Err(Errno::EISDIR.into());
2172            }
2173            if flags.contains(OFlag::O_NOATIME) && !placeholder_owner_or_capable(&directory)? {
2174                return Err(Errno::EPERM.into());
2175            }
2176            if flags.contains(OFlag::O_DIRECT) {
2177                return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
2178            }
2179        }
2180        let object = Self::placeholder_object(guest);
2181        let object_path = match object {
2182            PlaceholderObject::ProcfsRoot => PathBuf::from("/proc"),
2183            PlaceholderObject::Placeholder => guest
2184                .config()
2185                .directory_placeholder
2186                .clone()
2187                .ok_or(Errno::ENOENT)?,
2188        };
2189        // A path-only open is an `O_PATH` descriptor on the object as well,
2190        // so that every call Linux refuses on one is refused beneath too, as
2191        // on Linux, those Detcore does not answer itself included (`epoll_ctl`,
2192        // `poll`, the socket calls). Such a descriptor takes no tag, which
2193        // `lseek` refuses on it, and so is not sent over `SCM_RIGHTS`
2194        // ([`Self::placeholder_copies_for_send`]).
2195        let cloexec = flags & OFlag::O_CLOEXEC;
2196        let object_flags = if path_only {
2197            OFlag::O_PATH | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY | cloexec
2198        } else {
2199            OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY | cloexec
2200        };
2201        let fd = Self::inject_openat(
2202            guest,
2203            libc::AT_FDCWD,
2204            object_path.as_os_str().as_bytes(),
2205            object_flags,
2206        )
2207        .await?;
2208        // The status flags Linux keeps: an `O_PATH` open keeps only
2209        // `O_PATH_FLAGS` (`build_open_how`); any other gets `O_LARGEFILE`
2210        // (`force_o_largefile`) and loses the open-time-only flags
2211        // (`do_dentry_open`).
2212        let status = if path_only {
2213            flags & (OFlag::O_PATH | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY | OFlag::O_NOFOLLOW | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC)
2214        } else {
2215            // The kernel's `O_LARGEFILE`, which the C library's value (0 on
2216            // x86_64) does not name.
2217            (flags & !(OFlag::O_CREAT | OFlag::O_EXCL | OFlag::O_NOCTTY | OFlag::O_TRUNC))
2218                | OFlag::from_bits_retain(0o100000)
2219        };
2220        let stat = match self
2221            .add_fd_with_stat(guest, fd, status, FdType::Regular)
2222            .await
2223        {
2224            Ok(Some(stat)) => Ok(stat),
2225            Ok(None) => self.inject_fstat(guest, fd).await,
2226            Err(errno) => Err(errno),
2227        };
2228        let stat = match stat {
2229            Ok(stat) => stat,
2230            Err(errno) => {
2231                guest.thread_state().remove_fd(fd);
2232                let _ = guest.inject(syscalls::Close::new().with_fd(fd)).await;
2233                return Err(errno.into());
2234            }
2235        };
2236        PLACEHOLDER_IDENTITIES
2237            .lock()
2238            .unwrap()
2239            .insert((stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino));
2240        if object == PlaceholderObject::Placeholder {
2241            HIDDEN_IDENTITY
2242                .lock()
2243                .unwrap()
2244                .get_or_insert((stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino));
2245        }
2246        OPENED_ANY.store(true, Ordering::SeqCst);
2247        // Registered with the open file description it is, which a receiver
2248        // of the descriptor takes as its own ([`Self::recognize_received_placeholder`]).
2249        let open_file = guest
2250            .thread_state()
2251            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.open_file_id())?;
2252        let tag = if path_only {
2253            None
2254        } else {
2255            crate::tool_global::register_placeholder(guest, directory, open_file).await
2256        };
2257        // The kernel position tags the descriptor for whoever meets it without
2258        // its record. A backend that cannot seek it leaves it untagged.
2259        let tag = match tag {
2260            Some(tag) => guest
2261                .inject(
2262                    syscalls::Lseek::new()
2263                        .with_fd(fd)
2264                        .with_offset(tag as i64)
2265                        .with_whence(syscalls::Whence::SEEK_SET),
2266                )
2267                .await
2268                .ok()
2269                .filter(|position| *position as u64 == tag)
2270                .map(|_| tag),
2271            None => None,
2272        };
2273        let canonical = directory.canonical();
2274        guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
2275            detfd.set_path(canonical.clone());
2276            detfd.set_placeholder(PlaceholderState {
2277                directory,
2278                tag,
2279                position: 0,
2280                path_only,
2281            });
2282        })?;
2283        self.follow_placeholder_references(guest, fd).await;
2284        tracing::debug!(
2285            "[detcore] descriptor {fd}: {} served on {}",
2286            canonical.display(),
2287            object_path.display()
2288        );
2289        Ok(fd as i64)
2290    }
2291
2292    /// Name descriptor `fd`'s description, a placeholder's, to the lock
2293    /// ledger with the slots of the caller's table that name it, and mark it
2294    /// followed, so that every later close, replacement, fork and exec of
2295    /// the tables that reference it reaches the ledger
2296    /// (`procfs::locks::LockLedger`): a `flock` on the directory is then
2297    /// released with the description's last reference, as Linux's `__fput`
2298    /// releases it, also where the first lock comes after a fork.
2299    async fn follow_placeholder_references<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &mut G, fd: RawFd) {
2300        let Ok(open_file) = guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
2301            detfd.set_lock_tracked();
2302            detfd.open_file_id()
2303        }) else {
2304            return;
2305        };
2306        let slots = guest.thread_state().slots_naming(open_file);
2307        crate::tool_global::note_description_locks(guest, open_file, Ok(Vec::new()), slots).await;
2308    }
2309
2310    /// Make descriptor `fd`, which Detcore just recorded without knowing what
2311    /// it is (received over `SCM_RIGHTS`, `Detcore::record_received_descriptors`),
2312    /// a placeholder directory if it is one: its identity (`stat`) is the
2313    /// placeholder's, which no other object has, and its kernel position,
2314    /// read by one injected `lseek`, is a placeholder's tag, which says which
2315    /// directory it stands for. No guest descriptor of that identity is ever
2316    /// seeked by the guest ([`Self::seek_would_place_a_tag`]). Until a
2317    /// placeholder is opened in this tool process, whose global tool
2318    /// registers every tag, no received descriptor can be one, and nothing is
2319    /// injected to look.
2320    pub(crate) async fn recognize_received_placeholder<G: Guest<Self>>(
2321        &self,
2322        guest: &mut G,
2323        fd: RawFd,
2324        stat: &libc::stat,
2325        status_flags: i32,
2326    ) {
2327        if stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT != libc::S_IFDIR
2328            || !OPENED_ANY.load(Ordering::SeqCst)
2329            || !Self::serves_placeholders(guest)
2330        {
2331            return;
2332        }
2333        if !PLACEHOLDER_IDENTITIES
2334            .lock()
2335            .unwrap()
2336            .contains(&(stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino))
2337        {
2338            return;
2339        }
2340        let position = guest
2341            .inject(
2342                syscalls::Lseek::new()
2343                    .with_fd(fd)
2344                    .with_offset(0)
2345                    .with_whence(syscalls::Whence::SEEK_CUR),
2346            )
2347            .await;
2348        let Ok(position) = position.map(|position| position as u64) else {
2349            return;
2350        };
2351        if fd_directory::tag_index(position).is_none() {
2352            return;
2353        }
2354        let Some((directory, open_file)) = crate::tool_global::placeholder(guest, position).await
2355        else {
2356            return;
2357        };
2358        // The description is the sender's, as Linux shares it: the locks
2359        // Detcore follows by description are that one's, and this copy is no
2360        // longer in flight (`procfs::locks::LockLedger::placeholder_copies`).
2361        let _ = guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
2362            detfd.adopt_open_file_id(open_file);
2363            detfd.set_path(directory.canonical());
2364            detfd.set_placeholder(PlaceholderState {
2365                directory,
2366                tag: Some(position),
2367                position: 0,
2368                path_only: status_flags & libc::O_PATH != 0,
2369            })
2370        });
2371        let _ = crate::tool_global::placeholder_copies(
2372            guest,
2373            crate::procfs::locks::PlaceholderCopies::Received(open_file),
2374        )
2375        .await;
2376        self.follow_placeholder_references(guest, fd).await;
2377    }
2378
2379    /// Before a send whose messages pass the descriptors `sent` over
2380    /// `SCM_RIGHTS`: the open file descriptions of the placeholders among
2381    /// them, one per copy, counted in flight
2382    /// (`procfs::locks::LockLedger::placeholder_copies`), so that none takes
2383    /// a placeholder `flock` until its copy is received
2384    /// ([`Self::recognize_received_placeholder`]) or the send fails (the
2385    /// caller then uncounts them). Refused by name instead (`Err`, the
2386    /// refusal): a placeholder whose description holds a placeholder `flock`,
2387    /// which Linux keeps while the message is queued, a reference the ledger
2388    /// does not follow; an untagged placeholder (an `O_PATH` one), which its
2389    /// receiver could not tell for one; and any placeholder `sendmmsg` sends,
2390    /// of whose messages Detcore does not know which the batch sent.
2391    pub(crate) async fn placeholder_copies_for_send<G: Guest<Self>>(
2392        &self,
2393        guest: &mut G,
2394        sysno: Sysno,
2395        sent: &[RawFd],
2396    ) -> Result<Vec<crate::types::OpenFileId>, Result<i64, Error>> {
2397        if !OPENED_ANY.load(Ordering::SeqCst) || !Self::serves_placeholders(guest) {
2398            return Ok(Vec::new());
2399        }
2400        let mut copies = Vec::new();
2401        for fd in sent {
2402            let Some(state) = Self::placeholder_of(guest, *fd) else {
2403                continue;
2404            };
2405            let unsupported = if state.tag.is_none() {
2406                Some("carries no tag (an O_PATH descriptor) that its receiver could tell it by")
2407            } else if sysno == Sysno::sendmmsg {
2408                Some("is sent in a batch, of whose messages Detcore does not know which it sent")
2409            } else {
2410                None
2411            };
2412            if let Some(why) = unsupported {
2413                let reason = format!(
2414                    "descriptor {fd}, which stands for {}, a /proc directory Detcore serves, \
2415                     is passed over SCM_RIGHTS and {why}",
2416                    state.directory.canonical().display()
2417                );
2418                return Err(self
2419                    .refuse_placeholder_call(guest, sysno, Errno::EOPNOTSUPP, &reason)
2420                    .await);
2421            }
2422            match guest
2423                .thread_state()
2424                .with_detfd(*fd, |detfd| detfd.open_file_id())
2425            {
2426                Ok(open_file) => copies.push(open_file),
2427                Err(error) => return Err(Err(error.into())),
2428            }
2429        }
2430        if copies.is_empty() {
2431            return Ok(copies);
2432        }
2433        match crate::tool_global::placeholder_copies(
2434            guest,
2435            crate::procfs::locks::PlaceholderCopies::Sending(copies.clone()),
2436        )
2437        .await
2438        {
2439            Ok(()) => Ok(copies),
2440            Err(_) => {
2441                let reason = "a descriptor it passes over SCM_RIGHTS stands for a /proc directory \
2442                              Detcore serves and holds a flock on it, which Linux keeps while the \
2443                              message is queued, a reference Detcore does not follow";
2444                Err(self
2445                    .refuse_placeholder_call(guest, sysno, Errno::EOPNOTSUPP, reason)
2446                    .await)
2447            }
2448        }
2449    }
2450
2451    /// Whether a send's `SCM_RIGHTS` descriptors can matter to the `/proc`
2452    /// directories Detcore serves: it serves them, and this tool process has
2453    /// opened or recognized a placeholder. Asked before anything else of a
2454    /// send, from Detcore's own state alone.
2455    pub(crate) fn may_send_placeholders<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G) -> bool {
2456        OPENED_ANY.load(Ordering::SeqCst) && Self::serves_placeholders(guest)
2457    }
2458
2459    /// The placeholders among the descriptors `sent`, each with its open file
2460    /// description, in the order sent.
2461    fn placeholders_among<G: Guest<Self>>(
2462        guest: &G,
2463        sent: &[RawFd],
2464    ) -> Vec<(RawFd, crate::types::OpenFileId)> {
2465        sent.iter()
2466            .filter(|fd| Self::placeholder_of(guest, **fd).is_some())
2467            .filter_map(|fd| {
2468                guest
2469                    .thread_state()
2470                    .with_detfd(*fd, |detfd| (*fd, detfd.open_file_id()))
2471                    .ok()
2472            })
2473            .collect()
2474    }
2475
2476    /// Before a `sendmsg` or `sendmmsg` on `socket` whose messages' headers
2477    /// are at the guest addresses `headers`, read here for the descriptors
2478    /// they pass over `SCM_RIGHTS` (`syscalls::io::sent_descriptors`; `unread`
2479    /// below where a header or control buffer cannot be read in full, so that
2480    /// the list may be incomplete), where Detcore serves placeholders
2481    /// ([`Self::may_send_placeholders`]): the open file
2482    /// descriptions of the placeholders among them, counted in flight
2483    /// ([`Self::placeholder_copies_for_send`]), and what each attempt of the
2484    /// send checks again ([`SendInspection`]), or the refusal by name. Besides
2485    /// the refusals [`Self::placeholder_copies_for_send`] makes, a send is
2486    /// refused while this process holds a placeholder and its socket can
2487    /// pass descriptors (`AF_UNIX`, [`Self::socket_carries_descriptors`]),
2488    /// where Detcore cannot know which descriptors Linux reads:
2489    /// - its messages cannot be read in full (`unread`): a control buffer
2490    ///   longer than the 65536 bytes Detcore reads, which Linux takes up to
2491    ///   the socket's `net.core.optmem_max` (128 KiB in a new network
2492    ///   namespace, a host setting in the initial one), or memory Detcore
2493    ///   cannot read. Detcore refuses rather than reading as much as the
2494    ///   kernel takes: that limit is the host's to raise, and no length helps
2495    ///   with memory Detcore cannot read;
2496    /// - the send runs outside this thread's turn in a run that sequentializes
2497    ///   threads (`outside_turn`, a blocking call on a descriptor that blocks
2498    ///   in the kernel, `Detcore::nonblockable_call_runs_outside_turn`), where
2499    ///   another thread may rewrite its messages, or what their descriptor
2500    ///   numbers name, before Linux reads them, whatever this inspection read.
2501    ///   Without sequentialized threads no thread has a turn, and every check
2502    ///   Detcore makes may race the guest's other threads (that mode's named
2503    ///   residual).
2504    pub(crate) async fn inspect_placeholder_send<G: Guest<Self>>(
2505        &self,
2506        guest: &mut G,
2507        sysno: Sysno,
2508        socket: RawFd,
2509        headers: &[usize],
2510        outside_turn: bool,
2511    ) -> Result<(Vec<crate::types::OpenFileId>, SendInspection), Result<i64, Error>> {
2512        let (sent, unread) = crate::syscalls::io::sent_descriptors(guest, headers);
2513        if (unread || outside_turn)
2514            && guest.thread_state().holds_placeholder()
2515            && Self::socket_carries_descriptors(guest, socket).await
2516        {
2517            let reason = if unread {
2518                format!(
2519                    "a message whose SCM_RIGHTS control data Detcore cannot read in full (more \
2520                     than {MAX_SEND_CONTROL_BYTES} bytes of it, which Linux takes up to the \
2521                     socket's net.core.optmem_max, or memory Detcore cannot read) may pass a \
2522                     descriptor that stands for a /proc directory Detcore serves, whose copies \
2523                     and lock Detcore follows"
2524                )
2525            } else {
2526                "a send on a socket that passes descriptors runs outside Detcore's turn, where \
2527                 another thread may change its SCM_RIGHTS messages, or what their descriptor \
2528                 numbers name, before Linux reads them, while this process holds a descriptor \
2529                 that stands for a /proc directory Detcore serves, whose copies and lock Detcore \
2530                 follows"
2531                    .to_string()
2532            };
2533            return Err(self
2534                .refuse_placeholder_call(guest, sysno, Errno::EOPNOTSUPP, &reason)
2535                .await);
2536        }
2537        let copies = self
2538            .placeholder_copies_for_send(guest, sysno, &sent)
2539            .await?;
2540        let inspection = SendInspection {
2541            sysno,
2542            socket,
2543            headers: headers.to_vec(),
2544            placeholders: Self::placeholders_among(guest, &sent),
2545        };
2546        Ok((copies, inspection))
2547    }
2548
2549    /// Why the next attempt of a send Detcore inspected (`inspection`,
2550    /// [`Self::inspect_placeholder_send`]) must not be made, read right
2551    /// before it: its messages now pass other placeholders over `SCM_RIGHTS`
2552    /// than they did when Detcore counted their copies (another thread
2553    /// replaced what a descriptor number names while an earlier attempt
2554    /// waited for room; a rewrite of the messages themselves is undone for
2555    /// each attempt, as Linux copied them when the call began, `EntryImport`),
2556    /// or they can no longer be read in full
2557    /// while this process holds a placeholder and the socket passes
2558    /// descriptors. `None` where the attempt passes the placeholders
2559    /// inspected, and nothing Detcore cannot see.
2560    pub(crate) async fn changed_placeholder_send<G: Guest<Self>>(
2561        guest: &mut G,
2562        inspection: &SendInspection,
2563    ) -> Option<String> {
2564        let (sent, unread) = crate::syscalls::io::sent_descriptors(guest, &inspection.headers);
2565        let placeholders = Self::placeholders_among(guest, &sent);
2566        if placeholders != inspection.placeholders {
2567            return Some(format!(
2568                "the descriptors its SCM_RIGHTS messages pass changed while the send waited for \
2569                 room, and with them the descriptors among them that stand for /proc \
2570                 directories Detcore serves (descriptors {:?} where Detcore inspected {:?}), \
2571                 whose copies and locks Detcore follows",
2572                placeholders.iter().map(|(fd, _)| *fd).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
2573                inspection
2574                    .placeholders
2575                    .iter()
2576                    .map(|(fd, _)| *fd)
2577                    .collect::<Vec<_>>(),
2578            ));
2579        }
2580        (unread
2581            && guest.thread_state().holds_placeholder()
2582            && Self::socket_carries_descriptors(guest, inspection.socket).await)
2583            .then(|| {
2584                "its SCM_RIGHTS messages could no longer be read in full when the send tried \
2585                 again, while this process holds a descriptor that stands for a /proc \
2586                 directory Detcore serves, whose copies and lock Detcore follows"
2587                    .to_string()
2588            })
2589    }
2590
2591    /// After a send on `socket` that ran outside this thread's turn
2592    /// (`Detcore::nonblockable_call_runs_outside_turn`): the refusal by name
2593    /// where this process now holds a placeholder and the socket passes
2594    /// descriptors. [`Self::inspect_placeholder_send`] refused the send if it
2595    /// held one before, so another thread opened or received this one while
2596    /// the send ran, and may have put it in the messages before Linux read
2597    /// them. `None` otherwise.
2598    pub(crate) async fn placeholder_send_after<G: Guest<Self>>(
2599        &self,
2600        guest: &mut G,
2601        sysno: Sysno,
2602        socket: RawFd,
2603    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
2604        if !Self::may_send_placeholders(guest)
2605            || !guest.thread_state().holds_placeholder()
2606            || !Self::socket_carries_descriptors(guest, socket).await
2607        {
2608            return None;
2609        }
2610        let reason = "a descriptor that stands for a /proc directory Detcore serves joined this \
2611                      process's table while a send on a socket that passes descriptors ran \
2612                      outside Detcore's turn, where another thread may have put it in the send's \
2613                      SCM_RIGHTS messages before Linux read them";
2614        Some(
2615            self.refuse_placeholder_call(guest, sysno, Errno::EOPNOTSUPP, reason)
2616                .await,
2617        )
2618    }
2619
2620    /// The placeholder directory descriptor `fd` stands for, where Detcore
2621    /// keeps no record of `fd` (one received over `SCM_RIGHTS` without
2622    /// sequentialized threads, which `Detcore::record_received_descriptors`
2623    /// leaves unrecorded): told as [`Self::recognize_received_placeholder`]
2624    /// tells it, by its identity, which only the placeholder has (one injected
2625    /// `fstat`), and its tag (one injected `lseek`). Without sequentialized
2626    /// threads another thread may replace the descriptor between the two, as
2627    /// it may between this and the guest's own call; the tag is checked
2628    /// against the registered placeholders, so a descriptor taken for one is
2629    /// a placeholder there. `None` for a recorded descriptor, any other
2630    /// object, and where Detcore serves no placeholder.
2631    pub(crate) async fn unrecorded_placeholder<G: Guest<Self>>(
2632        guest: &mut G,
2633        fd: RawFd,
2634    ) -> Option<PlaceholderDirectory> {
2635        if fd < 0
2636            || !OPENED_ANY.load(Ordering::SeqCst)
2637            || !Self::serves_placeholders(guest)
2638            || guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |_| ()).is_ok()
2639        {
2640            return None;
2641        }
2642        let fstat = move |statptr: reverie::syscalls::StatPtr<'_>| {
2643            Syscall::Fstat(syscalls::Fstat::new().with_fd(fd).with_stat(Some(statptr)))
2644        };
2645        let stat = Self::stat_with_fallback(guest, fd, fstat, None)
2646            .await
2647            .ok()?;
2648        if stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT != libc::S_IFDIR
2649            || !PLACEHOLDER_IDENTITIES
2650                .lock()
2651                .unwrap()
2652                .contains(&(stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino))
2653        {
2654            return None;
2655        }
2656        let position = guest
2657            .inject(
2658                syscalls::Lseek::new()
2659                    .with_fd(fd)
2660                    .with_offset(0)
2661                    .with_whence(syscalls::Whence::SEEK_CUR),
2662            )
2663            .await
2664            .ok()? as u64;
2665        fd_directory::tag_index(position)?;
2666        crate::tool_global::placeholder(guest, position)
2667            .await
2668            .map(|(directory, _)| directory)
2669    }
2670
2671    /// One injected `openat(dirfd, path, flags)` of a directory, with the path
2672    /// staged on Reverie's guest stack.
2673    async fn inject_openat<G: Guest<Self>>(
2674        guest: &mut G,
2675        dirfd: RawFd,
2676        path: &[u8],
2677        flags: OFlag,
2678    ) -> Result<RawFd, Errno> {
2679        const PATH_CAPACITY: usize = 64;
2680        if path.len() >= PATH_CAPACITY {
2681            return Err(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG);
2682        }
2683        let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
2684        let staged = stack.push(super::rendered_procfs::c_string::<PATH_CAPACITY>(path));
2685        let stack_guard = stack.commit().map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)?;
2686        let opened = guest
2687            .inject(
2688                syscalls::Openat::new()
2689                    .with_dirfd(dirfd)
2690                    .with_path(PathPtr::from_ptr(staged.as_raw() as *const libc::c_char))
2691                    .with_flags(flags)
2692                    .with_mode(None),
2693            )
2694            .await;
2695        drop(stack_guard);
2696        opened.map(|fd| fd as RawFd)
2697    }
2698
2699    /// The absolute spelling Linux looks a name up below, for a placeholder
2700    /// descriptor `fd` Detcore keeps no record of
2701    /// ([`Self::unrecorded_placeholder`]): the directory's lookup base
2702    /// (`/proc`, or the descriptor directory's spelling for the caller).
2703    pub(super) async fn unrecorded_placeholder_base<G: Guest<Self>>(
2704        guest: &mut G,
2705        fd: RawFd,
2706    ) -> Option<PathBuf> {
2707        let directory = Self::unrecorded_placeholder(guest, fd).await?;
2708        let caller = Self::caller_numbers(guest)?;
2709        Some(directory.lookup_base(caller))
2710    }
2711
2712    /// Whether the guest's `lseek(fd, offset, whence)` of a descriptor that
2713    /// Detcore does not answer itself would move the kernel position of a
2714    /// descriptor on a placeholder's object into the tags' range, where it
2715    /// would pass for a placeholder ([`Self::recognize_received_placeholder`]):
2716    /// a descriptor on procfs's root, where that is the object (ptrace), or
2717    /// on the placeholder directory (KVM). `handle_lseek` refuses that seek
2718    /// with `EINVAL`; Linux accepts any position up to 2^31 - 1 on procfs's
2719    /// root, and lists nothing from one this high (named difference).
2720    ///
2721    /// Decided whether or not a placeholder has been opened yet, whether or
2722    /// not Detcore recorded the descriptor's metadata
2723    /// (`--no-virtualize-metadata`), and whether or not the guest's table has
2724    /// a free slot, for nothing is opened to decide: the target position
2725    /// first, from the call alone (a negative or zero `SEEK_CUR` step cannot
2726    /// reach the range from a position below it, which every position of
2727    /// procfs's root is, this guard included), then the descriptor itself
2728    /// ([`Self::on_placeholder_object`]). A descriptor whose kind or
2729    /// filesystem cannot be told is refused (fail closed). Not where Detcore
2730    /// serves no placeholder ([`Self::serves_placeholders`]), where no
2731    /// placeholder is served or recognized.
2732    pub(crate) async fn seek_would_place_a_tag<G: Guest<Self>>(
2733        &self,
2734        guest: &mut G,
2735        fd: RawFd,
2736        offset: i64,
2737        whence: i32,
2738    ) -> bool {
2739        if !Self::serves_placeholders(guest) {
2740            return false;
2741        }
2742        let in_range = |target: i64| {
2743            u64::try_from(target)
2744                .ok()
2745                .and_then(fd_directory::tag_index)
2746                .is_some()
2747        };
2748        match whence {
2749            libc::SEEK_SET | libc::SEEK_END if !in_range(offset) => return false,
2750            libc::SEEK_SET | libc::SEEK_END => {}
2751            libc::SEEK_CUR if offset <= 0 => return false,
2752            libc::SEEK_CUR => {}
2753            _ => return false,
2754        }
2755        let recorded = guest
2756            .thread_state()
2757            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.stat())
2758            .ok()
2759            .flatten()
2760            .map(|stat| (stat.mode, stat.dev, stat.inode));
2761        let identity = match recorded {
2762            Some(identity) => identity,
2763            None => match self.inject_fstat(guest, fd).await {
2764                Ok(stat) => (stat.st_mode, stat.st_dev, stat.st_ino),
2765                // Not a descriptor: the kernel's own EBADF.
2766                Err(Errno::EBADF) => return false,
2767                // A descriptor that cannot be told is refused (fail closed).
2768                Err(_) => return true,
2769            },
2770        };
2771        if identity.0 & libc::S_IFMT != libc::S_IFDIR {
2772            return false;
2773        }
2774        let filesystem = match Self::placeholder_object(guest) {
2775            PlaceholderObject::ProcfsRoot => Some(Self::inject_fstatfs_type(guest, fd).await),
2776            PlaceholderObject::Placeholder => None,
2777        };
2778        if !Self::on_placeholder_object(
2779            (identity.1, identity.2),
2780            filesystem,
2781            &PLACEHOLDER_IDENTITIES.lock().unwrap(),
2782        ) {
2783            return false;
2784        }
2785        if whence != libc::SEEK_CUR {
2786            return true;
2787        }
2788        let current = guest
2789            .inject(
2790                syscalls::Lseek::new()
2791                    .with_fd(fd)
2792                    .with_offset(0)
2793                    .with_whence(syscalls::Whence::SEEK_CUR),
2794            )
2795            .await;
2796        match current {
2797            Ok(current) => in_range(current.wrapping_add(offset)),
2798            Err(_) => false,
2799        }
2800    }
2801
2802    /// Whether a directory descriptor of identity `identity` (`(dev, ino)`,
2803    /// as the kernel beneath the backend reports it) may be on a
2804    /// placeholder's object: its identity is one this tool process's
2805    /// placeholders' objects have (`objects`), or, where the object is
2806    /// procfs's root (`filesystem` given: its `fstatfs` `f_type`, or why that
2807    /// failed), it is procfs (`PROC_SUPER_MAGIC`) at the root inode
2808    /// (`PROC_ROOT_INO`, 1), or its filesystem cannot be told (fail closed).
2809    fn on_placeholder_object(
2810        identity: (u64, u64),
2811        filesystem: Option<Result<i64, Errno>>,
2812        objects: &BTreeSet<(u64, u64)>,
2813    ) -> bool {
2814        objects.contains(&identity)
2815            || match filesystem {
2816                None => false,
2817                Some(Ok(f_type)) => {
2818                    f_type as u64 == reverie::syscalls::linux_abi::PROC_SUPER_MAGIC as u64
2819                        && identity.1 == procfs_inode::PROC_ROOT_INO
2820                }
2821                Some(Err(_)) => true,
2822            }
2823    }
2824
2825    /// The filesystem type (`f_type`) of descriptor `fd`, by one injected
2826    /// `fstatfs` whose buffer is in Reverie's guest scratch or, where that
2827    /// cannot hold it, in a transient private page unmapped before the guest
2828    /// resumes (as `Detcore::inject_fstat`,
2829    /// <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3328>). No descriptor is
2830    /// opened.
2831    // GNU declares this kernel word as i64, musl as u64; retain its bits in
2832    // the common signed result, including filesystem magic values with bit 63 set.
2833    #[allow(clippy::unnecessary_cast)]
2834    async fn inject_fstatfs_type<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &mut G, fd: RawFd) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
2835        let fstatfs = |buf: AddrMut<'_, libc::statfs>| {
2836            syscalls::Fstatfs::new().with_fd(fd).with_buf(Some(buf))
2837        };
2838        let on_stack = {
2839            let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
2840            let buf = stack.reserve::<libc::statfs>();
2841            match stack.commit() {
2842                Ok(guard) => {
2843                    let result = match guest.inject_with_retry(fstatfs(buf)).await {
2844                        Ok(_) => guest
2845                            .memory()
2846                            .read_value(buf)
2847                            .map(|statfs| statfs.f_type as i64),
2848                        Err(errno) => Err(errno),
2849                    };
2850                    drop(guard);
2851                    Some(result)
2852                }
2853                Err(_) => None,
2854            }
2855        };
2856        match on_stack {
2857            Some(Err(Errno::EFAULT)) | None => {}
2858            Some(result) => return result,
2859        }
2860        let length = std::mem::size_of::<libc::statfs>();
2861        let page = super::procfs_levels::map_transient(guest, length)
2862            .await
2863            .ok_or(Errno::ENOMEM)?;
2864        let buf = page.cast::<libc::statfs>();
2865        let result = match guest.inject_with_retry(fstatfs(buf)).await {
2866            Ok(_) => guest
2867                .memory()
2868                .read_value(buf)
2869                .map(|statfs| statfs.f_type as i64),
2870            Err(errno) => Err(errno),
2871        };
2872        super::procfs_levels::unmap_transient(guest, page, length).await;
2873        result
2874    }
2875
2876    /// The descriptors of the table a descriptor directory bound to `binding`
2877    /// lists now, ascending, with the table's capacity, read by the caller:
2878    /// `Ok(None)` for a process whose thread group leader has exited (Linux
2879    /// has released its `files`), `ENOENT` for a task that is gone
2880    /// (`get_proc_task` finds none). The caller's own table (the process's
2881    /// for its process, or a thread's for a thread whose table it holds) comes
2882    /// from Detcore's model unless a receive may have installed descriptors
2883    /// the model cannot name; that, and another process's or thread's table,
2884    /// is listed by the kernel beneath the backend, through `object`, a
2885    /// placeholder descriptor on procfs's root where there is one
2886    /// ([`Self::kernel_listing_base`]).
2887    async fn descriptor_directory_table<G: Guest<Self>>(
2888        &self,
2889        guest: &mut G,
2890        binding: &DescriptorDirectoryBinding,
2891        object: Option<RawFd>,
2892    ) -> Result<Option<(Vec<i32>, u64)>, Errno> {
2893        let caller = Self::caller_numbers(guest).ok_or(Errno::EACCES)?;
2894        let (answer, current) = Self::bound_task(guest, binding).await;
2895        if !current {
2896            return Err(Errno::ENOENT);
2897        }
2898        if binding.table == DescriptorDirectoryTable::Process && answer.without_mm {
2899            return Ok(None);
2900        }
2901        let own = {
2902            let state = guest.thread_state();
2903            match binding.table {
2904                DescriptorDirectoryTable::Process => {
2905                    caller.0 == binding.pid
2906                        && (state.dettid.as_raw() == caller.0 || state.holds_process_files())
2907                }
2908                DescriptorDirectoryTable::Thread => {
2909                    caller.1 == binding.tid || binding.files == Some(state.files_id())
2910                }
2911            }
2912        };
2913        let base = self.kernel_listing_base(guest, object);
2914        if own {
2915            let (numbers, capacity, unaccounted) = guest.thread_state().descriptor_table();
2916            if !unaccounted {
2917                return Ok(Some((numbers, capacity)));
2918            }
2919            let path: &[u8] = match binding.table {
2920                DescriptorDirectoryTable::Process => b"self/fd",
2921                DescriptorDirectoryTable::Thread => b"thread-self/fd",
2922            };
2923            let listed = self
2924                .kernel_descriptor_numbers(guest, base, path, true)
2925                .await?;
2926            let capacity = capacity.max(fd_directory::capacity(&listed));
2927            return Ok(Some((listed, capacity)));
2928        }
2929        // Another table: the descriptor opened to read it is in the caller's,
2930        // and a descriptor of the same number in the one listed stays listed
2931        // (a fork's child listing the directory it inherited). (A process
2932        // that shares its table with another process, `CLONE_FILES` without
2933        // `CLONE_THREAD`, and lists that process's directory sees that
2934        // descriptor too: named.)
2935        let within = binding.within().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
2936        let listed = self
2937            .kernel_descriptor_numbers(guest, base, &within[1..], false)
2938            .await?;
2939        let capacity = fd_directory::capacity(&listed);
2940        Ok(Some((listed, capacity)))
2941    }
2942
2943    /// Where the kernel beneath the backend looks up a path within procfs
2944    /// for Detcore (a table it lists, a `/proc` entry it stats): from
2945    /// `object`, a placeholder descriptor on procfs's root, where the
2946    /// placeholder's object is that root (ptrace), so that the lookup starts
2947    /// at the guest's procfs whatever the caller's root or PID namespace;
2948    /// else from `/proc`, by its absolute spelling (`AT_FDCWD`).
2949    fn kernel_listing_base<G: Guest<Self>>(&self, guest: &G, object: Option<RawFd>) -> RawFd {
2950        match (Self::placeholder_object(guest), object) {
2951            (PlaceholderObject::ProcfsRoot, Some(object)) => object,
2952            _ => libc::AT_FDCWD,
2953        }
2954    }
2955
2956    /// A descriptor table as the kernel beneath the backend lists the
2957    /// descriptor directory at `path` within procfs (`self/fd`,
2958    /// `<pid>/task/<tid>/fd`), looked up from `base` (a descriptor on procfs's
2959    /// root, or `AT_FDCWD`, below `/proc`): one injected open, `getdents64`
2960    /// until the end into a buffer on Reverie's guest stack, and a close,
2961    /// leaving out the descriptor this opens to read it where the table
2962    /// listed is the caller's own (`own`), which holds it.
2963    async fn kernel_descriptor_numbers<G: Guest<Self>>(
2964        &self,
2965        guest: &mut G,
2966        base: RawFd,
2967        path: &[u8],
2968        own: bool,
2969    ) -> Result<Vec<i32>, Errno> {
2970        const PATH_CAPACITY: usize = 64;
2971        const BUFFER: usize = 512;
2972        let mut spelled = Vec::with_capacity(path.len() + 6);
2973        if base == libc::AT_FDCWD {
2974            spelled.extend_from_slice(b"/proc/");
2975        }
2976        spelled.extend_from_slice(path);
2977        if spelled.len() >= PATH_CAPACITY {
2978            return Err(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG);
2979        }
2980        let mut stack = guest.stack().await;
2981        let staged = stack.push(super::rendered_procfs::c_string::<PATH_CAPACITY>(&spelled));
2982        let buffer = stack.reserve::<[u8; BUFFER]>();
2983        let stack_guard = stack.commit()?;
2984        let opened = guest
2985            .inject(
2986                syscalls::Openat::new()
2987                    .with_dirfd(base)
2988                    .with_path(PathPtr::from_ptr(staged.as_raw() as *const libc::c_char))
2989                    .with_flags(OFlag::O_RDONLY | OFlag::O_DIRECTORY | OFlag::O_CLOEXEC)
2990                    .with_mode(None),
2991            )
2992            .await;
2993        let mut numbers = Vec::new();
2994        let mut result = opened.map(|fd| fd as RawFd);
2995        if let Ok(fd) = result {
2996            loop {
2997                let read = guest
2998                    .inject(
2999                        syscalls::Getdents64::new()
3000                            .with_fd(fd as u32)
3001                            .with_dirent(Some(buffer.cast()))
3002                            .with_count(BUFFER as u32),
3003                    )
3004                    .await;
3005                let length = match read {
3006                    Ok(0) => break,
3007                    Ok(length) => length as usize,
3008                    Err(errno) => {
3009                        result = Err(errno);
3010                        break;
3011                    }
3012                };
3013                let mut bytes = vec![0; length];
3014                if let Err(errno) = guest.memory().read_exact(buffer.cast::<u8>(), &mut bytes) {
3015                    result = Err(errno);
3016                    break;
3017                }
3018                numbers.extend(crate::util::descriptor_names(&bytes));
3019            }
3020            let _ = guest.inject(syscalls::Close::new().with_fd(fd)).await;
3021        }
3022        drop(stack_guard);
3023        let fd = result?;
3024        if own {
3025            numbers.retain(|number| *number != fd);
3026        }
3027        numbers.sort_unstable();
3028        Ok(numbers)
3029    }
3030
3031    /// The inode number of the directory a binding names itself (its `.`,
3032    /// and its `fstat` and `stat`), from Detcore's pool by the directory's
3033    /// path on the device of the guest's `/proc`: the key the entry `fd` of
3034    /// the process's directory is listed with (`modeled_directory_snapshot`),
3035    /// so the two agree.
3036    async fn directory_inode<G: Guest<Self>>(
3037        &self,
3038        guest: &mut G,
3039        device: u64,
3040        within: &Path,
3041        mark: crate::tool_global::SnapshotMark,
3042    ) -> u64 {
3043        let inodes = DirectoryInodes::new(device, Some(ProcfsDirectory::new(within.to_path_buf())));
3044        let (host, name) = inodes.entry_inode(b".", procfs_inode::path_inode(device, within).ino);
3045        crate::tool_global::determinize_listed_inode(guest, host, name, mark)
3046            .await
3047            .0
3048            .as_raw()
3049    }
3050
3051    /// Answer a `getdents` or `getdents64` of the descriptor directory open as
3052    /// `fd`, whose state is `state`, into the guest's buffer `buf` of
3053    /// `capacity` bytes, as `proc_readfd_common` answers it: the entries from
3054    /// the position on that fit, of the table as it is now, each with the
3055    /// inode number Detcore's pool gives its path, and the position moved
3056    /// past the entries copied (see [`fd_directory`]).
3057    async fn serve_descriptor_directory<G: Guest<Self>>(
3058        &self,
3059        guest: &mut G,
3060        fd: RawFd,
3061        state: PlaceholderState,
3062        buf: Option<AddrMut<'_, u8>>,
3063        capacity: usize,
3064        format: DirentFormat,
3065    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
3066        if state.path_only {
3067            return Err(Errno::EBADF.into());
3068        }
3069        let PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(binding) = state.directory else {
3070            return Err(Errno::EBADF.into());
3071        };
3072        let table = self
3073            .descriptor_directory_table(guest, &binding, Some(fd))
3074            .await?;
3075        let (entries, end) = fd_directory::entries_from(
3076            state.position,
3077            table
3078                .as_ref()
3079                .map(|(numbers, capacity)| (numbers.as_slice(), *capacity)),
3080        );
3081        let save = |guest: &mut G, position: u64| {
3082            let _ = guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
3083                if let Some(current) = detfd.placeholder() {
3084                    detfd.set_placeholder(PlaceholderState {
3085                        position,
3086                        ..current
3087                    });
3088                }
3089            });
3090        };
3091        if entries.is_empty() {
3092            save(guest, end);
3093            return Ok(0);
3094        }
3095        let count = fd_directory::fitting(&entries, capacity, |name| format.record_len(name));
3096        let Some(buf) = buf.filter(|_| count > 0) else {
3097            save(guest, entries[0].position);
3098            return Err(if count == 0 {
3099                Errno::EINVAL
3100            } else {
3101                Errno::EFAULT
3102            }
3103            .into());
3104        };
3105        let offsets = fd_directory::offsets(&entries, count, end);
3106        let mark = crate::tool_global::directory_snapshot_mark(guest).await;
3107        let within = binding.within();
3108        let inodes =
3109            DirectoryInodes::new(binding.device, Some(ProcfsDirectory::new(within.clone())));
3110        let mut records = Vec::new();
3111        let mut names = Vec::with_capacity(count);
3112        for (entry, offset) in entries.iter().zip(&offsets) {
3113            let ino = if entry.name == b"." {
3114                self.directory_inode(guest, binding.device, &within, mark)
3115                    .await
3116            } else {
3117                let entry_path = match entry.name.as_slice() {
3118                    b".." => within.parent().unwrap_or(&within).to_path_buf(),
3119                    name => within.join(std::ffi::OsStr::from_bytes(name)),
3120                };
3121                let key = procfs_inode::path_inode(binding.device, &entry_path).ino;
3122                let (host, name) = inodes.entry_inode(&entry.name, key);
3123                crate::tool_global::determinize_listed_inode(guest, host, name, mark)
3124                    .await
3125                    .0
3126                    .as_raw()
3127            };
3128            let record = DirEntry {
3129                name: entry.name.clone(),
3130                ino: 0,
3131                off: *offset,
3132                ty: entry.ty,
3133            };
3134            format.encode(&record, ino, *offset, &mut records);
3135            names.push(entry.name.len());
3136        }
3137        let (passed, copied) = super::copy_records(
3138            &mut guest.memory(),
3139            buf,
3140            format,
3141            &records,
3142            &names,
3143            entries[0].position,
3144        );
3145        save(guest, fd_directory::position_after(&entries, passed, end));
3146        Ok(copied.map(|length| length as i64)?)
3147    }
3148
3149    /// The entries of `/proc` as Detcore's procfs model lists them
3150    /// (`procfs::listing`, for the run's `--proc` level and the processes the
3151    /// scheduler lists), and how to key their inode numbers, for a `/proc`
3152    /// placeholder's directory stream: what `modeled_directory_snapshot`
3153    /// gives a descriptor on the kernel's `/proc`, but with every entry
3154    /// looked up by its absolute spelling (the placeholder holds none), no
3155    /// kernel cookies (the stream's positions are the guest's), and keyed by
3156    /// the procfs key device ([`Self::procfs_key_device`]). On the kernel's
3157    /// procfs an entry the lookup does not find is left out unless Detcore
3158    /// renders it, as there; on a backend's own `/proc` (KVM), whose lookups
3159    /// follow its executor at host-timed moments, a lookup decides no entry,
3160    /// and an entry it does not answer is numbered by its path's key.
3161    async fn root_placeholder_snapshot<G: Guest<Self>>(
3162        &self,
3163        guest: &mut G,
3164        fd: RawFd,
3165    ) -> Result<(Vec<DirEntry>, DirectoryInodes, DirectoryOrder), Error> {
3166        use crate::procfs::listing;
3167        use crate::procfs::listing::ModeledDirectory;
3168        let directory = ModeledDirectory::System(Vec::new());
3169        let facts = crate::tool_global::procfs_listing(guest, None).await;
3170        let names = listing::entries(&directory, self.cfg.proc_level, &facts).unwrap_or_default();
3171        let device = Self::procfs_key_device(guest);
3172        let proc = Path::new("/proc");
3173        // Each entry looked up where the kernel finds it: by name from the
3174        // descriptor, where it is open on procfs's root (ptrace), whatever the
3175        // caller's root; else by its absolute spelling.
3176        let base = self.kernel_listing_base(guest, Some(fd));
3177        let spellings = names
3178            .iter()
3179            .map(|(name, _)| {
3180                if base == libc::AT_FDCWD {
3181                    proc.join(OsStr::from_bytes(name))
3182                } else {
3183                    PathBuf::from(OsStr::from_bytes(name))
3184                }
3185            })
3186            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
3187        let looked_up = self.looked_up_inodes(guest, base, &spellings).await?;
3188        let backend_procfs = !procfs_inode::tool_can_name_guest_descriptors(&self.cfg.backend);
3189        let within = listing::within(&directory);
3190        let session_files = self.rendered_files(guest);
3191        let entries = names
3192            .into_iter()
3193            .zip(looked_up)
3194            .filter_map(|((name, ty), looked_up)| {
3195                let entry = match name.as_slice() {
3196                    b"." => within.clone(),
3197                    b".." => within.parent().unwrap_or(&within).to_path_buf(),
3198                    name => within.join(OsStr::from_bytes(name)),
3199                };
3200                // A file Detcore renders exists whatever the kernel has. (Not
3201                // a task's `comm` where the kernel serves every one.)
3202                let rendered = crate::procfs::ProcfsFile::from_path(
3203                    &proc.join(entry.strip_prefix("/").unwrap_or(&entry)),
3204                )
3205                .is_some_and(|file| {
3206                    !self.kernel_serves_every_comm(&file)
3207                        && !self.kernel_serves_every_status(&file)
3208                        && !self.kernel_serves_every_environ(&file)
3209                        && file.rendered_path(session_files).is_some()
3210                });
3211                let ino = match looked_up {
3212                    Ok(ino) => ino,
3213                    Err(Errno::ENOENT)
3214                        if name != b"." && name != b".." && !rendered && !backend_procfs =>
3215                    {
3216                        return None;
3217                    }
3218                    Err(_) => procfs_inode::path_inode(device, &entry).ino,
3219                };
3220                Some(DirEntry {
3221                    ino,
3222                    off: 0,
3223                    ty,
3224                    name,
3225                })
3226            })
3227            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
3228        let inodes = DirectoryInodes::new(device, Some(ProcfsDirectory::new(within)));
3229        Ok((entries, inodes, DirectoryOrder::ProcfsIds))
3230    }
3231
3232    /// The entries of the system-wide directory `directory` as Detcore lists
3233    /// it, and how to key their inode numbers, for its placeholder's
3234    /// directory stream: what `modeled_directory_snapshot` gives a
3235    /// descriptor on the kernel's directory (ptrace), from the same table
3236    /// (`procfs::listing`, at the run's `--proc` level), in the same order
3237    /// (`DirectoryOrder::NameBytes`), with the same entries left out: one
3238    /// the kernel's procfs does not have (a name the level lists that this
3239    /// kernel lacks: the object the guest's lookup reached has no such
3240    /// entry, `SystemObject::lacks`, read in that object when it was
3241    /// reached), unless Detcore renders it. A system-wide entry is the same
3242    /// for every task. No kernel cookies (the stream's positions are the
3243    /// guest's), and each entry numbered by its path's key on the procfs key
3244    /// device ([`Self::procfs_key_device`]), as a stat of a directory below
3245    /// numbers it ([`Self::system_directory_metadata`]).
3246    pub(super) fn system_placeholder_snapshot<G: Guest<Self>>(
3247        &self,
3248        guest: &G,
3249        directory: &SystemPlaceholder,
3250    ) -> Result<(Vec<DirEntry>, DirectoryInodes, DirectoryOrder), Error> {
3251        use crate::procfs::listing;
3252        let names = listing::entries(
3253            &directory.modeled(),
3254            self.cfg.proc_level,
3255            &listing::ListingFacts::default(),
3256        )
3257        .unwrap_or_default();
3258        let device = Self::procfs_key_device(guest);
3259        let within = directory.within();
3260        let canonical = directory.canonical();
3261        let session_files = self.rendered_files(guest);
3262        let entries = names
3263            .into_iter()
3264            .enumerate()
3265            .filter_map(|(index, (name, ty))| {
3266                let entry = match name.as_slice() {
3267                    b"." => within.clone(),
3268                    b".." => within.parent().unwrap_or(&within).to_path_buf(),
3269                    name => {
3270                        let path = canonical.join(OsStr::from_bytes(name));
3271                        // A file Detcore renders exists whatever the kernel
3272                        // has.
3273                        let rendered = crate::procfs::ProcfsFile::from_path(&path)
3274                            .is_some_and(|file| file.rendered_path(session_files).is_some());
3275                        if !rendered && directory.object.lacks(index) {
3276                            return None;
3277                        }
3278                        within.join(OsStr::from_bytes(name))
3279                    }
3280                };
3281                Some(DirEntry {
3282                    ino: procfs_inode::path_inode(device, &entry).ino,
3283                    off: 0,
3284                    ty,
3285                    name,
3286                })
3287            })
3288            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
3289        let inodes = DirectoryInodes::new(device, Some(ProcfsDirectory::new(within)));
3290        Ok((entries, inodes, DirectoryOrder::NameBytes))
3291    }
3292
3293    /// The entries of the task directory `binding` as Detcore lists it, the
3294    /// caller's own (package K slice K10) or another process's (slice 2b-1),
3295    /// and how to key their inode numbers,
3296    /// for its placeholder's directory stream: the model's names
3297    /// (`procfs::listing`, at the run's `--proc` level, the threads as the
3298    /// scheduler lists them), kept where every answer about the entry is
3299    /// Detcore's own, which is what Linux lists there less what this backend
3300    /// would answer otherwise than Linux:
3301    /// - in a process's or a thread's directory, the per-process files
