4885                    Ok(count) if 24 + 32 * usize::from(count) > 4096 => Err(Errno::ENOMEM),
4886                    Ok(count) => {
4887                        let mut request = vec![0u8; 24 + 32 * usize::from(count)];
4888                        match reverie::syscalls::Addr::<u8>::from_raw(argument)
4889                            .map(|address| guest.memory().read_exact(address, &mut request))
4890                        {
4891                            Some(Ok(())) if request[18..24].iter().any(|byte| *byte != 0) => {
4892                                Err(Errno::EINVAL)
4893                            }
4894                            Some(Ok(())) => Err(Errno::EISDIR),
4895                            _ => Err(Errno::EFAULT),
4896                        }
4897                    }
4898                }
4899            }
4900            FS_IOC_SETFLAGS => flag(guest).and(Err(Errno::ENOTTY)),
4901            // Every other command, FS_IOC_GETFLAGS and FS_IOC_FSGETXATTR
4902            // among them: procfs has no `fileattr_get` and directories no
4903            // `unlocked_ioctl`.
4904            _ => Err(Errno::ENOTTY),
4905        })
4906    }
4907
4908    /// Linux's answer to cloning descriptor `source` into a directory on the
4909    /// guest's procfs (`ioctl_file_clone`, `vfs_clone_file_range`): `EBADF`
4910    /// for no such descriptor or an `O_PATH` one, `EXDEV` for one on another
4911    /// filesystem (checked first), else `EISDIR` (`generic_file_rw_checks`):
4912    /// a placeholder, a procfs file Detcore renders, or a descriptor on the
4913    /// guest's procfs device.
4914    async fn placeholder_clone_answer<G: Guest<Self>>(
4915        &self,
4916        guest: &mut G,
4917        source: RawFd,
4918    ) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
4919        let facts = self.descriptor_facts(guest, source).await?;
4920        if facts.path_only {
4921            return Err(Errno::EBADF);
4922        }
4923        if facts.on_procfs {
4924            Err(Errno::EISDIR)
4925        } else {
4926            Err(Errno::EXDEV)
4927        }
4928    }
4929
4930    /// What Linux's checks of a call with a second descriptor operand need of
4931    /// descriptor `fd`: `EBADF` where it is not open; else its access mode,
4932    /// `O_PATH`, `O_APPEND`, whether it is a pipe, and whether it is on the
4933    /// guest's procfs (a placeholder, a procfs file Detcore renders, or a
4934    /// descriptor on the guest's procfs device). A placeholder's come from
4935    /// Detcore's model; any other descriptor's from one injected
4936    /// `fcntl(F_GETFL)` and one injected `fstat`.
4937    async fn descriptor_facts<G: Guest<Self>>(
4938        &self,
4939        guest: &mut G,
4940        fd: RawFd,
4941    ) -> Result<DescriptorFacts, Errno> {
4942        if fd < 0 {
4943            return Err(Errno::EBADF);
4944        }
4945        if let Some(state) = Self::placeholder_of(guest, fd) {
4946            let status = guest
4947                .thread_state()
4948                .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.status_flags())
4949                .unwrap_or(libc::O_DIRECTORY);
4950            return Ok(DescriptorFacts {
4951                readable: !state.path_only,
4952                writable: false,
4953                path_only: state.path_only,
4954                append: status & libc::O_APPEND != 0,
4955                pipe: false,
4956                on_procfs: true,
4957                placeholder: Some(state),
4958            });
4959        }
4960        let status = guest
4961            .inject(
4962                syscalls::Fcntl::new()
4963                    .with_fd(fd)
4964                    .with_cmd(FcntlCmd::F_GETFL),
4965            )
4966            .await? as i32;
4967        let rendered = guest
4968            .thread_state()
4969            .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.procfs_rendered_path().is_some())
4970            .unwrap_or(false);
4971        let stat = if status & libc::O_PATH == 0 {
4972            self.inject_fstat(guest, fd).await.ok()
4973        } else {
4974            None
4975        };
4976        let accmode = status & libc::O_ACCMODE;
4977        Ok(DescriptorFacts {
4978            readable: status & libc::O_PATH == 0 && accmode != libc::O_WRONLY,
4979            writable: status & libc::O_PATH == 0 && accmode != libc::O_RDONLY,
4980            path_only: status & libc::O_PATH != 0,
4981            append: status & libc::O_APPEND != 0,
4982            pipe: stat.is_some_and(|stat| stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT == libc::S_IFIFO),
4983            on_procfs: rendered
4984                || stat.is_some_and(|stat| stat.st_dev == Self::procfs_key_device(guest)),
4985            placeholder: None,
4986        })
4987    }
4988
4989    /// `flock` through a placeholder descriptor (see
4990    /// [`Self::answer_placeholder_call`]): each directory is locked apart
4991    /// (`procfs::locks::LockLedger::placeholder_flock`), the description
4992    /// owning the lock as on Linux, and losing it with its last reference. A
4993    /// contended blocking request is refused as `Detcore::handle_flock`
4994    /// refuses one, and so is a lock asked for by a process whose descriptor
4995    /// tables the ledger cannot tell apart (named residual).
4996    async fn placeholder_flock<G: Guest<Self>>(
4997        &self,
4998        guest: &mut G,
4999        fd: RawFd,
5000        state: PlaceholderState,
5001        operation: i32,
5002    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
5003        use crate::procfs::locks::FlockMode;
5004        use crate::procfs::locks::PlaceholderFlockOutcome;
5005        let mode = match operation & !libc::LOCK_NB {
5006            libc::LOCK_SH => Some(FlockMode::Shared),
5007            libc::LOCK_EX => Some(FlockMode::Exclusive),
5008            libc::LOCK_UN => None,
5009            _ => return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into()),
5010        };
5011        // An `O_PATH` descriptor, after the command (`flock_translate_cmd`).
5012        if state.path_only {
5013            return Err(Errno::EBADF.into());
5014        }
5015        let open_file = guest.thread_state().with_detfd(fd, |detfd| {
5016            detfd.set_lock_tracked();
5017            detfd.open_file_id()
5018        })?;
5019        let slots = guest.thread_state().slots_naming(open_file);
5020        let outcome = crate::tool_global::placeholder_flock(
5021            guest,
5022            crate::procfs::locks::PlaceholderFlock {
5023                key: state.directory.lock_key(),
5024                open_file,
5025                slots,
5026                mode,
5027                nonblocking: operation & libc::LOCK_NB != 0,
5028            },
5029        )
5030        .await;
5031        match outcome {
5032            PlaceholderFlockOutcome::Done => Ok(0),
5033            PlaceholderFlockOutcome::WouldBlock => Err(Errno::EWOULDBLOCK.into()),
5034            PlaceholderFlockOutcome::Contended => {
5035                let dettid = guest.thread_state().dettid;
5036                super::report_flock_refusal(&format!(
5037                    "[dtid {dettid}] blocking flock(fd={fd}, operation={operation:#x}) of a /proc \
5038                     directory Detcore serves refused: another open file holds a conflicting \
5039                     lock. {}",
5040                    super::FLOCK_REFUSAL_ADVICE
5041                ));
5042                self.refuse_unserviceable_operation(guest, Sysno::flock, Errno::ENOLCK)
5043                    .await
5044            }
5045            PlaceholderFlockOutcome::Unfollowable => {
5046                self.refuse_placeholder_call(
5047                    guest,
5048                    Sysno::flock,
5049                    Errno::ENOLCK,
5050                    &format!(
5051                        "flock(fd={fd}) of {}, a /proc directory Detcore serves, where a \
5052                         process that has had a thread with a descriptor table of its own \
5053                         holds the lock or asks for it: Detcore cannot follow that process's \
5054                         references to the lock's open file",
5055                        state.directory.canonical().display()
5056                    ),
5057                )
5058                .await
5059            }
5060            PlaceholderFlockOutcome::InFlight => {
5061                self.refuse_placeholder_call(
5062                    guest,
5063                    Sysno::flock,
5064                    Errno::ENOLCK,
5065                    &format!(
5066                        "flock(fd={fd}) of {}, a /proc directory Detcore serves, while a copy of \
5067                         its open file is in flight over SCM_RIGHTS, a reference Detcore does \
5068                         not follow",
5069                        state.directory.canonical().display()
5070                    ),
5071                )
5072                .await
5073            }
5074        }
5075    }
5076
5077    /// The extended-attribute calls through a placeholder descriptor:
5078    /// procfs's answers (`rendered_procfs::placeholder_xattr_answer`), as
5079    /// for a file global root owns on `/proc`, and for one the caller owns
5080    /// on its descriptor directory. An `O_PATH` descriptor is `EBADF` after
5081    /// the arguments Linux reads first (the flags, the name, the size and the
5082    /// value: `filename_setxattr`'s `setxattr_copy`, `import_xattr_name`).
5083    async fn placeholder_xattr<G: Guest<Self>>(
5084        &self,
5085        guest: &mut G,
5086        call: &Syscall,
5087        state: PlaceholderState,
5088    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
5089        let owned = match state.directory {
5090            PlaceholderDirectory::Root | PlaceholderDirectory::System(_) => None,
5091            PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(binding) => Some(binding.pid),
5092            PlaceholderDirectory::Task(binding) => Some(binding.pid),
5093        };
5094        if state.path_only {
5095            return Err(xattr_arguments_answer(&guest.memory(), call)
5096                .err()
5097                .unwrap_or(Errno::EBADF)
5098                .into());
5099        }
5100        self.placeholder_xattr_answer(guest, call, owned).await
5101    }
5102
5103    /// The `utimensat` of a placeholder itself, with no path (`futimens`) or
5104    /// an empty one and `AT_EMPTY_PATH`, in Linux's order (`utimensat`,
5105    /// `do_utimes_fd` or `do_utimes_path`, `vfs_utimes`, `notify_change`):
5106    /// both times omitted is 0 before anything else is looked at; then the
5107    /// flags (`futimens` takes none), an `O_PATH` descriptor without a path
5108    /// (`EBADF`), the times' nanoseconds (`EINVAL`); then, on global root's
5109    /// `/proc`, explicit times need its owner (`EPERM`, `setattr_prepare`)
5110    /// and the current time write access (`EACCES`, `may_setattr`); the
5111    /// caller's own descriptor directory is its own (0, the times kept no
5112    /// further: named).
5113    async fn placeholder_utimensat<G: Guest<Self>>(
5114        &self,
5115        guest: &mut G,
5116        state: PlaceholderState,
5117        futimens: bool,
5118        times: usize,
5119        flags: i32,
5120    ) -> Result<i64, Error> {
5121        const UTIME_NOW: i64 = (1 << 30) - 1;
5122        const UTIME_OMIT: i64 = (1 << 30) - 2;
5123        let times = match reverie::syscalls::Addr::<[libc::timespec; 2]>::from_raw(times) {
5124            None => None,
5125            Some(address) => Some(guest.memory().read_value(address)?),
5126        };
5127        if times.is_some_and(|times| times.iter().all(|time| time.tv_nsec == UTIME_OMIT)) {
5128            return Ok(0);
5129        }
5130        if futimens && flags != 0
5131            || !futimens && flags & !(libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW | libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH) != 0
5132        {
5133            return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
5134        }
5135        if futimens && state.path_only {
5136            return Err(Errno::EBADF.into());
5137        }
5138        let valid = |nsec: i64| {
5139            nsec == UTIME_NOW || nsec == UTIME_OMIT || (0..1_000_000_000).contains(&nsec)
5140        };
5141        if times.is_some_and(|times| times.iter().any(|time| !valid(time.tv_nsec))) {
5142            return Err(Errno::EINVAL.into());
5143        }
5144        // A system-wide directory is the caller's to touch where it may act
5145        // as the directory's owner (`setattr_prepare`; `proc_sys_setattr`
5146        // and `proc_notify_change` then keep the times), as a descriptor
5147        // directory is its own; otherwise it is answered as global root's
5148        // `/proc` is.
5149        let owned = match &state.directory {
5150            PlaceholderDirectory::Root => false,
5151            PlaceholderDirectory::System(_) => placeholder_owner_or_capable(&state.directory)?,
5152            PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(_) | PlaceholderDirectory::Task(_) => true,
5153        };
5154        if owned {
5155            return Ok(0);
5156        }
5157        match times {
5158            Some(times) if times.iter().any(|time| time.tv_nsec != UTIME_NOW) => {
5159                Err(Errno::EPERM.into())
5160            }
5161            _ => Err(Errno::EACCES.into()),
5162        }
5163    }
5164
5165    /// The path-less `faccessat2` of a placeholder: `/proc`'s mode (0555)
5166    /// grants no write, and global root's directory no override; the
5167    /// caller's own descriptor directory is open to it
5168    /// (`proc_fd_permission`).
5169    fn placeholder_access(
5170        &self,
5171        directory: &PlaceholderDirectory,
5172        mode: i32,
5173        flags: i32,
5174    ) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
5175        if mode & !(libc::R_OK | libc::W_OK | libc::X_OK) != 0
5176            || flags & !(libc::AT_EACCESS | libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW | libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH) != 0
5177        {
5178            return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
5179        }
5180        // A system-wide directory grants no write either: a sysctl
5181        // directory to no one (`test_perm` of its 0555 table entry), and
5182        // another only to a caller that may override its owner's mode.
5183        let unwritable = match directory {
5184            PlaceholderDirectory::Root => true,
5185            PlaceholderDirectory::System(system) => {
5186                system.is_sysctl() || !placeholder_owner_or_capable(directory)?
5187            }
5188            PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(_) | PlaceholderDirectory::Task(_) => false,
5189        };
5190        if unwritable && mode & libc::W_OK != 0 {
5191            return Err(Errno::EACCES);
5192        }
5193        Ok(0)
5194    }
5195
5196    /// `sendfile`, `splice`, `tee` and `copy_file_range` with a placeholder
5197    /// descriptor on either side, each in Linux's order of checks (v6.17
5198    /// `fs/read_write.c`, `fs/splice.c`), the other descriptor's facts from
5199    /// [`Self::descriptor_facts`]. A directory has no `splice_read` and cannot
5200    /// be written or copied. `None` where neither operand is a placeholder.
5201    /// - `copy_file_range`: both descriptors (`EBADF`), both offsets
5202    ///   (`EFAULT`), the flags (`EINVAL`), then a directory on either side
5203    ///   (`EISDIR`), whatever the length;
5204    /// - `sendfile`: the offset (`EFAULT`); the input (`EBADF`, not readable
5205    ///   `EBADF`, an offset into a pipe `ESPIPE`, a count past `SSIZE_MAX` or
5206    ///   a negative position `EINVAL`); the output (`EBADF`, not writable
5207    ///   `EBADF`); a directory's position past procfs's limit (`EOVERFLOW`),
5208    ///   an appending output (`EINVAL`); then a count of 0 is 0 and any other
5209    ///   `EINVAL`;
5210    /// - `splice`: a length of 0 is 0 before anything; the flags (`EINVAL`);
5211    ///   both descriptors (`EBADF`); an offset given for a pipe (`ESPIPE`);
5212    ///   the offsets (`EFAULT`); the input not readable or the output not
5213    ///   writable (`EBADF`); then `EINVAL`;
5214    /// - `tee`: the flags (`EINVAL`), a length of 0 (0), both descriptors
5215    ///   (`EBADF`), the input not readable or the output not writable
5216    ///   (`EBADF`), then not two pipes (`EINVAL`).
5217    ///
5218    /// Each flags argument is read at its Linux type's width, as the kernel
5219    /// reads the register (`unsigned int` for `copy_file_range`, `splice`
5220    /// and `tee`).
5221    ///
5222    /// A transfer from a placeholder into a pipe (`sendfile` to a pipe, or
5223    /// `splice` with a pipe output), past the checks above, is refused by
5224    /// name: Linux makes it only once the pipe has a reader and room
5225    /// (`splice_file_to_pipe`'s `wait_for_space`: `SIGPIPE` and `EPIPE`
5226    /// without a reader, a wait while it is full), which Detcore does not
5227    /// model, and only then fails for the directory (`EINVAL`).
5228    async fn answer_placeholder_transfer<G: Guest<Self>>(
5229        &self,
5230        guest: &mut G,
5231        sysno: Sysno,
5232        args: &syscalls::SyscallArgs,
5233    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
5234        const SPLICE_F_ALL: u32 = 0xf;
5235        let (input_fd, output_fd) = match sysno {
5236            Sysno::sendfile => (args.arg1 as i32, args.arg0 as i32),
5237            Sysno::splice | Sysno::copy_file_range => (args.arg0 as i32, args.arg2 as i32),
5238            _ => (args.arg0 as i32, args.arg1 as i32),
5239        };
5240        if Self::placeholder_of(guest, input_fd).is_none()
5241            && Self::placeholder_of(guest, output_fd).is_none()
5242        {
5243            return None;
5244        }
5245        let readable_offset = |guest: &G, address: usize| -> Result<Option<i64>, Errno> {
5246            if address == 0 {
5247                return Ok(None);
5248            }
5249            let address = reverie::syscalls::Addr::<i64>::from_raw(address).ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)?;
5250            guest
5251                .memory()
5252                .read_value(address)
5253                .map(Some)
5254                .map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)
5255        };
5256        // `Ok(None)`: a transfer into a pipe, refused by name below.
5257        let answer: Result<Option<i64>, Errno> = async {
5258            match sysno {
5259                Sysno::copy_file_range => {
5260                    let input = self.descriptor_facts(guest, input_fd).await?;
5261                    let output = self.descriptor_facts(guest, output_fd).await?;
5262                    if input.path_only || output.path_only {
5263                        return Err(Errno::EBADF);
5264                    }
5265                    readable_offset(guest, args.arg1)?;
5266                    readable_offset(guest, args.arg3)?;
5267                    if args.arg5 as u32 != 0 {
5268                        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
5269                    }
5270                    Err(Errno::EISDIR)
5271                }
5272                Sysno::sendfile => {
5273                    let offset = readable_offset(guest, args.arg2)?;
5274                    let input = self.descriptor_facts(guest, input_fd).await?;
5275                    if input.path_only || !input.readable {
5276                        return Err(Errno::EBADF);
5277                    }
5278                    if offset.is_some() && input.pipe {
5279                        return Err(Errno::ESPIPE);
5280                    }
5281                    let count = args.arg3;
5282                    let position = match (offset, input.placeholder) {
5283                        (Some(offset), _) => offset,
5284                        (None, Some(state)) => self.placeholder_position(guest, input_fd, state),
5285                        (None, None) => 0,
5286                    };
5287                    if isize::try_from(count).is_err() || position < 0 {
5288                        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
5289                    }
5290                    let output = self.descriptor_facts(guest, output_fd).await?;
5291                    if output.path_only || !output.writable {
5292                        return Err(Errno::EBADF);
5293                    }
5294                    if input.placeholder.is_some() && position >= PROCFS_MAX_BYTES {
5295                        return Err(Errno::EOVERFLOW);
5296                    }
5297                    if output.pipe && input.placeholder.is_some() {
5298                        return Ok(None);
5299                    }
5300                    if !output.pipe && output.append {
5301                        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
5302                    }
5303                    if count == 0 {
5304                        Ok(Some(0))
5305                    } else {
5306                        Err(Errno::EINVAL)
5307                    }
5308                }
5309                Sysno::splice => {
5310                    if args.arg4 == 0 {
5311                        return Ok(Some(0));
5312                    }
5313                    if args.arg5 as u32 & !SPLICE_F_ALL != 0 {
5314                        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
5315                    }
5316                    let input = self.descriptor_facts(guest, input_fd).await?;
5317                    let output = self.descriptor_facts(guest, output_fd).await?;
5318                    if input.path_only || output.path_only {
5319                        return Err(Errno::EBADF);
5320                    }
5321                    if (input.pipe && args.arg1 != 0) || (output.pipe && args.arg3 != 0) {
5322                        return Err(Errno::ESPIPE);
5323                    }
5324                    readable_offset(guest, args.arg1)?;
5325                    readable_offset(guest, args.arg3)?;
5326                    if !input.readable || !output.writable {
5327                        return Err(Errno::EBADF);
5328                    }
5329                    if output.pipe && input.placeholder.is_some() {
5330                        return Ok(None);
5331                    }
5332                    Err(Errno::EINVAL)
5333                }
5334                _ => {
5335                    if args.arg3 as u32 & !SPLICE_F_ALL != 0 {
5336                        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
5337                    }
5338                    if args.arg2 == 0 {
5339                        return Ok(Some(0));
5340                    }
5341                    let input = self.descriptor_facts(guest, input_fd).await?;
5342                    let output = self.descriptor_facts(guest, output_fd).await?;
5343                    if input.path_only || output.path_only || !input.readable || !output.writable {
5344                        return Err(Errno::EBADF);
5345                    }
5346                    Err(Errno::EINVAL)
5347                }
5348            }
5349        }
5350        .await;
5351        Some(match answer {
5352            Ok(Some(value)) => Ok(value),
5353            Err(errno) => Err(errno.into()),
5354            Ok(None) => {
5355                self.refuse_placeholder_call(
5356                    guest,
5357                    sysno,
5358                    Errno::EINVAL,
5359                    &format!(
5360                        "{sysno} from descriptor {input_fd}, a /proc directory Detcore serves, \
5361                         into the pipe {output_fd}: Linux makes the transfer only once the pipe \
5362                         has a reader and room (SIGPIPE and EPIPE without a reader, a wait while \
5363                         it is full), which Detcore does not model"
5364                    ),
5365                )
5366                .await
5367            }
5368        })
5369    }
5370
5371    /// The guest's position in the directory placeholder descriptor `fd`
5372    /// stands for: a descriptor directory's, or `/proc`'s directory stream's.
5373    fn placeholder_position<G: Guest<Self>>(
5374        &self,
5375        guest: &G,
5376        fd: RawFd,
5377        state: PlaceholderState,
5378    ) -> i64 {
5379        match state.directory {
5380            PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(_) => state.position as i64,
5381            PlaceholderDirectory::Root
5382            | PlaceholderDirectory::Task(_)
5383            | PlaceholderDirectory::System(_) => guest
5384                .thread_state()
5385                .with_detfd(fd, |detfd| detfd.directory_stream_position())
5386                .map_or(0, |position| position as i64),
5387        }
5388    }
5389
5390    /// Whether [`Self::respelled_below_descriptor_directory`] may respell
5391    /// anything in `call`: it looks a path up, a placeholder has been opened
5392    /// or recognized in this tool process (until then no path can lead below
5393    /// one), and Detcore serves placeholders. Asked before each injection that
5394    /// carries the guest's paths, so it reads nothing from the guest.
5395    pub(crate) fn may_respell_below_descriptor_directory<G: Guest<Self>>(
5396        guest: &G,
5397        call: &Syscall,
5398    ) -> bool {
5399        let sysno = call.number();
5400        (!relative_operands(sysno).is_empty() || !absolute_operands(sysno).is_empty())
5401            && OPENED_ANY.load(Ordering::SeqCst)
5402            && Self::serves_placeholders(guest)
5403    }
5404
5405    /// The path operands of `call` that lead below a placeholder directory,
5406    /// each respelled so that the kernel beneath the backend resolves it as
5407    /// Linux resolves it below the directory the placeholder stands for
5408    /// ([`Self::respelled_lookup`]). The caller stages the respelled paths
5409    /// for one injection (`Detcore::procfs_translated_call`), before anything
5410    /// else runs. A stale descriptor directory (its task gone, or its numbers
5411    /// held by another task) fails the lookup with `ENOENT`, as
5412    /// `get_proc_task` does; more than `MAXSYMLINKS` links, `ELOOP`; a
5413    /// respelled path longer than `PATH_MAX`, `ENAMETOOLONG`; an `openat2`
5414    /// whose resolution is scoped ([`SCOPED_RESOLVE`]), `EXDEV`. Empty when
5415    /// nothing leads below one.
5416    pub(crate) async fn respelled_below_descriptor_directory<G: Guest<Self>>(
5417        guest: &mut G,
5418        call: &Syscall,
5419    ) -> Result<Vec<RespelledOperand>, Errno> {
5420        if !Self::serves_placeholders(guest) {
5421            return Ok(Vec::new());
5422        }
5423        let (sysno, args) = call.into_parts();
5424        let read_operand = |guest: &G, pointer: usize| -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
5425            PathPtr::from_ptr(pointer as *const libc::c_char)
5426                .and_then(|pointer| pointer.read(&guest.memory()).ok())
5427                .map(|path: PathBuf| path.into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes())
5428        };
5429        // `openat2`'s resolution flags, in its `struct open_how`
5430        // (`{ flags, mode, resolve }`, at least 24 bytes).
5431        let resolve = if sysno == Sysno::openat2 {
5432            match reverie::syscalls::Addr::<[u64; 3]>::from_raw(args.arg2) {
5433                Some(how) if args.arg3 >= 24 => guest
5434                    .memory()
5435                    .read_value(how)
5436                    .map(|how| how[2])
5437                    .unwrap_or(0),
5438                _ => 0,
5439            }
5440        } else {
5441            0
5442        };
5443        let mut respelled = Vec::new();
5444        let operands = relative_operands(sysno)
5445            .iter()
5446            .map(|&(dirfd, path)| (Some(dirfd), path))
5447            .chain(absolute_operands(sysno).iter().map(|&path| (None, path)))
5448            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
5449        for (dirfd_index, path_index) in operands {
5450            let pointer = argument(&args, path_index);
5451            if pointer == 0 {
5452                continue;
5453            }
5454            let Some(path) = read_operand(guest, pointer) else {
5455                continue;
5456            };
5457            if path.is_empty() {
5458                continue;
5459            }
5460            let dirfd = dirfd_index.map(|index| argument(&args, index) as i32);
5461            let follows = follows_final(sysno, &args, path_index);
5462            let Some((bytes, absolute)) =
5463                Self::respelled_lookup(guest, dirfd, &path, follows, resolve).await?
5464            else {
5465                continue;
5466            };
5467            if resolve & SCOPED_RESOLVE != 0 {
5468                return Err(Errno::EXDEV);
5469            }
5470            if bytes.len() >= PATH_LIMIT {
5471                return Err(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG);
5472            }
5473            respelled.push(RespelledOperand {
5474                dirfd: if absolute { dirfd_index } else { None },
5475                path: path_index,
5476                bytes,
5477            });
5478        }
5479        Ok(respelled)
5480    }
5481
5482    /// `path`, looked up from `dirfd` (`None` for a call that looks it up
5483    /// from the working directory or the root), respelled where it leads
5484    /// below a placeholder directory, as `Some` of the respelled bytes and
5485    /// whether they are absolute (looked up from `AT_FDCWD`) or looked up
5486    /// from where the guest's path was. `follows` says whether the call
5487    /// follows a final link (a path that is a descriptor link to a
5488    /// placeholder, and nothing more, then names the directory). By the
5489    /// placeholder's object ([`PlaceholderObject`]):
5490    /// - procfs's root (ptrace): the kernel resolves every path through the
5491    ///   descriptor below procfs, so `/proc` itself needs nothing, and a path
5492    ///   below a descriptor directory goes on below that directory's own path
5493    ///   within procfs: relative to the descriptor, `<pid>/fd/<path>`, and
5494    ///   through a descriptor link to one, the same path after the link
5495    ///   (`/proc/self/fd/D/<pid>/fd/<rest>`, [`Self::within_after_links`]),
5496    ///   so that each lookup starts where the guest's does, from the
5497    ///   descriptor or the guest's own root, in every state of the caller;
5498    /// - the placeholder (KVM): a path below the descriptor, or through its
5499    ///   link, at the directory's absolute spelling, with every link counted
5500    ///   against `MAXSYMLINKS` ([`Self::through_own_links`]).
5501    ///
5502    /// A path relative to the working directory, or to a descriptor that is
5503    /// no placeholder, that leads through a descriptor link to one (`fd/D/N`
5504    /// from `/proc/self`, `proc/self/fd/D/N` from `/`) is respelled as Detcore
5505    /// decides about it ([`Self::respelled_through_links_from`]).
5506    async fn respelled_lookup<G: Guest<Self>>(
5507        guest: &mut G,
5508        dirfd: Option<RawFd>,
5509        path: &[u8],
5510        follows: bool,
5511        resolve: u64,
5512    ) -> Result<Option<(Vec<u8>, bool)>, Errno> {
5513        let object = Self::placeholder_object(guest);
5514        if !path.starts_with(b"/") {
5515            // A call without a directory descriptor looks its path up from the
5516            // working directory.
5517            let dirfd = dirfd.unwrap_or(libc::AT_FDCWD);
5518            // A placeholder Detcore keeps no record of (one received without
5519            // sequentialized threads) is told at its use.
5520            let directory = match Self::placeholder_of(guest, dirfd) {
5521                Some(state) => state.directory,
5522                None => match Self::unrecorded_placeholder(guest, dirfd).await {
5523                    Some(directory) => directory,
5524                    None => {
5525                        return Self::respelled_through_links_from(guest, dirfd, path, follows)
5526                            .await;
5527                    }
5528                },
5529            };
5530            if !Self::placeholder_current(guest, &directory).await {
5531                return Err(Errno::ENOENT);
5532            }
5533            let caller = Self::caller_numbers(guest).ok_or(Errno::ENOENT)?;
5534            return Ok(match object {
5535                PlaceholderObject::ProcfsRoot => {
5536                    let (within, logical) = match &directory {
5537                        PlaceholderDirectory::Root => (Vec::new(), PathBuf::from("/proc")),
5538                        PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(binding) => {
5539                            let within = binding.within().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5540                            (within[1..].to_vec(), binding.canonical())
5541                        }
5542                        // Served only where the guest's procfs is not the
5543                        // kernel's ([`Self::serves_task_directories`]); spelled
5544                        // as a descriptor directory is should one be received.
5545                        PlaceholderDirectory::Task(binding) => {
5546                            let within = binding.within().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5547                            (within[1..].to_vec(), binding.canonical())
5548                        }
5549                        PlaceholderDirectory::System(directory) => {
5550                            let within = directory.within().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5551                            (within[1..].to_vec(), directory.canonical())
5552                        }
5553                    };
5554                    let (rest, changed) =
5555                        Self::within_after_links(guest, &logical, path, follows, caller);
5556                    if within.is_empty() {
5557                        changed.then_some((rest, false))
5558                    } else {
5559                        Some((below(Path::new(OsStr::from_bytes(&within)), &rest), false))
5560                    }
5561                }
5562                PlaceholderObject::Placeholder => {
5563                    // With `RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS` the directory is spelled by
5564                    // number, which crosses no symbolic link of its own.
5565                    let base = if resolve & libc::RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS != 0 {
5566                        directory.canonical()
5567                    } else {
5568                        directory.lookup_base(caller)
5569                    };
5570                    let (bytes, _) =
5571                        Self::through_own_links(guest, below(&base, path), caller, false, follows)
5572                            .await?;
5573                    Some((bytes, true))
5574                }
5575            });
5576        }
5577        let Some(caller) = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest) else {
5578            return Ok(None);
5579        };
5580        Ok(match object {
5581            PlaceholderObject::ProcfsRoot => {
5582                let (bytes, changed) =
5583                    Self::within_after_links(guest, Path::new(""), path, follows, caller);
5584                changed.then_some((bytes, false))
5585            }
5586            PlaceholderObject::Placeholder => {
5587                let (bytes, through) =
5588                    Self::through_own_links(guest, path.to_vec(), caller, true, follows).await?;
5589                through.then_some((bytes, true))
5590            }
5591        })
5592    }
5593
5594    /// `path`, relative, looked up from `dirfd`, the working directory
5595    /// (`AT_FDCWD`) or a descriptor that is no placeholder, respelled where it
5596    /// leads through one of the caller's descriptor links to a placeholder
5597    /// directory, as Detcore decides about it: the path made absolute from
5598    /// the base's path (`Detcore::observed_path`, the decisions'
5599    /// [`Self::path_below_descriptor_directory`] and the `--proc` level
5600    /// table's), then through the links ([`Self::decided_path`]). On ptrace
5601    /// it stays relative, with the directory's path within procfs inserted
5602    /// after each such link ([`Self::within_after_links`], from the base);
5603    /// on KVM it is the absolute spelling ([`Self::through_own_links`]).
5604    /// `None` where the path leads through no such link, or where Detcore
5605    /// does not know the base's path (from a descriptor whose recorded path
5606    /// is a link's spelling, [`Self::starts_at_a_recorded_link`], or one it
5607    /// keeps no path for), where nothing is decided through the links
5608    /// either and the kernel looks the guest's path up.
5609    async fn respelled_through_links_from<G: Guest<Self>>(
5610        guest: &mut G,
5611        dirfd: RawFd,
5612        path: &[u8],
5613        follows: bool,
5614    ) -> Result<Option<(Vec<u8>, bool)>, Errno> {
5615        let Some(caller) = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest) else {
5616            return Ok(None);
5617        };
5618        let relative = Path::new(OsStr::from_bytes(path));
5619        if Self::starts_at_a_recorded_link(guest, dirfd, relative) {
5620            return Ok(None);
5621        }
5622        Self::prepare_guest_cwd(guest, dirfd, relative).await;
5623        let Some(observed) = Self::observed_path(guest, dirfd, relative)
5624            .ok()
5625            .filter(|observed| observed.is_absolute())
5626        else {
5627            return Ok(None);
5628        };
5629        match Self::placeholder_object(guest) {
5630            PlaceholderObject::ProcfsRoot => {
5631                // The base is the absolute path without the guest's relative
5632                // spelling, which `observed_path` appends to it.
5633                let observed = observed.as_os_str().as_bytes();
5634                let Some(base) = observed.strip_suffix(path) else {
5635                    return Ok(None);
5636                };
5637                let base = base.strip_suffix(b"/").unwrap_or(base);
5638                let base = if base.is_empty() { &b"/"[..] } else { base };
5639                let (rest, changed) = Self::within_after_links(
5640                    guest,
5641                    Path::new(OsStr::from_bytes(base)),
5642                    path,
5643                    follows,
5644                    caller,
5645                );
5646                Ok(changed.then_some((rest, false)))
5647            }
5648            PlaceholderObject::Placeholder => {
5649                let (bytes, through) = Self::through_own_links(
5650                    guest,
5651                    observed.into_os_string().into_vec(),
5652                    caller,
5653                    true,
5654                    follows,
5655                )
5656                .await?;
5657                Ok(through.then_some((bytes, true)))
5658            }
5659        }
5660    }
5661
5662    /// `rest`, a path the kernel resolves from the procfs directory
5663    /// `logical` (absolute, `/proc` or `/proc/<pid>/fd`; empty for an
5664    /// absolute `rest`), with the path within procfs of each descriptor
5665    /// directory a descriptor link to a placeholder reaches inserted after
5666    /// that link (`fd/D/x` from `/proc/self` becomes `fd/D/<pid>/fd/x`), as
5667    /// the kernel needs it where every placeholder is open on procfs's root
5668    /// (ptrace): the link leads there, and the rest of the path then goes
5669    /// on below the directory the placeholder stands for. A link to the
5670    /// root placeholder needs nothing; a final link that the call does not
5671    /// follow (`follows`) is the link itself. Each link is told with the
5672    /// caller's numbers `caller` ([`Self::own_link_directory_of`]); the scan
5673    /// stops at a link that is no placeholder of the caller's, whose file
5674    /// only the kernel knows. Returns the path and whether anything was
5675    /// inserted. The kernel counts every link it follows on the way, as it
5676    /// does for the guest's own path, which this lengthens by no link.
5677    fn within_after_links<G: Guest<Self>>(
5678        guest: &G,
5679        logical: &Path,
5680        rest: &[u8],
5681        follows: bool,
5682        caller: (i32, i32),
5683    ) -> (Vec<u8>, bool) {
5684        let mut logical = logical.as_os_str().as_bytes().to_vec();
5685        let mut rest = rest;
5686        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(rest.len());
5687        let mut changed = false;
5688        let mut hops = 0;
5689        loop {
5690            let mut candidate = logical.clone();
5691            if !candidate.is_empty() {
5692                candidate.push(b'/');
5693            }
5694            let offset = candidate.len();
5695            candidate.extend_from_slice(rest);
5696            let Some((link, end)) = descriptor_link_prefix(&candidate) else {
5697                break;
5698            };
5699            if end <= offset {
5700                break;
5701            }
5702            let consumed = end - offset;
5703            let directory = match Self::own_link_directory_of(guest, &link, caller) {
5704                Some(directory) => directory,
5705                None => break,
5706            };
5707            let tail = &rest[consumed..];
5708            if tail.is_empty() && !follows {
5709                break;
5710            }
5711            hops += 1;
5712            if hops > LINK_LIMIT {
5713                break;
5714            }
5715            out.extend_from_slice(&rest[..consumed]);
5716            rest = tail;
5717            match directory {
5718                PlaceholderDirectory::Root => logical = b"/proc".to_vec(),
5719                PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(binding) => {
5720                    let within = binding.within().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5721                    out.extend_from_slice(&within);
5722                    logical = binding.canonical().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5723                    changed = true;
5724                }
5725                PlaceholderDirectory::Task(binding) => {
5726                    let within = binding.within().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5727                    out.extend_from_slice(&within);
5728                    logical = binding.canonical().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5729                    changed = true;
5730                }
5731                PlaceholderDirectory::System(directory) => {
5732                    let within = directory.within().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5733                    out.extend_from_slice(&within);
5734                    logical = directory.canonical().into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes();
5735                    changed = true;
5736                }
5737            }
5738        }
5739        out.extend_from_slice(rest);
5740        (out, changed)
5741    }
5742
5743    /// `path`, absolute, spelled as Linux resolves it where it leads through
5744    /// one of the caller's descriptor links to a placeholder directory and on
5745    /// below it ([`through_descriptor_link`]), or ends at one that the call
5746    /// follows (`follows`): the directory's absolute spelling joined with
5747    /// the rest, again for each such link the result leads through, and
5748    /// whether any did. `ENOENT` for a descriptor directory whose task is
5749    /// gone, `ELOOP` where the links Linux follows on the way (each
5750    /// descriptor link, and, where `guest_spelled` says the first one is the
5751    /// guest's own spelling, the selector links before it,
5752    /// [`selector_links`]) pass `MAXSYMLINKS`.
5753    async fn through_own_links<G: Guest<Self>>(
5754        guest: &mut G,
5755        mut path: Vec<u8>,
5756        caller: (i32, i32),
5757        guest_spelled: bool,
5758        follows: bool,
5759    ) -> Result<(Vec<u8>, bool), Errno> {
5760        let mut hops = 0;
5761        let mut links = 0;
5762        loop {
5763            // A final link the call follows names the directory itself,
5764            // spelled as the guest spells it.
5765            let (link, rest) = match through_descriptor_link(&path) {
5766                Some((link, rest)) => (link, Some(rest)),
5767                None if follows => match descriptor_link_prefix(&path) {
5768                    Some((link, end)) if end == path.len() => (link, None),
5769                    _ => break,
5770                },
5771                None => break,
5772            };
5773            let Some(directory) = Self::own_link_directory(guest, &link) else {
5774                break;
5775            };
5776            if hops == 0 && guest_spelled {
5777                links += selector_links(&link);
5778            }
5779            hops += 1;
5780            links += 1;
5781            if links > LINK_LIMIT {
5782                return Err(Errno::ELOOP);
5783            }
5784            if !Self::placeholder_current(guest, &directory).await {
5785                return Err(Errno::ENOENT);
5786            }
5787            let base = directory.lookup_base(caller);
5788            path = match rest {
5789                Some(rest) => below(&base, &rest),
5790                None => base.into_os_string().into_encoded_bytes(),
5791            };
5792        }
5793        Ok((path, hops > 0))
5794    }
5795
5796    /// Whether a lookup of `path` relative to `dirfd` starts at a descriptor
5797    /// that is no placeholder and whose recorded path is, or walks through,
5798    /// a descriptor link (`/proc/<pid>/fd/N`, `/dev/fd/N`). An open through a
5799    /// link records the link's own spelling where the link may reach another
5800    /// file than the one it reached (another process's link, or a link whose
5801    /// descriptor a thread replaced while the open waited:
5802    /// `syscalls::files::path_to_record_for_open`). Linux starts the lookup
5803    /// at the file that open returned, which no later walk of that spelling
5804    /// need reach, so Detcore decides nothing about it through the
5805    /// descriptor links (a FIFO a blocked open returned stays a FIFO, below
5806    /// which every name is `ENOTDIR`, though its link's number now holds
5807    /// `/proc`), and the kernel answers it.
5808    pub(super) fn starts_at_a_recorded_link<G: Guest<Self>>(
5809        guest: &G,
5810        dirfd: RawFd,
5811        path: &Path,
5812    ) -> bool {
5813        if dirfd == libc::AT_FDCWD
5814            || path.is_absolute()
5815            || Self::placeholder_of(guest, dirfd).is_some()
5816        {
5817            return false;
5818        }
5819        guest
5820            .thread_state()
5821            .with_detfd(dirfd, |detfd| detfd.path())
5822            .ok()
5823            .flatten()
5824            .is_some_and(|base| {
5825                let mut walked = base.into_os_string().into_vec();
5826                walked.push(b'/');
5827                through_descriptor_link(&walked).is_some()
5828            })
5829    }
5830
5831    /// The absolute guest path an open of `path` relative to `dirfd`, whose
5832    /// guest spelling made absolute is `observed` (`Detcore::observed_path`),
5833    /// reaches where it leads below a placeholder directory Detcore serves: a
5834    /// name relative to a placeholder descriptor, or a path through the
5835    /// caller's descriptor links to one ([`Self::decided_path`]). `None` for
5836    /// any other path, which names what it spells.
5837    pub(super) fn path_below_descriptor_directory<G: Guest<Self>>(
5838        guest: &G,
5839        dirfd: RawFd,
5840        path: &Path,
5841        observed: &Path,
5842    ) -> Option<PathBuf> {
5843        Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest)?;
5844        if Self::starts_at_a_recorded_link(guest, dirfd, path) {
5845            return None;
5846        }
5847        let relative_below = !path.is_absolute()
5848            && !path.as_os_str().is_empty()
5849            && Self::placeholder_of(guest, dirfd).is_some();
5850        let (decided, _) = Self::decided_path(guest, observed).ok()?;
5851        (relative_below || decided.as_path() != observed).then_some(decided)
5852    }
5853
5854    /// The absolute guest `path` as Detcore decides about it (which
5855    /// directory an open, a `stat` or a `readlink` names): where it leads
5856    /// through the caller's descriptor links to placeholder directories,
5857    /// below each directory's canonical spelling (`/proc/<pid>/fd/rest`,
5858    /// `/proc/rest`), as Linux resolves it ([`Self::through_own_links`]);
5859    /// `path` itself otherwise; with the symbolic links Linux follows on the
5860    /// way (the selector links before the first descriptor link,
5861    /// [`selector_links`], and each descriptor link), or `ELOOP` where they
5862    /// pass `MAXSYMLINKS`. The canonical spellings it walks through name
5863    /// tasks by number, so only the guest's own spelling has selector links
5864    /// to count. Whether a binding is stale is left to the injection of the
5865    /// call, which respells the same path.
5866    pub(super) fn decided_path<G: Guest<Self>>(
5867        guest: &G,
5868        path: &Path,
5869    ) -> Result<(PathBuf, usize), Errno> {
5870        let mut bytes = path.as_os_str().as_bytes().to_vec();
5871        let mut links = 0;
5872        let mut first = true;
5873        while let Some((link, rest)) = through_descriptor_link(&bytes) {
5874            let Some(directory) = Self::own_link_directory(guest, &link) else {
5875                break;
5876            };
5877            if first {
5878                links += selector_links(&link);
5879                first = false;
5880            }
5881            links += 1;
5882            if links > LINK_LIMIT {
5883                return Err(Errno::ELOOP);
5884            }
5885            bytes = below(&directory.canonical(), &rest);
5886        }
5887        Ok((PathBuf::from(std::ffi::OsString::from_vec(bytes)), links))
5888    }
5889
5890    /// The absolute guest path `bytes` where it walks through one of the
5891    /// caller's descriptor links to a directory Detcore serves on a
5892    /// placeholder, as Linux follows each such link (`/proc/self/fd/D/N`,
5893    /// with descriptor `D` open on `/proc/self/fd`, is `/proc/<pid>/fd/N`;
5894    /// [`Self::readlink_walk`]), where Detcore serves the caller's own task
5895    /// directories ([`Self::serves_task_directories`], package K slice K10):
5896    /// there no backend directory stands behind the link for the rest of
5897    /// the walk. `Ok(None)` where the path walks through none of them, or
5898    /// Detcore does not serve them; `ELOOP` past the lookup's link budget.
5899    pub(crate) fn through_own_descriptor_links<G: Guest<Self>>(
5900        guest: &G,
5901        bytes: &[u8],
5902    ) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, Errno> {
5903        if !Self::serves_task_directories(guest) {
5904            return Ok(None);
5905        }
5906        let (path, hops, _) = Self::readlink_walk(guest, bytes)?;
5907        Ok((hops > 0).then_some(path))
5908    }
5909
5910    /// The absolute guest path `bytes` of a `readlink` with each of the
5911    /// caller's descriptor links to a placeholder directory it walks through
5912    /// replaced by the directory's canonical spelling (`/proc/<pid>/fd`,
5913    /// `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/fd`, `/proc`), how many it replaced, and how
5914    /// many symbolic links Linux's walk follows for the part replaced (the
5915    /// count below, without the links of the part still spelled), which a
5916    /// walk of the rest adds to.
5917    ///
5918    /// `ELOOP` where the symbolic links Linux follows for the whole lookup
5919    /// pass `MAXSYMLINKS` (40; `reserve_stack` and `pick_link`, fs/namei.c),
5920    /// counted across every replacement: each descriptor link followed, the
5921    /// selector links each is spelled through ([`selector_links`]: the
5922    /// guest's own `/dev/fd` and `self` or `thread-self`, and every `self` a
5923    /// replaced `/proc` exposes below it, `/proc/self/fd/P/self/fd/N`),
5924    /// those of the descriptor link the walk ends at, which `readlink` does
5925    /// not follow, and that link itself where a final `/` or `/.` follows it
5926    /// (`lookup_last` follows it then; a `.` makes it no last component) and
5927    /// the caller's table holds its descriptor
5928    /// ([`Self::holds_own_descriptor`]): an absent entry is `ENOENT`, never
5929    /// one link too many. A `..` right after a replaced link climbs out of
5930    /// the directory's canonical spelling ([`below_climbed`]), so the walk
5931    /// goes on from where Linux's does (`/proc/self/fd/D/../fd/N`).
5932    /// Unlike [`Self::decided_path`], which counts the selectors of the
5933    /// first link only (the stat and open respelling's undercount,
5934    /// <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/5133>).
5935    fn readlink_walk<G: Guest<Self>>(
5936        guest: &G,
5937        bytes: &[u8],
5938    ) -> Result<(Vec<u8>, usize, usize), Errno> {
5939        let mut path = bytes.to_vec();
5940        let mut links = 0;
5941        let mut hops = 0;
5942        while let Some((link, rest)) = through_descriptor_link(&path) {
5943            let Some(directory) = Self::own_link_directory(guest, &link) else {
5944                break;
5945            };
5946            links += selector_links(&link) + 1;
5947            if links > LINK_LIMIT {
5948                return Err(Errno::ELOOP);
5949            }
5950            hops += 1;
5951            path = below_climbed(&directory.canonical(), &rest);
5952        }
5953        // The links of the part replaced; what follows charges the links of
5954        // the part still spelled in `path`, which a walk of it counts again.
5955        let replaced = links;
5956        if let Some((link, end)) = descriptor_link_prefix(&path) {
5957            links += selector_links(&link);
5958            if links > LINK_LIMIT {
5959                return Err(Errno::ELOOP);
5960            }
5961            let after = &path[end..];
5962            let followed = !after.is_empty()
5963                && after
5964                    .split(|byte| *byte == b'/')
5965                    .all(|name| name.is_empty() || name == b".");
5966            if followed && Self::holds_own_descriptor(guest, &link) {
5967                links += 1;
5968                if links > LINK_LIMIT {
5969                    return Err(Errno::ELOOP);
5970                }
5971            }
5972        }
5973        Ok((path, hops, replaced))
5974    }
5975
5976    /// Whether the descriptor link `link` (`/proc/<pid>/fd/N`, spelled as
5977    /// [`descriptor_link_prefix`] spells it) names a descriptor the caller's
5978    /// own table holds, as Detcore keeps that table: the entry Linux's walk
5979    /// finds (`proc_lookupfd_common`, fs/proc/fd.c) before it follows the
5980    /// link. Linux charges `MAXSYMLINKS` only for a symbolic link that
5981    /// exists and that it follows (`step_into`, then `pick_link` and
5982    /// `reserve_stack`, fs/namei.c): a closed descriptor or a name that is no
5983    /// number is `ENOENT`, whatever the walk has followed before. `false`
5984    /// also for a table Detcore does not keep for the caller (another
5985    /// task's, or the process's read by a thread that does not hold it),
5986    /// whose entry it cannot establish, so it charges no follow for it.
5987    fn holds_own_descriptor<G: Guest<Self>>(guest: &G, link: &Path) -> bool {
5988        let Some(own) = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest) else {
5989            return false;
5990        };
5991        let Some(descriptor) = crate::syscalls::namespace::own_descriptor_link(link, Some(own))
5992        else {
5993            return false;
5994        };
5995        let state = guest.thread_state();
5996        if descriptor.table == crate::syscalls::namespace::DescriptorTable::Process
5997            && state.dettid.as_raw() != own.0
5998            && !state.holds_process_files()
5999        {
6000            return false;
6001        }
6002        state.with_detfd(descriptor.fd, |_| ()).is_ok()
6003    }
6004
6005    /// Answer a `readlink` of the absolute guest path `path` into the buffer
6006    /// `buf` of `buf_len` bytes if it is a descriptor link of the caller's to
6007    /// a placeholder directory ([`Self::own_link_directory`]): the directory's
6008    /// path (`/proc/<pid>/fd`, which `proc_fd_link` reads, or `/proc`),
6009    /// truncated to the buffer and without a NUL, as `do_readlinkat` copies
6010    /// it. The placeholder's own path never reaches the guest. The walk to
6011    /// the link keeps the lookup's whole link budget ([`Self::readlink_walk`]).
6012    pub(crate) async fn answer_descriptor_directory_link<G: Guest<Self>>(
6013        &self,
6014        guest: &mut G,
6015        path: &Path,
6016        buf: Option<AddrMut<'_, libc::c_char>>,
6017        buf_len: usize,
6018    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
6019        if !self.serves_descriptor_directories(guest) {
6020            return None;
6021        }
6022        let decided = match Self::readlink_walk(guest, path.as_os_str().as_bytes()) {
6023            Ok((decided, _, _)) => PathBuf::from(std::ffi::OsString::from_vec(decided)),
6024            Err(errno) => return Some(Err(errno.into())),
6025        };
6026        let target = Self::own_link_directory(guest, &decided)?.canonical();
6027        let size = match crate::syscalls::namespace::readlink_size(buf_len) {
6028            Ok(size) => size,
6029            Err(errno) => return Some(Err(errno.into())),
6030        };
6031        let bytes = target.as_os_str().as_bytes();
6032        let length = bytes.len().min(size);
6033        let Some(buf) = buf else {
6034            return Some(Err(Errno::EFAULT.into()));
6035        };
6036        Some(
6037            guest
6038                .memory()
6039                .write_exact(buf.cast::<u8>(), &bytes[..length])
6040                .map(|()| length as i64)
6041                .map_err(Into::into),
6042        )
6043    }
6044
6045    /// Answer a `readlink` of `original`, the guest's own pathname, spelled
6046    /// absolutely as `spelled` ([`Self::answer_descriptor_directory_link`]'s
6047    /// path), where it reaches one of the system-wide procfs directories of
6048    /// the `--proc` level (`/proc/sys`, `/proc/sys/kernel/random`): no link,
6049    /// so EINVAL, as Linux's `do_readlinkat` answers for any object that is
6050    /// not one. Only where the guest's procfs is not the kernel's
6051    /// ([`Self::serves_task_directories`]): KVM's executor serves no procfs
6052    /// directory and refuses every procfs object with EACCES, which procps
6053    /// 4's `sysctl` takes for a refusal when it checks each component of a
6054    /// key's path.
6055    ///
6056    /// Which directories it answers for is [`Self::system_directory_reached`]'s,
6057    /// without following a final symbolic link, as `readlink` does not. In
6058    /// the kernel's order: a pathname of `PATH_MAX` bytes or more is
6059    /// refused (ENAMETOOLONG, `getname_flags`) before any lookup, so it is
6060    /// left to the kernel's path; and the lookup keeps one `MAXSYMLINKS`
6061    /// budget across the descriptor links [`Self::readlink_walk`] replaced
6062    /// and the rest of the walk, which Detcore walks and counts itself
6063    /// (ELOOP past it). And the answer is decided by the object the
6064    /// lookup reaches in the guest's mount namespace, which the tool shares,
6065    /// and by the kernel's name for it
6066    /// (`rendered_procfs::procfs_directory_reached`), never by the spelling,
6067    /// as [`Self::own_link_directory`] answers from the descriptor's own
6068    /// identity: a user mount over part of `/proc` (`--mount
6069    /// ...,target=/proc/sys`) leaves its own objects, links included, to the
6070    /// kernel, and a spelling through a symbolic link to `/proc/sys` reaches
6071    /// the procfs directory it names.
6072    pub(crate) fn answer_system_directory_link<G: Guest<Self>>(
6073        &self,
6074        guest: &G,
6075        original: &Path,
6076        spelled: &Path,
6077    ) -> Option<Result<i64, Error>> {
6078        if !Self::serves_task_directories(guest) {
6079            return None;
6080        }
6081        let (decided, _, links) =
6082            Self::readlink_walk(guest, spelled.as_os_str().as_bytes()).ok()?;
6083        let decided = PathBuf::from(std::ffi::OsString::from_vec(decided));
6084        match self.system_directory_reached(guest, original, &decided, links, false) {
6085            Ok(Some(_)) => Some(Err(Errno::EINVAL.into())),
6086            Ok(None) => None,
6087            Err(errno) => Some(Err(errno.into())),
6088        }
6089    }
6090
6091    /// The system-wide procfs directory of the `--proc` level
6092    /// ([`SystemDirectory`]: `/proc/sys`, `/proc/sys/kernel/random`, ...)
6093    /// that a lookup of the absolute guest path `decided` reaches, or `/proc`
6094    /// itself where the lookup climbs back to it from one
6095    /// (`/proc/sys/..`, which `names_procfs_root` leaves to the kernel's
6096    /// walk), as the placeholder directory Detcore serves for it, following
6097    /// a final symbolic link where `follow` says so, where Detcore serves
6098    /// these directories on placeholders ([`Self::serves_task_directories`]:
6099    /// KVM, whose executor serves no procfs directory and refuses every
6100    /// procfs object with EACCES), for a call whose own pathname is
6101    /// `original`. Where the guest's procfs is the kernel's (ptrace) the
6102    /// kernel serves them, and Detcore lists them from its model
6103    /// (`Detcore::modeled_directory_snapshot`), from the same table.
6104    ///
6105    /// In the kernel's order: a pathname of `PATH_MAX` bytes or more is
6106    /// refused (ENAMETOOLONG, `getname_flags`) before any lookup, so it is
6107    /// left to the backend. The rest is decided by the object the lookup
6108    /// reaches in the guest's mount namespace, which the tool shares, and by
6109    /// the procfs mount it was reached through: that mount's root within
6110    /// procfs and the path below its mount point
6111    /// (`rendered_procfs::procfs_directory_reached`, by ptrace's
6112    /// `procfs_inode::path_within`), never by the spelling, as
6113    /// [`Self::own_link_directory`] answers from the descriptor's own
6114    /// identity: a user mount over part of `/proc` (`--mount
6115    /// ...,target=/proc/sys`) keeps its own objects, a bind mount of
6116    /// `/proc/sys/vm` over `/proc/sys/kernel` or onto a directory outside
6117    /// `/proc` is `/proc/sys/vm`, and a spelling through a symbolic link to
6118    /// `/proc/sys` reaches the procfs directory it names. `links` counts the symbolic links the guest's lookup followed
6119    /// before it reached `decided` (the descriptor links Detcore replaced,
6120    /// [`Self::decided_path`], [`Self::readlink_walk`]), so that one
6121    /// `MAXSYMLINKS` budget covers the whole lookup; an `Err` is the lookup's
6122    /// own failure (ELOOP past that budget), the guest's answer.
6123    ///
6124    /// Detcore walks the path itself, counting every link
6125    /// (`rendered_procfs::procfs_directory_reached`, which never follows a
6126    /// final link; a call that follows it walks the path with a final `/`,
6127    /// which the walk follows as the trailing link, `WALK_TRAILING`, with
6128    /// `pick_link`'s checks, as Linux follows the last name of a lookup with
6129    /// `LOOKUP_FOLLOW`; the `LOOKUP_DIRECTORY` that a `/` adds refuses only
6130    /// what is no directory, which is never served here). Where no
6131    /// descriptor link was replaced, one kernel `stat` of the path decides
6132    /// first whether it can reach a procfs directory at all, so that the
6133    /// lookups that cannot, nearly every one, cost the tool one call rather
6134    /// than one per name: a kernel lookup can only count more links than
6135    /// Linux's walk of the same path (`walk_counting_links`), never fewer, so
6136    /// the object it reaches is the walk's, and only its ELOOP is left to the
6137    /// walk to decide. Every other refusal of that stat (`fs.protected_symlinks`
6138    /// for a followed final link, a security module's) is the tool's kernel
6139    /// lookup of the same path, which is what the walk itself falls back to
6140    /// in exactly those cases (`procfs_directory_reached`'s `kernel_lookup`),
6141    /// so it decides nothing the walk would decide otherwise.
6142    pub(crate) fn system_directory_reached<G: Guest<Self>>(
6143        &self,
6144        guest: &G,
6145        original: &Path,
6146        decided: &Path,
6147        links: usize,
6148        follow: bool,
6149    ) -> Result<Option<PlaceholderDirectory>, Errno> {
6150        if original.as_os_str().len() >= libc::PATH_MAX as usize
6151            || !Self::serves_task_directories(guest)
6152        {
6153            return Ok(None);
6154        }
6155        if links == 0 {
6156            match super::rendered_procfs::kernel_stat(decided, follow) {
6157                Ok(stat)
6158                    if stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT == libc::S_IFDIR
6159                        && super::rendered_procfs::proc_device() == Some(stat.st_dev) => {}
6160                Err(Errno::ELOOP) => {}
6161                _ => return Ok(None),
6162            }
6163        }
6164        let walked = if follow {
6165            let mut bytes = decided.as_os_str().as_bytes().to_vec();
6166            bytes.push(b'/');
6167            PathBuf::from(std::ffi::OsString::from_vec(bytes))
6168        } else {
6169            decided.to_path_buf()
6170        };
6171        let Some(reached) = super::rendered_procfs::procfs_directory_reached(&walked, links)?
6172        else {
6173            return Ok(None);
6174        };
6175        if reached.name() == Path::new("/proc") {
6176            return Ok(Some(PlaceholderDirectory::Root));
6177        }
6178        Ok(
6179            SystemDirectory::named(reached.name(), self.cfg.proc_level).map(|directory| {
6180                PlaceholderDirectory::System(SystemPlaceholder {
6181                    directory,
6182                    object: self.system_object(&reached, directory),
6183                })
6184            }),
6185        )
6186    }
6187
6188    /// The facts of the procfs directory object `reached`, which a lookup
6189    /// reached and named `directory` ([`SystemObject`]), read from the
6190    /// object itself: its metadata from its own descriptor, and which of the
6191    /// names the level table lists in it the object lacks, by Linux's lookup
6192    /// of each in it. Never by looking the directory's name up again, which
6193    /// can reach another object (a mount over the name).
6194    fn system_object(
6195        &self,
6196        reached: &super::rendered_procfs::ReachedDirectory,
6197        directory: SystemDirectory,
6198    ) -> SystemObject {
6199        use crate::procfs::listing;
6200        let names = listing::entries(
6201            &directory.modeled(),
6202            self.cfg.proc_level,
6203            &listing::ListingFacts::default(),
6204        )
6205        .unwrap_or_default();
6206        let absent = names
6207            .iter()
6208            .enumerate()
6209            .filter(|(index, (name, _))| {
6210                *index < 64 && name != b"." && name != b".." && reached.lacks(name)
6211            })
6212            .fold(0_u64, |absent, (index, _)| absent | 1 << index);
6213        let stat = reached.stat();
6214        // st_nlink is u64 on x86_64 and u32 on aarch64.
6215        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
6216        let nlink = stat.st_nlink;
6217        #[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]
6218        let nlink = u64::from(stat.st_nlink);
6219        SystemObject {
6220            mode: stat.st_mode,
6221            nlink,
6222            uid: stat.st_uid,
6223            gid: stat.st_gid,
6224            size: stat.st_size,
6225            blocks: stat.st_blocks,
6226            absent,
6227        }
6228    }
6229
6230    /// The system-wide procfs directory an open of `observed`, the guest's
6231    /// absolute spelling of its pathname `original`, names, if Detcore
6232    /// serves it ([`Self::system_directory_reached`], through the caller's
6233    /// descriptor links to placeholders as [`Self::decided_path`] resolves
6234    /// them; following the last component unless the open opens a link
6235    /// itself, `opens_the_link_itself`). As for every directory Detcore
6236    /// serves ([`Self::descriptor_directory_opened`]), the flags
6237    /// `build_open_flags` refuses are the answer first; `open_placeholder`
6238    /// checks the rest.
6239    pub(super) fn system_directory_opened<G: Guest<Self>>(
6240        &self,
6241        guest: &G,
6242        original: &Path,
6243        observed: &Path,
6244        flags: OFlag,
6245    ) -> Option<Result<OpenedDirectory, Errno>> {
6246        if !Self::serves_task_directories(guest) {
6247            return None;
6248        }
6249        let (decided, links) = Self::decided_path(guest, observed).ok()?;
6250        let follow = !super::opens_the_link_itself(observed, flags);
6251        let reached = self.system_directory_reached(guest, original, &decided, links, follow);
6252        let opened = match reached {
6253            Ok(Some(PlaceholderDirectory::System(directory))) => {
6254                Ok(OpenedDirectory::System(directory))
6255            }
6256            Ok(Some(PlaceholderDirectory::Root)) => Ok(OpenedDirectory::Root),
6257            Ok(_) => return None,
6258            Err(errno) => Err(errno),
6259        };
6260        Some(match super::rendered_procfs::open_flags_refused(flags) {
6261            Some(errno) => Err(errno),
6262            // `do_tmpfile` asks the mount for write access (`mnt_want_write`)
6263            // before `vfs_tmpfile` checks the directory's permission
6264            // (fs/namei.c v6.17, do_tmpfile): EROFS where the directory was
6265            // reached through a read-only mount, a read-only bind of it
6266            // included. Only for a real `O_TMPFILE` open: with `O_PATH` the
6267            // legacy `open` and `openat` keep only `O_PATH_FLAGS`
6268            // (fs/open.c v6.17, `build_open_how`, which `do_sys_open` applies
6269            // before `build_open_flags`), so `O_TMPFILE` and the access mode
6270            // are dropped and the open is a path-only one.
6271            None => opened.and_then(|opened| {
6272                let tmpfile = matches!(opened, OpenedDirectory::System(_))
6273                    && flags.contains(OFlag::O_TMPFILE)
6274                    && !flags.contains(OFlag::O_PATH);
6275                if tmpfile && super::rendered_procfs::on_read_only_mount(&decided) {
6276                    Err(Errno::EROFS)
6277                } else {
6278                    Ok(opened)
6279                }
6280            }),
6281        })
6282    }
6283
6284    /// Whether the absolute guest path `observed`, followed, names a
6285    /// placeholder directory Detcore serves: `/proc` by any spelling (through
6286    /// tasks that exist, [`NamedTask`]), one of the caller's own descriptor
6287    /// directories, a descriptor link of the caller's to a placeholder, or a
6288    /// system-wide directory ([`Self::system_directory_reached`], for the
6289    /// guest's own pathname `original`), as `statfs` reaches it.
6290    pub(super) async fn names_placeholder_directory<G: Guest<Self>>(
6291        &self,
6292        guest: &mut G,
6293        original: &Path,
6294        observed: &Path,
6295    ) -> bool {
6296        let Some(own) = Self::descriptor_directory_caller(guest) else {
6297            return false;
6298        };
6299        let Ok((decided, links)) = Self::decided_path(guest, observed) else {
6300            return false;
6301        };
6302        if fd_directory::classify(&decided, own).is_some()
6303            || Self::own_link_directory(guest, &decided).is_some()
6304        {
6305            return true;
6306        }
6307        match names_procfs_root(&decided) {
6308            Some(tasks) => Self::named_tasks_exist(guest, &tasks).await,
6309            // A system-wide directory, by the object the lookup reaches
6310            // from the guest's own pathname `original`.
6311            None => matches!(
6312                self.system_directory_reached(guest, original, &decided, links, true),
6313                Ok(Some(_))
6314            ),
6315        }
6316    }
6317
6318    /// The absolute spelling of `path` looked up from `dirfd` where `dirfd`
6319    /// is a placeholder descriptor ([`below`] its lookup base), for the
6320    /// `readlinkat` of a name relative to one (`readlinkat(proc, "self/fd/D")`),
6321    /// whose link Detcore answers by its absolute spelling
6322    /// ([`Self::answer_descriptor_directory_link`]). `None` for any other
6323    /// `dirfd`, or an absolute or empty `path`.
6324    pub(crate) fn spelling_below_placeholder<G: Guest<Self>>(
6325        guest: &G,
6326        dirfd: RawFd,
6327        path: &Path,
6328    ) -> Option<PathBuf> {
6329        if !Self::serves_placeholders(guest) || path.is_absolute() || path.as_os_str().is_empty() {
6330            return None;
6331        }
6332        let caller = Self::caller_numbers(guest)?;
6333        let state = Self::placeholder_of(guest, dirfd)?;
6334        Some(PathBuf::from(std::ffi::OsString::from_vec(below(
6335            &state.directory.lookup_base(caller),
6336            path.as_os_str().as_bytes(),
6337        ))))
6338    }
6339}
6340
6341/// The path of the pathname `AF_UNIX` address at `address`, `length` bytes
6342/// long, as `unix_mkname_bsd` takes it: the bytes of `sun_path` up to its
6343/// first NUL. `None` for no address, another family, an abstract address
6344/// (a NUL first) or an unnamed one, or one that cannot be read.
6345fn unix_address_path<M: MemoryAccess>(
6346    memory: &M,
6347    address: usize,
6348    length: usize,
6349) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
6350    let family_size = std::mem::size_of::<libc::sa_family_t>();
6351    let length = length.min(std::mem::size_of::<libc::sockaddr_un>());
6352    if address == 0 || length <= family_size {
6353        return None;
6354    }
6355    let mut bytes = vec![0u8; length];
6356    memory
6357        .read_exact(
6358            reverie::syscalls::Addr::<u8>::from_raw(address)?,
6359            &mut bytes,
6360        )
6361        .ok()?;
6362    let family = u16::from_ne_bytes([bytes[0], bytes[1]]);
6363    if family != libc::AF_UNIX as u16 || bytes[family_size] == 0 {
6364        return None;
6365    }
6366    let path = &bytes[family_size..];
6367    let end = path
6368        .iter()
6369        .position(|byte| *byte == 0)
6370        .unwrap_or(path.len());
6371    Some(path[..end].to_vec())
6372}
6373
6374/// The pathname `AF_UNIX` addresses of the `count` message headers at
6375/// `headers` (`struct msghdr`, or `struct mmsghdr` every `stride` bytes),
6376/// each its `msg_name` of `msg_namelen` bytes ([`unix_address_path`]), up to
6377/// `UIO_MAXIOV` headers, as `sendmmsg` takes at most. A header that cannot
6378/// be read ends the list.
6379fn message_addresses<M: MemoryAccess>(
6380    memory: &M,
6381    headers: usize,
6382    count: usize,
6383    stride: usize,
6384) -> Vec<Vec<u8>> {
6385    const UIO_MAXIOV: usize = 1024;
6386    let mut paths = Vec::new();
6387    for index in 0..count.min(UIO_MAXIOV) {
6388        let Some(header) =
6389            reverie::syscalls::Addr::<[u64; 2]>::from_raw(headers.wrapping_add(index * stride))
6390        else {
6391            break;
6392        };
6393        let Ok([name, length]) = memory.read_value(header) else {
6394            break;
6395        };
6396        if let Some(path) = unix_address_path(memory, name as usize, length as u32 as usize) {
6397            paths.push(path);
6398        }
6399    }
6400    paths
6401}
6402
6403/// Whether x86-64's `access_ok(address, size)` accepts a user range of a
6404/// size not known at compile time (`__access_ok`, v6.17
6405/// `arch/x86/include/asm/uaccess_64.h`): its end, `address + size` modulo
6406/// 2^64, neither wraps below `address` nor passes `USER_PTR_MAX`, which this
6407/// host's paging mode sets (`threads::user_ptr_max`, measured once).
6408fn access_ok(address: usize, size: usize) -> bool {
6409    let end = address.wrapping_add(size);
6410    end >= address && end <= crate::syscalls::threads::user_ptr_max()
6411}
6412
6413/// A read of a directory (`read`, `readv`, `pread64`, `preadv`, `preadv2`),
6414/// in Linux's order of checks (v6.17 `fs/read_write.c`, `lib/iov_iter.c`),
6415/// after the descriptor lookup and a negative position's `EINVAL`
6416/// ([`Detcore::answer_placeholder_call`]): for `read` and `pread64` the
6417/// buffer's `access_ok` (`EFAULT`, which also refuses every count past
6418/// `SSIZE_MAX`) and a position whose end overflows (`EINVAL`); for the
6419/// vectored reads `import_iovec` (more than `UIO_MAXIOV` vectors `EINVAL`
6420/// before the array is read, an unreadable array `EFAULT`, a length past
6421/// `SSIZE_MAX` `EINVAL`, a vector `access_ok` refuses `EFAULT`, a lone
6422/// vector's length clamped to `MAX_RW_COUNT` first), nothing to read (0),
6423/// and a `preadv2` flag the loop does not take (`EOPNOTSUPP`); then
6424/// `generic_read_dir`'s `EISDIR`, also for a count of 0.
6425fn read_answer<M: MemoryAccess>(
6426    memory: &M,
6427    sysno: Sysno,
6428    args: &syscalls::SyscallArgs,
6429) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
6430    const UIO_MAXIOV: usize = 1024;
6431    const RWF_HIPRI: u32 = 1;
6432    const MAX_RW_COUNT: usize = 0x7fff_f000;
6433    let position_overflows =
6434        |position: i64, count: usize| position.checked_add(count as i64).is_none_or(|end| end < 0);
6435    match sysno {
6436        Sysno::read | Sysno::pread64 => {
6437            let count = args.arg2;
6438            if !access_ok(args.arg1, count) {
6439                return Err(Errno::EFAULT);
6440            }
6441            if sysno == Sysno::pread64 && position_overflows(args.arg3 as i64, count) {
6442                return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
6443            }
6444            Err(Errno::EISDIR)
6445        }
6446        _ => {
6447            // `import_iovec` takes the count as an `unsigned int`.
6448            let count = args.arg2 as u32 as usize;
6449            if count > UIO_MAXIOV {
6450                return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
6451            }
6452            let mut total: usize = 0;
6453            for index in 0..count {
6454                let Some(vector) = reverie::syscalls::Addr::<[u64; 2]>::from_raw(
6455                    args.arg1.wrapping_add(index * 16),
6456                ) else {
6457                    return Err(Errno::EFAULT);
6458                };
6459                let [base, length] = memory.read_value(vector).map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)?;
6460                if i64::try_from(length).is_err() {
6461                    return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
6462                }
6463                let length = if count == 1 {
6464                    (length as usize).min(MAX_RW_COUNT)
6465                } else {
6466                    length as usize
6467                };
6468                if !access_ok(base as usize, length) {
6469                    return Err(Errno::EFAULT);
6470                }
6471                total = total.saturating_add(length);
6472            }
6473            if total == 0 {
6474                return Ok(0);
6475            }
6476            if sysno != Sysno::readv
6477                && !(sysno == Sysno::preadv2 && args.arg3 as i64 == -1)
