6478                && position_overflows(args.arg3 as i64, total)
6479            {
6480                return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
6481            }
6482            // The flags are an `rwf_t`, an `int`: Linux reads the register's
6483            // low 32 bits.
6484            if sysno == Sysno::preadv2 && args.arg5 as u32 & !RWF_HIPRI != 0 {
6485                return Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP);
6486            }
6487            Err(Errno::EISDIR)
6488        }
6489    }
6490}
6491
6492/// The arguments Linux reads before an extended-attribute call through a
6493/// descriptor looks the descriptor up, and the error the first that fails
6494/// gives (v6.17 `fs/xattr.c`): for `fsetxattr` the flags (`EINVAL`), the
6495/// name, the size (`E2BIG` past `XATTR_SIZE_MAX`) and the value (`EFAULT`);
6496/// for `fgetxattr` and `fremovexattr` the name: an unreadable one `EFAULT`,
6497/// an empty one or one of 256 bytes or more `ERANGE` (`import_xattr_name`).
6498fn xattr_arguments_answer<M: MemoryAccess>(memory: &M, call: &Syscall) -> Result<(), Errno> {
6499    const XATTR_NAME_MAX: usize = 255;
6500    const XATTR_SIZE_MAX: usize = 65536;
6501    let (sysno, args) = call.into_parts();
6502    let name = |address: usize| -> Result<(), Errno> {
6503        let mut bytes = Vec::with_capacity(XATTR_NAME_MAX + 1);
6504        for offset in 0..=XATTR_NAME_MAX {
6505            let byte = reverie::syscalls::Addr::<u8>::from_raw(address.wrapping_add(offset))
6506                .ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)
6507                .and_then(|address| memory.read_value(address).map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT))?;
6508            if byte == 0 {
6509                break;
6510            }
6511            bytes.push(byte);
6512        }
6513        if bytes.is_empty() || bytes.len() > XATTR_NAME_MAX {
6514            return Err(Errno::ERANGE);
6515        }
6516        Ok(())
6517    };
6518    match sysno {
6519        Sysno::fsetxattr => {
6520            if args.arg4 as i32 & !(libc::XATTR_CREATE | libc::XATTR_REPLACE) != 0 {
6521                return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
6522            }
6523            name(args.arg1)?;
6524            let size = args.arg3;
6525            if size > XATTR_SIZE_MAX {
6526                return Err(Errno::E2BIG);
6527            }
6528            if size > 0 {
6529                let mut value = vec![0u8; size];
6530                reverie::syscalls::Addr::<u8>::from_raw(args.arg2)
6531                    .ok_or(Errno::EFAULT)
6532                    .and_then(|address| {
6533                        memory
6534                            .read_exact(address, &mut value)
6535                            .map_err(|_| Errno::EFAULT)
6536                    })?;
6537            }
6538            Ok(())
6539        }
6540        Sysno::fgetxattr | Sysno::fremovexattr => name(args.arg1),
6541        _ => Ok(()),
6542    }
6543}

6545/// What Linux's checks of a transfer need of its other descriptor
6546/// ([`Detcore::descriptor_facts`]).
6547#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
6548struct DescriptorFacts {
6549    /// Opened for reading (`FMODE_READ`).
6550    readable: bool,
6551    /// Opened for writing (`FMODE_WRITE`).
6552    writable: bool,
6553    /// An `O_PATH` descriptor, which `fdget` refuses.
6554    path_only: bool,
6555    /// `O_APPEND`.
6556    append: bool,
6557    /// A pipe.
6558    pipe: bool,
6559    /// On the guest's procfs.
6560    on_procfs: bool,
6561    /// The placeholder state, for a placeholder.
6562    placeholder: Option<PlaceholderState>,
6563}
6564
6565/// procfs's largest file offset (`MAX_NON_LFS`, `s_maxbytes`, v6.17
6566/// `fs/proc/root.c`), past which `sendfile` from one of its directories is
6567/// `EOVERFLOW`.
6568const PROCFS_MAX_BYTES: i64 = (1 << 31) - 1;
6569
6570/// The largest write hint `F_SET_RW_HINT` takes (`RWH_WRITE_LIFE_EXTREME`).
6571const RWH_WRITE_LIFE_EXTREME: u64 = 5;
6572
6573/// Whether the caller may act as the owner of the directory `directory`
6574/// stands for (`inode_owner_or_capable`), which `O_NOATIME`, `F_SETFL`'s
6575/// `O_NOATIME` and `F_SET_RW_HINT` require. Detcore takes the caller to be
6576/// the guest's root, with its capabilities in the guest's user namespace,
6577/// as it renders every live task's files as owned by it
6578/// (`rendered_file_owner`, named approximation): a task's descriptor
6579/// directory is its own; `/proc` is global root's, whom the caller may act
6580/// as only where the guest's user namespace maps it, as the guest's own stat
6581/// of `/proc` shows (`guest_procfs_root`: an unmapped owner reads as the
6582/// overflow ID, which no mapped caller has); a system-wide directory is its
6583/// owner's as the kernel reports it in the guest's user namespace
6584/// (`SystemObject`, from the object the lookup reached: global root, or the root of the
6585/// network namespace's owner for `/proc/sys/net`).
6586fn placeholder_owner_or_capable(directory: &PlaceholderDirectory) -> Result<bool, Errno> {
6587    const OVERFLOW_ID: u32 = 65534;
6588    match directory {
6589        PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(_) | PlaceholderDirectory::Task(_) => Ok(true),
6590        PlaceholderDirectory::Root => {
6591            let (_, uid, gid) = super::rendered_procfs::guest_procfs_root()?;
6592            Ok(uid != OVERFLOW_ID && gid != OVERFLOW_ID)
6593        }
6594        PlaceholderDirectory::System(system) => {
6595            Ok(system.object.uid != OVERFLOW_ID && system.object.gid != OVERFLOW_ID)
6596        }
6597    }
6598}
6599
6600/// Whether the caller owns the directory `directory` stands for, as
6601/// `vfs_setlease` asks for a lease (the file's owner is the caller's
6602/// filesystem uid; `CAP_LEASE` counts only in the initial user namespace):
6603/// Detcore takes the caller to be the guest's root, which owns its own task's
6604/// descriptor directory (`rendered_file_owner`), and owns `/proc` only where
6605/// global root is the guest's root, as the guest's own stat of `/proc` shows
6606/// (`guest_procfs_root`: uid 0), and a system-wide directory where its owner
6607/// is (`SystemObject`). Read from the same facts as
6608/// [`placeholder_owner_or_capable`], which also lets a caller act as an owner
6609/// its user namespace maps, as `inode_owner_or_capable` does and
6610/// `vfs_setlease` does not.
6611fn placeholder_lease_owner(directory: &PlaceholderDirectory) -> Result<bool, Errno> {
6612    match directory {
6613        PlaceholderDirectory::Descriptors(_) | PlaceholderDirectory::Task(_) => Ok(true),
6614        PlaceholderDirectory::Root => {
6615            let (_, uid, _) = super::rendered_procfs::guest_procfs_root()?;
6616            Ok(uid == 0)
6617        }
6618        PlaceholderDirectory::System(system) => Ok(system.object.uid == 0),
6619    }
6620}

6622/// The bytes of a sent message's control buffer Detcore reads
6623/// (`syscalls::io::sent_descriptors`).
6624const MAX_SEND_CONTROL_BYTES: usize = 64 * 1024;
6625
6626/// What a `sendmsg` or `sendmmsg` whose `SCM_RIGHTS` descriptors Detcore
6627/// inspected ([`Detcore::inspect_placeholder_send`]) keeps for the attempts
6628/// the internal polling loop makes of it, each of which may come after other
6629/// threads ran (`ThreadState::send_inspection`): the socket, the guest
6630/// addresses of its messages' headers, and the placeholders among the
6631/// descriptors they passed, with their open file descriptions, whose copies
6632/// Detcore counted in flight.
6633#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
6634pub(crate) struct SendInspection {
6635    /// The call, `sendmsg` or `sendmmsg`.
6636    pub(crate) sysno: Sysno,
6637    socket: RawFd,
6638    headers: Vec<usize>,
6639    placeholders: Vec<(RawFd, crate::types::OpenFileId)>,
6640}
6641
6642/// `fallocate` of a directory Linux refuses in `vfs_fallocate`'s order: a
6643/// negative offset or a length that is not positive (`EINVAL`), an unknown
6644/// mode bit or a mode with a flag it does not take (`EOPNOTSUPP`), then a
6645/// description opened without write access (`EBADF`), as every directory's
6646/// is.
6647fn fallocate_answer(mode: i32, offset: i64, length: i64) -> Errno {
6648    const KEEP_SIZE: i32 = 0x01;
6649    const PUNCH_HOLE: i32 = 0x02;
6650    const COLLAPSE_RANGE: i32 = 0x08;
6651    const ZERO_RANGE: i32 = 0x10;
6652    const INSERT_RANGE: i32 = 0x20;
6653    const UNSHARE_RANGE: i32 = 0x40;
6654    const WRITE_ZEROES: i32 = 0x80;
6655    const MODE_MASK: i32 =
6656        PUNCH_HOLE | COLLAPSE_RANGE | ZERO_RANGE | INSERT_RANGE | UNSHARE_RANGE | WRITE_ZEROES;
6657    if offset < 0 || length <= 0 {
6658        return Errno::EINVAL;
6659    }
6660    if mode & !(MODE_MASK | KEEP_SIZE) != 0 {
6661        return Errno::EOPNOTSUPP;
6662    }
6663    match mode & MODE_MASK {
6664        0 | UNSHARE_RANGE | ZERO_RANGE => {}
6665        PUNCH_HOLE if mode & KEEP_SIZE == 0 => return Errno::EOPNOTSUPP,
6666        PUNCH_HOLE => {}
6667        COLLAPSE_RANGE | INSERT_RANGE | WRITE_ZEROES if mode & KEEP_SIZE != 0 => {
6668            return Errno::EOPNOTSUPP;
6669        }
6670        COLLAPSE_RANGE | INSERT_RANGE | WRITE_ZEROES => {}
6671        _ => return Errno::EOPNOTSUPP,
6672    }
6673    Errno::EBADF
6674}
6675
6676/// `fadvise64` of a procfs directory (`generic_fadvise`): a negative length
6677/// is `EINVAL`; procfs has no backing device, so any of the six advices is
6678/// taken and ignored, and any other value is `EINVAL`.
6679fn fadvise_answer(length: i64, advice: i32) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
6680    if length < 0 {
6681        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
6682    }
6683    match advice {
6684        libc::POSIX_FADV_NORMAL
6685        | libc::POSIX_FADV_RANDOM
6686        | libc::POSIX_FADV_SEQUENTIAL
6687        | libc::POSIX_FADV_WILLNEED
6688        | libc::POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED
6689        | libc::POSIX_FADV_NOREUSE => Ok(0),
6690        _ => Err(Errno::EINVAL),
6691    }
6692}
6693
6694/// `sync_file_range` of a directory (`sync_file_range`): an unknown flag, a
6695/// negative offset or end, or an end that wraps is `EINVAL`; a directory
6696/// passes the file type check, and has nothing to write back.
6697fn sync_file_range_answer(offset: i64, bytes: i64, flags: u32) -> Result<i64, Errno> {
6698    const VALID_FLAGS: u32 = 1 | 2 | 4;
6699    if flags & !VALID_FLAGS != 0 {
6700        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
6701    }
6702    let end = offset.wrapping_add(bytes);
6703    if offset < 0 || end < 0 || end < offset {
6704        return Err(Errno::EINVAL);
6705    }
6706    Ok(0)
6707}
6708
6709/// `F_DUPFD_QUERY` (Linux 6.10).
6710const F_DUPFD_QUERY: libc::c_int = 1027;
6711/// `F_CREATED_QUERY` (Linux 6.12).
6712const F_CREATED_QUERY: libc::c_int = 1028;
6713/// `F_GET_RW_HINT`.
6714const F_GET_RW_HINT: libc::c_int = 1035;
6715/// `F_SET_RW_HINT`.
6716const F_SET_RW_HINT: libc::c_int = 1036;
6717/// `F_GETOWNER_UIDS`.
6718const F_GETOWNER_UIDS: libc::c_int = 17;
6719/// `F_SETSIG`.
6720const F_SETSIG: libc::c_int = 10;
6721/// `F_GETSIG`.
6722const F_GETSIG: libc::c_int = 11;
6723/// `F_SETOWN_EX`.
6724const F_SETOWN_EX: libc::c_int = 15;
6725/// `F_GETOWN_EX`.
6726const F_GETOWN_EX: libc::c_int = 16;
6727/// `flock`'s `LOCK_MAND`, which Linux ignores (`SYSCALL_DEFINE2(flock)`).
6728const LOCK_MAND: libc::c_int = 32;
6729
6730#[cfg(test)]
6731mod tests {
6732    use super::*;
6733
6734    #[test]
6735    fn a_name_below_a_directory_keeps_what_linux_resolves() {
6736        let base = Path::new("/proc/self/fd");
6737        let spelled = |relative: &str| String::from_utf8(below(base, relative.as_bytes())).unwrap();
6738        assert_eq!(spelled("3"), "/proc/self/fd/3");
6739        assert_eq!(spelled("3/"), "/proc/self/fd/3/");
6740        assert_eq!(spelled("."), "/proc/self/fd/.");
6741        assert_eq!(spelled("./././."), "/proc/self/fd/.");
6742        assert_eq!(spelled(".//3//./x"), "/proc/self/fd/3/x");
6743        assert_eq!(spelled("3/."), "/proc/self/fd/3/");
6744        assert_eq!(spelled(".."), "/proc/self/fd/..");
6745        // A 4093-byte path of `./` repeats and a final `.`, valid on Linux,
6746        // stays short.
6747        let long = "./".repeat(2046) + ".";
6748        assert_eq!(long.len(), 4093);
6749        assert_eq!(spelled(&long), "/proc/self/fd/.");
6750        // Below `/proc` itself.
6751        let root = |relative: &str| {
6752            String::from_utf8(below(Path::new("/proc"), relative.as_bytes())).unwrap()
6753        };
6754        assert_eq!(root("self/status"), "/proc/self/status");
6755        assert_eq!(root(".."), "/proc/..");
6756        assert_eq!(root("."), "/proc/.");
6757    }
6758
6759    #[test]
6760    fn a_dotdot_after_a_replaced_link_climbs_out_of_its_directory() {
6761        let climbed = |base: &str, relative: &str| {
6762            String::from_utf8(below_climbed(Path::new(base), relative.as_bytes())).unwrap()
6763        };
6764        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc/7/fd", "../fd/3"), "/proc/7/fd/3");
6765        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc/7/fd", "../../self/fd/3"), "/proc/self/fd/3");
6766        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc/7/fd", ".//..//./fd/3/"), "/proc/7/fd/3/");
6767        assert_eq!(
6768            climbed("/proc/7/task/8/fd", "../../8/fd/3"),
6769            "/proc/7/task/8/fd/3"
6770        );
6771        // The root's `..` is the root.
6772        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc", "../../proc/self/fd/3"), "/proc/self/fd/3");
6773        // A `..` after a name, or nothing but `..`, is left as `below` has it.
6774        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc/7/fd", "3/../4"), "/proc/7/fd/3/../4");
6775        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc/7/fd", "../fd/../fd/3"), "/proc/7/fd/../fd/3");
6776        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc/7/fd", ".."), "/proc/7/fd/..");
6777        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc/7/fd", "../.."), "/proc/7/fd/../..");
6778        assert_eq!(climbed("/proc/7/fd", "3"), "/proc/7/fd/3");
6779    }
6780
6781    #[test]
6782    fn a_path_through_a_descriptor_link_is_split_after_the_link() {
6783        let split = |path: &str| {
6784            through_descriptor_link(path.as_bytes())
6785                .map(|(link, rest)| (link, String::from_utf8(rest).unwrap()))
6786        };
6787        assert_eq!(
6788            split("/proc/self/fd/5/3"),
6789            Some((PathBuf::from("/proc/self/fd/5"), "3".to_string()))
6790        );
6791        assert_eq!(
6792            split("/dev/fd/5/3/x"),
6793            Some((PathBuf::from("/dev/fd/5"), "3/x".to_string()))
6794        );
6795        assert_eq!(
6796            split("/proc/7/task/9/fd/5/3"),
6797            Some((PathBuf::from("/proc/7/task/9/fd/5"), "3".to_string()))
6798        );
6799        assert_eq!(split("/proc/self/fd/5"), None);
6800        // A final `/` follows the link: the directory itself.
6801        assert_eq!(
6802            split("/proc/self/fd/5/"),
6803            Some((PathBuf::from("/proc/self/fd/5"), String::new()))
6804        );
6805        assert_eq!(split("/proc/self/status"), None);
6806        assert_eq!(split("/tmp/x/y/z/w"), None);
6807        // Empty and `.` components before the number name nothing.
6808        for spelling in [
6809            "//proc/self/fd/5/3",
6810            "/proc//self/fd/5/3",
6811            "/proc/./self/fd/5/3",
6812            "/proc/self/fd/./5//3",
6813            "/./proc/self//fd/5/3",
6814        ] {
6815            assert_eq!(
6816                split(spelling),
6817                Some((PathBuf::from("/proc/self/fd/5"), "3".to_string())),
6818                "{spelling}"
6819            );
6820        }
6821        // A `..` before the number is not recognized.
6822        assert_eq!(split("/proc/self/../self/fd/5/3"), None);
6823        assert_eq!(split("proc/self/fd/5/3"), None);
6824    }
6825
6826    #[test]
6827    fn the_rest_of_a_path_through_a_link_keeps_its_bytes() {
6828        let path = b"/proc/self/fd/5/7/\xff\xfe/x";
6829        let (link, rest) = through_descriptor_link(path).unwrap();
6830        assert_eq!(link, PathBuf::from("/proc/self/fd/5"));
6831        assert_eq!(rest, b"7/\xff\xfe/x".to_vec());
6832        assert_eq!(
6833            below(Path::new("/proc/self/fd"), &rest),
6834            b"/proc/self/fd/7/\xff\xfe/x".to_vec()
6835        );
6836        // A link component that is not ASCII is no link.
6837        assert_eq!(through_descriptor_link(b"/proc/\xffself/fd/5/3"), None);
6838    }

6840    #[test]
6841    fn the_links_before_a_descriptor_link_are_counted() {
6842        assert_eq!(selector_links(Path::new("/proc/self/fd/5")), 1);
6843        assert_eq!(selector_links(Path::new("/proc/thread-self/fd/5")), 1);
6844        assert_eq!(selector_links(Path::new("/proc/self/task/9/fd/5")), 1);
6845        assert_eq!(selector_links(Path::new("/dev/fd/5")), 2);
6846        assert_eq!(selector_links(Path::new("/proc/7/fd/5")), 0);
6847        assert_eq!(selector_links(Path::new("/proc/7/task/9/fd/5")), 0);
6848    }

6850    /// `/proc` itself by every spelling Linux's walk takes there: trailing
6851    /// and repeated slashes, `.` components, and `..` back from the
6852    /// directories every procfs has below it; nothing else.
6853    #[test]
6854    fn every_spelling_of_proc_itself_is_the_root() {
6855        for spelling in [
6856            "/proc",
6857            "/proc/",
6858            "//proc",
6859            "/proc/.",
6860            "/./proc/./",
6861            "/proc/self/..",
6862            "/proc/self/fd/../..",
6863            "/proc/7/task/9/../../..",
6864            "/proc/thread-self/../../..",
6865            "/proc/self/task/7/fd/../../../..",
6866            "/../proc",
6867            "/proc/../proc",
6868        ] {
6869            assert!(
6870                names_procfs_root(Path::new(spelling)).is_some(),
6871                "{spelling}"
6872            );
6873        }
6874        for spelling in [
6875            "proc",
6876            "/",
6877            "/proc/self",
6878            "/proc/self/fd/..",
6879            "/proc/thread-self/..",
6880            "/proc/sys/..",
6881            "/proc/self/cwd/..",
6882            "/proc/self/fd/3/..",
6883            "/proc/self/status/..",
6884            "/tmp/../proc",
6885            "/proc/07/..",
6886        ] {
6887            assert!(
6888                names_procfs_root(Path::new(spelling)).is_none(),
6889                "{spelling}"
6890            );
6891        }
6892        // A spelling through numbered tasks names `/proc` only while each
6893        // exists: they are reported, in the walk's order.
6894        let task = |process, thread| NamedTask { process, thread };
6895        assert_eq!(names_procfs_root(Path::new("/proc")), Some(Vec::new()));
6896        assert_eq!(
6897            names_procfs_root(Path::new("/proc/7/task/9/../../..")),
6898            Some(vec![task(Some(7), None), task(Some(7), Some(9))])
6899        );
6900        assert_eq!(
6901            names_procfs_root(Path::new("/proc/self/task/9/../../..")),
6902            Some(vec![task(None, Some(9))])
6903        );
6904        // A number past any `pid_t` names no task (0).
6905        assert_eq!(
6906            names_procfs_root(Path::new("/proc/2147483648/..")),
6907            Some(vec![task(Some(0), None)])
6908        );
6909    }
6910
6911    /// `..` resolves back over the directories every procfs has, as the
6912    /// kernel's walk does, and not over anything else.
6913    #[test]
6914    fn a_dotdot_resolves_over_procfs_directories_only() {
6915        let resolved = |path: &str| {
6916            resolved_procfs_dotdot(Path::new(path)).map(|(path, _)| path.display().to_string())
6917        };
6918        let tasks = |path: &str| resolved_procfs_dotdot(Path::new(path)).map(|(_, tasks)| tasks);
6919        let task = |process, thread| NamedTask { process, thread };
6920        // A numbered task on the way is looked up there, so the resolution
6921        // holds only while it exists (`Detcore::named_tasks_exist`): it is
6922        // reported, never taken to exist.
6923        assert_eq!(
6924            tasks("/proc/7/fd/../../fs/nfsd"),
6925            Some(vec![task(Some(7), None)])
6926        );
6927        assert_eq!(tasks("/proc/self/../fs"), Some(Vec::new()));
6928        assert_eq!(
6929            tasks("/proc/thread-self/../../../7/.."),
6930            Some(vec![task(Some(7), None)])
6931        );
6932        assert_eq!(resolved("/proc/self/../fs"), Some("/proc/fs".to_string()));
6933        assert_eq!(
6934            resolved("/proc/7/fd/../../fs/nfsd"),
6935            Some("/proc/fs/nfsd".to_string())
6936        );
6937        assert_eq!(
6938            resolved("/proc/thread-self/../stat"),
6939            Some("/proc/self/task/stat".to_string())
6940        );
6941        assert_eq!(
6942            resolved("/proc/thread-self/fd/.."),
6943            Some("/proc/thread-self".to_string())
6944        );
6945        // `fdinfo` is a directory every process has, as `fd` is.
6946        assert_eq!(
6947            resolved("/proc/7/fdinfo/../cmdline"),
6948            Some("/proc/7/cmdline".to_string())
6949        );
6950        assert_eq!(
6951            tasks("/proc/7/fdinfo/../cmdline"),
6952            Some(vec![task(Some(7), None)])
6953        );
6954        assert_eq!(
6955            resolved("/proc/self/task/9/fdinfo/.."),
6956            Some("/proc/self/task/9".to_string())
6957        );
6958        assert_eq!(
6959            resolved("/proc/thread-self/../../.."),
6960            Some("/proc".to_string())
6961        );
6962        assert_eq!(resolved("/../proc/./self/.."), Some("/proc".to_string()));
6963        assert_eq!(resolved("/proc/.."), Some("/".to_string()));
6964        assert_eq!(resolved("/proc/self/fd"), None, "no ..");
6965        assert_eq!(
6966            resolved("/proc/self/cwd/../x"),
6967            None,
6968            "a link leads elsewhere"
6969        );
6970        assert_eq!(resolved("/proc/self/status/.."), None, "a file");
6971        assert_eq!(resolved("/proc/sys/../fs"), None);
6972        assert_eq!(resolved("/tmp/../proc/fs"), None);
6973        assert_eq!(resolved("/proc/self/fd/3/.."), None, "a descriptor link");
6974        assert_eq!(resolved("/proc/self/fdinfo/3/.."), None, "a file");
6975    }
6976
6977    /// A `..` whose walk stays in the caller's own task tree is resolved for
6978    /// the task-directory placeholders, with a final `/` kept where Linux then
6979    /// requires a directory, and the tasks it names reported; one that leaves
6980    /// the tree, or that only the kernel's walk resolves, is not (package K
6981    /// slice K10).
6982    #[test]
6983    fn a_dotdot_within_the_callers_own_tree_is_resolved() {
6984        let own = |path: &str| {
6985            own_tree_dotdot(path.as_bytes(), 7)
6986                .map(|(path, tasks)| (String::from_utf8(path).unwrap(), tasks))
6987        };
6988        let task = |process, thread| NamedTask { process, thread };
6989        // A last `..` reaches a directory, spelled with a final `/`.
6990        assert_eq!(
6991            own("/proc/self/fd/.."),
6992            Some(("/proc/self/".into(), vec![]))
6993        );
6994        assert_eq!(
6995            own("/proc/self/fd/../"),
6996            Some(("/proc/self/".into(), vec![]))
6997        );
6998        assert_eq!(
6999            own("/proc/self/fd/../."),
7000            Some(("/proc/self/".into(), vec![]))
7001        );
7002        assert_eq!(
7003            own("/proc/self/fd/../fd"),
7004            Some(("/proc/self/fd".into(), vec![]))
7005        );
7006        assert_eq!(
7007            own("/proc/thread-self/fd/.."),
7008            Some(("/proc/thread-self/".into(), vec![]))
7009        );
7010        assert_eq!(
7011            own("/proc/thread-self/.."),
7012            Some(("/proc/self/task/".into(), vec![]))
7013        );
7014        assert_eq!(
7015            own("/proc/self/../self"),
7016            Some(("/proc/self".into(), vec![]))
7017        );
7018        assert_eq!(
7019            own("/proc/7/task/9/fdinfo/../.."),
7020            Some((
7021                "/proc/7/task/".into(),
7022                vec![task(Some(7), None), task(Some(7), Some(9))]
7023            ))
7024        );
7025        for path in [
7026            "/proc/self/fd",
7027            "/proc/self/..",
7028            "/proc/8/fd/..",
7029            "/proc/self/fd/../../8",
7030            "/proc/self/status/..",
7031            "/proc/self/fd/3/..",
7032            "/proc/self/cwd/..",
7033            "/tmp/../proc/self",
7034        ] {
7035            assert_eq!(own(path), None, "{path}");
7036        }
7037        // The selector link a spelling ends at, `..` resolved before it,
7038        // with the tasks named on the way; whether the call follows it is
7039        // the caller's to tell.
7040        let link = |path: &str| {
7041            selector_link_reached(Path::new(path))
7042                .map(|(link, tasks)| (link.to_str().unwrap().to_owned(), tasks))
7043        };
7044        for (path, expected) in [
7045            ("/proc/self", "/proc/self"),
7046            ("/proc/thread-self", "/proc/thread-self"),
7047            ("//proc/./self", "/proc/self"),
7048            ("/proc/self/../self", "/proc/self"),
7049            ("/proc/self/fd/../../self", "/proc/self"),
7050            ("/proc/self/../thread-self", "/proc/thread-self"),
7051            (
7052                "/proc/thread-self/../../../thread-self",
7053                "/proc/thread-self",
7054            ),
7055            ("/proc/self/../self/", "/proc/self"),
7056        ] {
7057            assert_eq!(link(path), Some((expected.into(), vec![])), "{path}");
7058        }
7059        assert_eq!(
7060            link("/proc/7/task/9/../../../self"),
7061            Some((
7062                "/proc/self".into(),
7063                vec![task(Some(7), None), task(Some(7), Some(9))]
7064            ))
7065        );
7066        for path in [
7067            "/proc/self/..",
7068            "/proc/self/../self/fd",
7069            "/proc/thread-self/../../self",
7070            "/proc/self/status/../self",
7071            "/proc/selfish",
7072            "/proc/self/task",
7073            // A last `..` lands on a directory, not on a link.
7074            "/proc/self/fd/..",
7075            "/proc/thread-self/../..",
7076            "/proc/self/task/../../self/fd/..",
7077        ] {
7078            assert_eq!(link(path), None, "{path}");
7079        }
7080        assert_eq!(
7081            walked_components(b"//proc/./self/../fd/"),
7082            [b"proc".as_slice(), b"self", b"..", b"fd"]
7083        );
7084        // Which spellings end in a name and a `/`, which an `O_CREAT` open
7085        // refuses with EISDIR before it looks the name up.
7086        for (path, expected) in [
7087            ("/proc/self/", true),
7088            ("/proc/self/../self/", true),
7089            ("/proc//", true),
7090            ("/proc/self", false),
7091            ("/proc/self/.", false),
7092            ("/proc/self/./", false),
7093            ("/proc/self/fd/../", false),
7094            ("/", false),
7095        ] {
7096            assert_eq!(
7097                ends_in_a_name_and_slash(Path::new(path)),
7098                expected,
7099                "{path}"
7100            );
7101        }
7102        // The directory the walk is in when it reaches the last name.
7103        for (path, expected) in [
7104            ("/proc/self/task/9/", Some("/proc/self/task")),
7105            ("/proc/self/task/./9//", Some("/proc/self/task/.")),
7106            ("/proc/self/task/9/../7/", Some("/proc/self/task/9/..")),
7107            ("/proc/", Some("/")),
7108            ("/proc", Some("/")),
7109            ("/", None),
7110        ] {
7111            assert_eq!(
7112                before_the_last_name(path.as_bytes()),
7113                expected.map(str::as_bytes),
7114                "{path}"
7115            );
7116        }
7117    }

7119    /// A spelling in the caller's own task tree loses its empty and `.`
7120    /// components, which name nothing, and keeps a final `/` where Linux
7121    /// then follows the last link; any other spelling, or one already
7122    /// without them, is left as it is (package K slice K10).
7123    #[test]
7124    fn an_own_tree_spelling_loses_the_components_that_name_nothing() {
7125        let normalized = |path: &str| {
7126            own_tree_normalized(path.as_bytes(), 7).map(|path| String::from_utf8(path).unwrap())
7127        };
7128        assert_eq!(normalized("//proc/self"), Some("/proc/self".into()));
7129        assert_eq!(
7130            normalized("/./proc/thread-self"),
7131            Some("/proc/thread-self".into())
7132        );
7133        assert_eq!(normalized("/proc//7/./stat"), Some("/proc/7/stat".into()));
7134        assert_eq!(normalized("//proc/self/."), Some("/proc/self/".into()));
7135        assert_eq!(normalized("/proc/self//"), Some("/proc/self/".into()));
7136        for path in [
7137            "/proc/self",
7138            "/proc/self/",
7139            "/proc/7/task/7",
7140            "//proc/8",
7141            "//proc/meminfo",
7142            "//tmp/self",
7143            "proc//self",
7144        ] {
7145            assert_eq!(normalized(path), None, "{path}");
7146        }
7147    }
7148
7149    /// A directory descriptor is told to be on a placeholder's object from
7150    /// its identity and filesystem alone, with nothing opened: its identity
7151    /// is an object's; or, where the object is procfs's root, its filesystem
7152    /// is procfs at the root inode, and one whose filesystem cannot be told
7153    /// is refused all the same (fail closed); where the object is the
7154    /// placeholder, the identity alone decides.
7155    #[test]
7156    fn a_procfs_root_is_told_without_opening_anything() {
7157        type Tool = Detcore<crate::record_or_replay::NoopTool>;
7158        let proc = reverie::syscalls::linux_abi::PROC_SUPER_MAGIC as i64;
7159        let none = BTreeSet::new();
7160        assert!(Tool::on_placeholder_object((22, 1), Some(Ok(proc)), &none));
7161        assert!(!Tool::on_placeholder_object((22, 2), Some(Ok(proc)), &none));
7162        assert!(!Tool::on_placeholder_object(
7163            (22, 1),
7164            Some(Ok(0x9123683e)),
7165            &none
7166        ));
7167        assert!(Tool::on_placeholder_object(
7168            (22, 5),
7169            Some(Err(Errno::EACCES)),
7170            &none
7171        ));
7172        assert!(Tool::on_placeholder_object(
7173            (22, 5),
7174            Some(Err(Errno::ENOMEM)),
7175            &none
7176        ));
7177        let objects = BTreeSet::from([(22, 5)]);
7178        assert!(Tool::on_placeholder_object(
7179            (22, 5),
7180            Some(Ok(0x9123683e)),
7181            &objects
7182        ));
7183        assert!(Tool::on_placeholder_object((22, 5), None, &objects));
7184        assert!(!Tool::on_placeholder_object((22, 1), None, &none));
7185    }

7187    #[test]
7188    fn every_redirected_call_has_its_operands() {
7189        assert_eq!(relative_operands(Sysno::fchmodat2), &[(0, 1)]);
7190        assert_eq!(relative_operands(Sysno::openat2), &[(0, 1)]);
7191        assert_eq!(relative_operands(Sysno::execveat), &[] as &[(usize, usize)]);
7192        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
7193        assert_eq!(absolute_operands(Sysno::rename), &[0, 1]);
7194        #[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]
7195        assert_eq!(relative_operands(Sysno::renameat), &[(0, 1), (2, 3)]);
7196        assert_eq!(absolute_operands(Sysno::execve), &[] as &[usize]);
7197        assert_eq!(absolute_operands(Sysno::read), &[] as &[usize]);
7198    }

7200    /// The calls that follow a final link, and so take a descriptor link to a
7201    /// placeholder for the directory, as each call's `LOOKUP_FOLLOW` says;
7202    /// the ones that operate on the link itself do not.
7203    #[test]
7204    fn the_calls_that_follow_a_final_link_are_told_apart() {
7205        let args = |values: [usize; 6]| syscalls::SyscallArgs {
7206            arg0: values[0],
7207            arg1: values[1],
7208            arg2: values[2],
7209            arg3: values[3],
7210            arg4: values[4],
7211            arg5: values[5],
7212        };
7213        let none = args([0; 6]);
7214        for sysno in [
7215            Sysno::chdir,
7216            Sysno::chroot,
7217            Sysno::statfs,
7218            Sysno::getxattr,
7219            Sysno::setxattr,
7220            Sysno::removexattr,
7221            Sysno::listxattr,
7222            Sysno::truncate,
7223        ] {
7224            assert!(follows_final(sysno, &none, 0), "{sysno}");
7225        }
7226        // x86_64-only numbers (aarch64 has the *at forms alone).
7227        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
7228        for sysno in [
7229            Sysno::access,
7230            Sysno::stat,
7231            Sysno::chmod,
7232            Sysno::chown,
7233            Sysno::utimes,
7234        ] {
7235            assert!(follows_final(sysno, &none, 0), "{sysno}");
7236        }
7237        for sysno in [Sysno::lgetxattr, Sysno::openat, Sysno::execve] {
7238            assert!(!follows_final(sysno, &none, 0), "{sysno}");
7239        }
7240        let nofollow = libc::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW as usize;
7241        assert!(follows_final(crate::arch::FSTATAT, &none, 1));
7242        assert!(!follows_final(
7243            crate::arch::FSTATAT,
7244            &args([0, 0, 0, nofollow, 0, 0]),
7245            1
7246        ));
7247        assert!(!follows_final(
7248            Sysno::statx,
7249            &args([0, 0, nofollow, 0, 0, 0]),
7250            1
7251        ));
7252        // x86_64-only numbers (aarch64 has the *at forms alone).
7253        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
7254        for sysno in [
7255            Sysno::lstat,
7256            Sysno::readlink,
7257            Sysno::lchown,
7258            Sysno::unlink,
7259            Sysno::rmdir,
7260            Sysno::mkdir,
7261            Sysno::rename,
7262            Sysno::open,
7263        ] {
7264            assert!(!follows_final(sysno, &none, 0), "{sysno}");
7265        }
7266        assert!(!follows_final(
7267            Sysno::fchownat,
7268            &args([0, 0, 0, 0, nofollow, 0]),
7269            1
7270        ));
7271        let follow = libc::AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW as usize;
7272        assert!(follows_final(
7273            Sysno::linkat,
7274            &args([0, 0, 0, 0, follow, 0]),
7275            1
7276        ));
7277        assert!(!follows_final(
7278            Sysno::linkat,
7279            &args([0, 0, 0, 0, follow, 0]),
7280            3
7281        ));
7282        assert!(!follows_final(Sysno::linkat, &none, 1));
7283        assert!(follows_final(Sysno::inotify_add_watch, &none, 1));
7284        assert!(!follows_final(
7285            Sysno::inotify_add_watch,
7286            &args([0, 0, 0x0200_0000, 0, 0, 0]),
7287            1
7288        ));
7289    }
7290
7291    /// A descriptor link's prefix ends just past the link, whatever follows,
7292    /// so that a respelling can insert after it; a bare link is all prefix.
7293    #[test]
7294    fn a_descriptor_links_prefix_ends_past_the_link() {
7295        let prefix = |path: &str| descriptor_link_prefix(path.as_bytes());
7296        assert_eq!(
7297            prefix("/proc/self/fd/5/x"),
7298            Some((PathBuf::from("/proc/self/fd/5"), 15))
7299        );
7300        assert_eq!(
7301            prefix("/proc/self/fd/5"),
7302            Some((PathBuf::from("/proc/self/fd/5"), 15))
7303        );
7304        assert_eq!(
7305            prefix("/proc//self/./fd/5/"),
7306            Some((PathBuf::from("/proc/self/fd/5"), 18))
7307        );
7308        assert_eq!(
7309            prefix("/dev/fd/12"),
7310            Some((PathBuf::from("/dev/fd/12"), 10))
7311        );
7312        assert_eq!(prefix("/proc/self/status"), None);
7313        assert_eq!(prefix("self/fd/5"), None);
7314    }

7316    /// A descriptor directory by any task's spelling, for the refusal where
7317    /// the backend's `/proc` is its own; no other path.
7318    #[test]
7319    fn every_descriptor_directory_spelling_is_told() {
7320        for path in [
7321            "/proc/self/fd",
7322            "/proc/thread-self/fd",
7323            "/proc/7/fd",
7324            "/proc/7/task/9/fd",
7325            "/proc/self/task/9/fd/",
7326            "/dev/fd",
7327            "//proc/./self/fd",
7328        ] {
7329            assert!(spells_a_descriptor_directory(Path::new(path)), "{path}");
7330        }
7331        for path in [
7332            "/proc",
7333            "/proc/self",
7334            "/proc/self/fdinfo",
7335            "/proc/self/fd/3",
7336            "/proc/x/fd",
7337            "/proc/self/fd/..",
7338            "/dev",
7339            "proc/self/fd",
7340        ] {
7341            assert!(!spells_a_descriptor_directory(Path::new(path)), "{path}");
7342        }
7343    }

7345    /// x86-64's `access_ok` for a size not known at compile time: the end
7346    /// may neither wrap nor pass `USER_PTR_MAX`.
7347    #[test]
7348    fn access_ok_refuses_a_wrapping_or_kernel_range() {
7349        let limit = crate::syscalls::threads::user_ptr_max();
7350        assert!(access_ok(0x1000, 1));
7351        assert!(access_ok(0, 0));
7352        assert!(access_ok(limit, 0));
7353        assert!(!access_ok(limit, 1));
7354        assert!(!access_ok(usize::MAX, 1));
7355        assert!(!access_ok(0x1000, usize::MAX));
7356        assert!(!access_ok(0x1000, isize::MAX as usize + 1));
7357    }
7358
7359    /// A directory read in Linux's order: the buffer before `EISDIR`, also
7360    /// for a count of 0; the vectors' count, array, lengths and ranges before
7361    /// anything; nothing to read is 0; `preadv2`'s flags last.
7362    #[test]
7363    fn a_directory_read_checks_in_linux_order() {
7364        let memory = reverie::syscalls::LocalMemory::new();
7365        let args = |values: [usize; 6]| syscalls::SyscallArgs {
7366            arg0: values[0],
7367            arg1: values[1],
7368            arg2: values[2],
7369            arg3: values[3],
7370            arg4: values[4],
7371            arg5: values[5],
7372        };
7373        let buffer = [0u8; 16];
7374        let at = buffer.as_ptr() as usize;
7375        assert_eq!(
7376            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::read, &args([3, at, 16, 0, 0, 0])),
7377            Err(Errno::EISDIR)
7378        );
7379        assert_eq!(
7380            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::read, &args([3, at, 0, 0, 0, 0])),
7381            Err(Errno::EISDIR)
7382        );
7383        assert_eq!(
7384            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::read, &args([3, usize::MAX, 1, 0, 0, 0])),
7385            Err(Errno::EFAULT)
7386        );
7387        assert_eq!(
7388            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::read, &args([3, at, usize::MAX, 0, 0, 0])),
7389            Err(Errno::EFAULT)
7390        );
7391        assert_eq!(
7392            read_answer(
7393                &memory,
7394                Sysno::pread64,
7395                &args([3, at, 16, i64::MAX as usize, 0, 0])
7396            ),
7397            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
7398        );
7399        let empty: [[u64; 2]; 1] = [[at as u64, 0]];
7400        let vectors = empty.as_ptr() as usize;
7401        assert_eq!(
7402            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::readv, &args([3, vectors, 1, 0, 0, 0])),
7403            Ok(0)
7404        );
7405        assert_eq!(
7406            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::readv, &args([3, vectors, 1025, 0, 0, 0])),
7407            Err(Errno::EINVAL)
7408        );
7409        let one: [[u64; 2]; 1] = [[at as u64, 16]];
7410        let vectors = one.as_ptr() as usize;
7411        assert_eq!(
7412            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::readv, &args([3, vectors, 1, 0, 0, 0])),
7413            Err(Errno::EISDIR)
7414        );
7415        assert_eq!(
7416            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::preadv2, &args([3, vectors, 1, 0, 0, 8])),
7417            Err(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP)
7418        );
7419        assert_eq!(
7420            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::preadv2, &args([3, vectors, 1, 0, 0, 1])),
7421            Err(Errno::EISDIR)
7422        );
7423        let bad: [[u64; 2]; 2] = [[at as u64, 1], [u64::MAX, 1]];
7424        let vectors = bad.as_ptr() as usize;
7425        assert_eq!(
7426            read_answer(&memory, Sysno::readv, &args([3, vectors, 2, 0, 0, 0])),
7427            Err(Errno::EFAULT)
7428        );
7429    }
7430
7431    /// The calls through a placeholder descriptor that never reach it are
7432    /// told by their descriptor operand; a path call only by an empty path
7433    /// with `AT_EMPTY_PATH`, or `futimens`'s none.
7434    #[test]
7435    fn the_answered_calls_name_their_descriptor() {
7436        let args = |values: [usize; 6]| syscalls::SyscallArgs {
7437            arg0: values[0],
7438            arg1: values[1],
7439            arg2: values[2],
7440            arg3: values[3],
7441            arg4: values[4],
7442            arg5: values[5],
7443        };
7444        let memory = reverie::syscalls::LocalMemory::new();
7445        for sysno in [
7446            Sysno::getdents64,
7447            Sysno::lseek,
7448            Sysno::fchdir,
7449            Sysno::fcntl,
7450            Sysno::read,
7451            Sysno::write,
7452            Sysno::ioctl,
7453            Sysno::fsync,
7454            Sysno::fdatasync,
7455            Sysno::syncfs,
7456            Sysno::flock,
7457            Sysno::fchmod,
7458            Sysno::fchown,
7459            Sysno::fgetxattr,
7460            Sysno::fsetxattr,
7461            Sysno::flistxattr,
7462            Sysno::fremovexattr,
7463            Sysno::ftruncate,
7464            Sysno::fallocate,
7465            Sysno::fadvise64,
7466            Sysno::readahead,
7467            Sysno::fstatfs,
7468        ] {
7469            assert_eq!(
7470                placeholder_operand(&memory, sysno, &args([3, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0])),
7471                Some(0),
7472                "{sysno}"
7473            );
7474        }
7475        // A file-backed mapping names its descriptor; an anonymous one does
7476        // not.
7477        assert_eq!(
7478            placeholder_operand(&memory, Sysno::mmap, &args([0, 4096, 1, 2, 3, 0])),
7479            Some(4)
7480        );
7481        assert_eq!(
7482            placeholder_operand(
7483                &memory,
7484                Sysno::mmap,
7485                &args([
7486                    0,
7487                    4096,
7488                    1,
7489                    (libc::MAP_PRIVATE | libc::MAP_ANONYMOUS) as usize,
7490                    3,
7491                    0
7492                ])
7493            ),
7494            None
7495        );
7496        // `futimens`.
7497        assert_eq!(
7498            placeholder_operand(&memory, Sysno::utimensat, &args([3, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0])),
7499            Some(0)
7500        );
7501        // A path call through the descriptor itself: an empty path with
7502        // `AT_EMPTY_PATH`; any name is a lookup below it.
7503        let empty = c"";
7504        let name = c"x";
7505        let at_empty = libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH as usize;
7506        assert_eq!(
7507            placeholder_operand(
7508                &memory,
7509                Sysno::fchmodat2,
7510                &args([3, empty.as_ptr() as usize, 0o600, at_empty, 0, 0])
7511            ),
7512            Some(0)
7513        );
7514        assert_eq!(
7515            placeholder_operand(
7516                &memory,
7517                Sysno::fchmodat2,
7518                &args([3, name.as_ptr() as usize, 0o600, at_empty, 0, 0])
7519            ),
7520            None
7521        );
7522        assert_eq!(
7523            placeholder_operand(
7524                &memory,
7525                Sysno::fchownat,
7526                &args([3, empty.as_ptr() as usize, 0, 0, at_empty, 0])
7527            ),
7528            Some(0)
7529        );
7530        assert_eq!(
7531            placeholder_operand(
7532                &memory,
7533                Sysno::faccessat2,
7534                &args([3, empty.as_ptr() as usize, 0, 0, 0, 0])
7535            ),
7536            None,
7537            "an empty path without AT_EMPTY_PATH names nothing"
7538        );
7539        // aarch64 number 84 (sync_file_range semantics) takes arg 0 too.
7540        // Pin the number so a syscalls-crate renaming cannot silently
7541        // change the arm above.
7542        #[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]
7543        assert_eq!(Sysno::sync_file_range.id(), 84);
7544        #[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]
7545        assert_eq!(
7546            placeholder_operand(&memory, Sysno::sync_file_range, &args([3, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0])),
7547            Some(0)
7548        );
7549        #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
7550        assert_eq!(
7551            placeholder_operand(&memory, Sysno::sync_file_range, &args([3, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0])),
7552            Some(0)
7553        );
7554        // The descriptor-table calls are the kernel's.
7555        for sysno in [
7556            Sysno::close,
7557            Sysno::dup,
7558            Sysno::dup3,
7559            #[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
7560            Sysno::dup2,
7561        ] {
7562            assert_eq!(
7563                placeholder_operand(&memory, sysno, &args([3, 4, 0, 0, 0, 0])),
7564                None
7565            );
7566        }
7567    }
7568
7569    /// `fallocate`, `fadvise64` and `sync_file_range` of a directory, in
7570    /// Linux's order of checks (v6.17 `vfs_fallocate`, `generic_fadvise`,
7571    /// `sync_file_range`).
7572    #[test]
7573    fn the_refused_writes_of_a_directory_follow_linux_order() {
7574        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0, -1, 1), Errno::EINVAL);
7575        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0, 0, 0), Errno::EINVAL);
7576        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0x100, 0, 1), Errno::EOPNOTSUPP);
7577        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0x02, 0, 1), Errno::EOPNOTSUPP);
7578        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0x03, 0, 1), Errno::EBADF);
7579        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0x09, 0, 1), Errno::EOPNOTSUPP);
7580        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0x08, 0, 1), Errno::EBADF);
7581        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0x0a, 0, 1), Errno::EOPNOTSUPP);
7582        assert_eq!(fallocate_answer(0, 0, 1), Errno::EBADF);
7583        assert_eq!(fadvise_answer(-1, 0), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
7584        assert_eq!(fadvise_answer(0, libc::POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED), Ok(0));
7585        assert_eq!(fadvise_answer(0, 99), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
7586        assert_eq!(sync_file_range_answer(0, 0, 8), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
7587        assert_eq!(sync_file_range_answer(-1, 0, 0), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
7588        assert_eq!(sync_file_range_answer(i64::MAX, 1, 0), Err(Errno::EINVAL));
7589        assert_eq!(sync_file_range_answer(0, 4096, 7), Ok(0));
7590    }
7591
7592    /// Every call Detcore answers through a placeholder descriptor passes the
7593    /// prefilter that lets `Detcore::handle_syscall_event` ask
7594    /// (`may_name_file_or_descriptor`), so that no such answer is dead code:
7595    /// each call [`placeholder_operand`] names a descriptor for, with the
7596    /// arguments that make it (a file-backed `mmap` and a path-less
7597    /// `utimensat` with zeros, the `AT_EMPTY_PATH` calls with an empty path),
7598    /// and the transfers.
7599    #[test]
7600    fn every_call_a_placeholder_answers_passes_the_prefilter() {
7601        use crate::syscall_classification::all_pinned_syscalls;
7602        use crate::syscalls::may_name_file_or_descriptor;
7603        let memory = reverie::syscalls::LocalMemory::new();
7604        // An empty path at an address whose low 32 bits carry
7605        // `AT_EMPTY_PATH`, so that one value serves as each call's path and
7606        // its flags.
7607        let buffer = vec![0u8; 3 * 0x1000];
7608        let start = buffer.as_ptr() as usize;
7609        let empty = (start..start + 2 * 0x1000)
7610            .find(|address| address & libc::AT_EMPTY_PATH as usize != 0)
7611            .expect("an address with the flag's bit");
7612        let names_itself = syscalls::SyscallArgs::new(0, empty, empty, empty, empty, empty);
7613        let zero = syscalls::SyscallArgs::new(0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0);
7614        let mut answered = 0;
7615        let mut unasked = Vec::new();
7616        for sysno in all_pinned_syscalls() {
7617            if placeholder_operand(&memory, sysno, &zero).is_some()
7618                || placeholder_operand(&memory, sysno, &names_itself).is_some()
7619                || matches!(
7620                    sysno,
7621                    Sysno::sendfile | Sysno::splice | Sysno::tee | Sysno::copy_file_range
7622                )
7623            {
7624                answered += 1;
7625                if !may_name_file_or_descriptor(sysno) {
7626                    unasked.push(sysno);
7627                }
7628            }
7629        }
7630        assert!(unasked.is_empty(), "answered but never asked: {unasked:?}");
7631        assert!(answered > 40, "{answered} calls answered");
7632    }
7633}
